# CHALICE HANDOFF — Windows-ABI compatibility layer for GAIA-OS

## C4-67 — CALL-SHAPE BISECTION: it is NOT the FF15 memory-indirect addressing form, and NOT cross-image targeting, that kills hello9's call — it is ANY indirect near call (opcode `FF /2`, `CALL r/m32`, memory OR register operand). A direct `E8` same-image call (V1) survives clean; a `FF15` memory-indirect SAME-image call (V2) dies BYTE-IDENTICAL to hello9's own crash; a register-indirect (`FF D6`) CROSS-image call through the exact same real, bound `GetStdHandle` IAT slot hello9 itself uses (V3) ALSO dies byte-identical. Measurement atom, no fix, per task law. Verdict class (B) (genuine vendored-blob bug) STRENGTHENS and NARROWS: fork (A) (DLL code pages never registered as guest-executable) is independently REFUTED by Step-1 recon — kernel32.dll's and kernelbase.dll's own full ranges are both confirmed live-registered with FEX's `InvalidationTracker` (rc=0) well before any of these calls execute, in every relevant receipt on disk.

**Step 1 — recon (no build)**: grepped `ntfile.c`/`ntboot.c`/`kmain.c`/`pemod.c` for every `BTCpu*`/notify call site. Two call sites exist on this floor, both already fix-and-proven live (C4-46/48/53/54): (1) `NtMapViewOfSection` (`ntfile.c`) calls `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` UNCONDITIONALLY for every dynamically-mapped guest x86 (Machine `0x14C`) image other than `ntdll.dll` (which has its own dedicated `BTCpuProcessInit`-driven `HandleImageMap` path, C4-29) — this fires for kernel32.dll AND kernelbase.dll's own FULL image ranges, not a stub/partial region; (2) `NtProtectVirtualMemory` calls `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` directly (bypassing the swallowing wrapper, C4-52/53) for any `(base,size)` once `nt_wow64_ready`. Grepped `scratch/c4-6*_*full.log` for `base=0x5D09`/`base=0x5E1C`: **`CHALICE C4-46: BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection name=syswow64/kernel32.dll base=0x5D090000 size=0x119000 rc=0x0`** and **`...name=syswow64/kernelbase.dll base=0x5E1C0000 size=0x435000 rc=0x0`** appear in every one of `c4-61`/`c4-64`/`c4-65`/`c4-66`'s own hello9-boot receipts — `rc=0` (`STATUS_SUCCESS`) confirms `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`→`HandleImageMap`→`InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` genuinely ran and inserted BOTH DLLs' entire ranges into `XIntervals` well before hello9's own fatal call. hello9's own crash target (`0x5D09D7AC`, kernel32.dll's `GetStdHandle` thunk, C4-65/66) sits comfortably inside the registered `[0x5D090000, 0x5D1A9000)` range. Read vendored `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp` (read-only, not modified): `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` (line 1009) calls `HandleImageMap(Address)` (line 175, `InvalidationTracker->HandleImageMap` + `ImageTracker->HandleImageMap`) — exactly the path this floor's own C4-46 fix wires and the receipts confirm firing. Six OTHER exported `BTCpu*` notify APIs exist in the same file (`BTCpuFlushInstructionCache2`/`Heavy`, `BTCpuNotifyMemoryDirty`/`Alloc`/`Free`, `BTCpuNotifyReadFile`, `BTCpuNotifyUnmapViewOfSection`) that this floor never calls at all — a real, separate donor-gap class, but structurally irrelevant to a freshly-mapped, never-protected-since, never-freed, never-`NtReadFile`-touched image region, so NOT implicated in hello9's specific crash. **Verdict on fork (A): REFUTED for this fault** — the DLL code pages ARE registered as guest-executable, confirmed live, not inferred; theory (A) as literally stated ("never registered") does not hold.

**Step 2 — test matrix, verified opcodes at build time** (`objdump -d`, before any boot): `hello9d_x86.c` (V1) — `e8 08 00 00 00` (`CALL rel32`, direct, same-image target). `hello9c_x86.c` (V2) — `ff 15 00 40 40 00` (`CALL dword ptr [0x404000]`, memory-indirect, own-image `.data` slot holding a same-image function pointer, needing only the already-proven HIGHLOW reloc, zero DLL imports). `hello9b_x86.c` (V3) — `a1 40 20 40 00` (`mov eax,[0x402040]`, the real bound `GetStdHandle` IAT slot — same mechanism C4-57/65/66 proved this floor binds and keeps durably correct) then `ff d6` (`call esi`, register-indirect, cross-image target); the naked-asm symbol reference (`movl __imp__GetStdHandle@4, %0`) was deliberately used over a typed `extern void *x asm(...)` after this atom's own build-time check showed the typed form triggers lld's MinGW runtime-pseudo-reloc auto-import indirection (an extra load through a stub cell only a real Windows loader patches — this floor's `PeMapImage` never does), confirmed via a side-by-side `objdump -d` diff of both forms.

**Boots** (established swap method, `build.sh`/`shell.c` two-line diff per `git show 19e4484`'s own convention; SERIAL, checkpoint commit before each; `timeout 240 python3 scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`):
- **V1 (hello9d, checkpoint `e2b1645`)**: `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x33` → **`pe10 ret=51`** — clean exit, zero occurrences of the `elr=0x26D0C02B0`/`rip=0x0` fault signature anywhere in the 12442-line receipt (`scratch/c4-67_v1_receipt{,_full}.log`). **V1 PASS.**
- **V2 (hello9c, checkpoint `03fcca8`)**: `CHALICE: NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0` → **`pe10 ret=3221225477`** — 10 occurrences of the `pc=0x26D0C02B0` trampoline address, 22 `rip=0x0` probe lines, byte-identical fault CLASS to hello9's own established C4-58-through-66 crash (`scratch/c4-67_v2_receipt{,_full}.log`). **V2 DIES.**
- **V3 (hello9b, checkpoint `2103a5c`)**: same `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0` → **`pe10 ret=3221225477`** — 9 occurrences of `pc=0x26D0C02B0`, 22 `rip=0x0` probe lines, same fault CLASS (`scratch/c4-67_v3_receipt{,_full}.log`). **V3 DIES.**

**Verdict** (task's own matrix, read honestly, not forced): the exact result is V1-PASS/V2-DIES, which the task's own matrix already assigns to "memory-indirect form broken generally → (B), JIT tracing next" — but V3 (register-indirect, NOT memory-indirect) ALSO dying sharpens this beyond that bucket: the common factor between the two DYING cases (V2, V3) and absent from the one PASSING case (V1) is not "memory operand" nor "cross-image target" (V2 is same-image and still dies; V3 is cross-image via the exact same slot mechanism C4-57/65/66 already proved correctly bound-and-durable, and still dies) — it is the **indirect-call opcode class itself** (`FF /2`, `CALL r/m32`, whether `r/m` is a memory operand or a register), versus the **direct-call opcode** (`E8`, `CALL rel32`), which alone survives. This both CONFIRMS fork (B) (genuine vendored-blob bug, not a floor registration gap — directly consistent with Step 1's own finding that the crash target's page is already correctly registered) and NARROWS it precisely: FEX's native call-dispatch machinery mishandles `FF /2`-class indirect calls as a FORM, independent of same-vs-cross-image targeting and independent of memory-vs-register operand submode. Interpreted honestly per the task's own instruction — this is a genuinely different, SHARPER finding than any single one of the four pre-named matrix buckets, not a forced fit into one.

**Named next atom (C4-68)**: JIT/blob tracing of FEX's own indirect-call (`FF /2`) dispatch path in the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob (NOT the vendored `FEX/Source` tree — C4-49/50/58-66's own established precedent that the source tree can mispredict the real Hangover-11.9 blob's compiled behavior) — scoped now to indirect-call handling specifically, NOT gated on any DLL/registration question (Step 1 already closed that line), and NOT gated on memory-vs-register operand addressing (V2 vs V3 both die) — the search space is `CALL r/m32`'s own JIT translation/dispatch, wherever it diverges from the already-proven-correct `CALL rel32` path V1 exercises successfully.

**Revert + canonical reconfirm**: all three TEMP swaps (`e2b1645`/`03fcca8`/`2103a5c`) fully reverted in one commit (`dc6c8d9`), `git diff f9d4fe9 -- build.sh shell.c` confirmed EMPTY (net-zero); PE-timestamp/build-artifact churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before every commit and after every gate/boot; `hello9{,d,c,b}_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked (rebuilt via the now-5-binary `mkpe32.sh`, same convention every prior hello9 atom used). `pe10 fex` re-run TWICE after the full revert — prefix (`scratch/c4-67_pe10_fex_canonical_prefix_receipt{,_full}.log`, immediately post-revert) and final (`scratch/c4-67_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt{,_full}.log`, after the full gate ladder) — both: `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` HOLDS both times**, zero regression. **Gates, no regression** (`scratch/c4-67_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` → `pe8 ret=78`, `GATE PASS (required-green)`. `pe9_gate.py` → both backends `pe9 ret=0`, `GATE PASS (required-green, no regression, both CPU backends)`. `ramfb_gate.py` → 7/7 pattern checks OK, `GATE PASS (ramfb pattern correct)`.

**Commits** (source-only, each stage separate): `2730b33` (3 new call-shape-bisection guest binaries + `mkpe32.sh` extension) → `e2b1645` (TEMP V1 swap, checkpoint) → boot/measure V1 → `03fcca8` (TEMP V2 swap, checkpoint) → boot/measure V2 → `2103a5c` (TEMP V3 swap, checkpoint) → boot/measure V3 → `dc6c8d9` (revert all three, net-zero) → canonical reconfirm + gate ladder → this documentation update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-67), final commit. Vendored `FEX/` tree read-only throughout (`Module.cpp` read for Step 1, zero edits). Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-66 — SECOND-INDIRECTION IAT gap NAMED, CONFIRMED unbound, and FIXED (generalized floor-side i386 IAT binding, kernel32.dll ↔ kernelbase.dll ↔ ntdll.dll's own imports) — but the hello9 crash is UNCHANGED, byte-identical to C4-58 through C4-65's own receipts, PROVING (not just reaffirming) C4-65's own verdict: the fault happens strictly upstream of the code this atom fixed, entirely inside FEX's own native call/branch-dispatch machinery, before hello9's own `FF15` call ever reaches kernel32.dll's thunk code where the second indirection would matter. This atom's own diagnostic (salvaged, uncommitted from a prior stalled lane; hardened with an in-bounds guard before any deref) measured a DIFFERENT slot from C4-65's own probe: real kernel32.dll's exported `GetStdHandle`/`WriteFile` are themselves thin thunks (`8bff 55 8bec 5d ff25 <imm32>`, byte-exact match confirmed live against the stock DLL) that tail-jump through kernel32.dll's OWN Import Directory slot for kernelbase.dll's real implementation — a slot nothing on this floor's C code bound before this atom (`nt_iat_bind_walk`, C4-57, only ever walked the BOOT EXE's own Import Directory). Live measurement: `k32_own_iat_slot_addr=0x5D0CD664` (INSIDE kernel32.dll's own mapped range `[0x5D090000, 0x5D090000+k32size)` — the new hardening guard did NOT fire, proving this specific operand IS correctly relocated, by the GUEST'S OWN x86 loader code executing between kernel32.dll's and kernelbase.dll's own `NtMapViewOfSection` calls, not by this floor's `PeMapImage`, which intentionally passes `apply_relocs=0` for this path, C3c-2q's own established convention) and `k32_own_iat_slot_live_value=0x0003FCAA` — NOT a valid kernelbase code VA (the real target, independently confirmed live via a genuine x86 function-prologue byte match — `66 90 55 89 e5 8b 4d 08`, hotpatch-nop/push-ebp/mov-ebp,esp/mov-ecx,[ebp+8] — at `kbbase+0x4acb0=0x5E20ACB0`), matching the task's own predicted "unbound" signature exactly. STOP LAW ("if slot value live-reads a correct kernelbase code VA, stop after docs — no fix") did NOT apply — proceeded to the gated fix.

**Fix** (`ntfile.c`/`pemod.c`/`pemod.h`): generalized the C4-57 i386 IAT binder from "boot EXE only, guess kernel32-then-kernelbase" to "every currently-mapped PE32 (i386) image, exact registry-basename resolution, name AND ordinal thunks" — `nt_iat_bind_image(img_base, img_size)` replaces `nt_iat_bind_walk`, called once per registry entry at the SAME existing one-shot bind gate (both syswow64 kernel32/kernelbase mapped); each import descriptor's DLL name is matched case-insensitively against the LIVE image registry's own basename (`nt_image_find_by_basename`, strips any synthetic `syswow64/` prefix), not a fixed two-DLL guess; ordinal imports (thunk bit31 set) now resolve via a new `pe_find_export_by_ordinal` (pemod.c, `AddressOfFunctions[ordinal-Base]`, same forwarder/not-found error contract as the existing `pe_find_named_export`); non-PE32 entries (native ARM64 wow64-support DLLs — wow64.dll/wow64win.dll/win32u.dll/the CPU backend) are silently skipped by the existing PE32-magic check, no hardcoded exclusion list. **IRON LAW held**: no hardcoded addresses/counts in the fix logic — every loop terminates on the descriptor/thunk arrays' own null terminators or the registry's own `used` flags, same convention C4-57 already used.

**Live engagement, confirmed exactly as diagnosed**: kernel32.dll's own import pass (`base=0x5D090000`) binds `GetStdHandle -> 0x5E20ACB0` — the EXACT kernelbase code VA this atom's own diagnostic independently named as the correct target — into the EXACT slot address the diagnostic measured (`0x5D0CD664`), 917/929 names bound (12 forwarder-shaped failures, e.g. `HeapAlloc`/`EnterCriticalSection`/`QueryPerformanceCounter` — kernelbase re-exports these as PE forwarders to further DLLs, `pe_find_named_export`'s pre-existing `-2` forwarder-not-chased convention, C4-51 — a python re-derivation of the same import/export tables offline found the same 10/12 core cases, confirming this is real forwarder shape, not a bug); kernelbase.dll's own import pass (`base=0x5E1C0000`) binds 429/432 names from ntdll.dll (3 similar forwarder-shaped gaps).

**Step 3 — PROVE, result: NOT WIN, no new deeper blocker either** — hello9 boot (fix engaged, live-confirmed above) still crashes BYTE-IDENTICAL to C4-58 through C4-65's own receipts: same `elr=0x26D0C02B0` self-fault trampoline, same `State.rip=0` at the exact instant of the fatal call, same `c465_iat0=0x5D09D7AC` (hello9's OWN IAT slot — the FIRST indirection, C4-65's own target) still correctly bound and stable throughout, same `pe10 ret=3221225477`/`STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` — `hello9: the door opens` NOT reached. This is the EXPECTED, PREDICTED result, not a surprise: C4-65 already proved `State.rip` zeros at the SAME INSTANT as hello9's own `FF15 CALL` dispatch, before that call ever lands inside kernel32.dll's thunk code — meaning execution never reaches the second indirection this atom's fix targets, so the second slot's bound-vs-unbound state was ALWAYS irrelevant to this specific, already-well-characterized crash. This atom's own fix is real, correct, live-confirmed, and generalizes a genuine floor gap (a real Windows loader would bind these too) — but it closes a DIFFERENT, narrower gap than the one actually gating hello9's own `WriteFile`-milestone chain. **Sharper unified account** (per this atom's own task): C4-62's frozen-`Eip` theory and C4-63's site-#7-ruled-out claim were already overturned by C4-64 (pinpointed the ACTUAL zeroing to one syscall boundary, a `call`-shaped vs `ret`-shaped divergence) and C4-65 (proved the first-indirection reloc+IAT data are both correct and durable at the moment of use); C4-57's own original "bind or no bind identical fault" finding is now fully explained end-to-end: C4-57's binder only ever addressed the FIRST indirection (hello9's own IAT slot → kernel32.dll code), which C4-65 already proved was never the blocker either way — C4-66 closes the LAST floor-satisfiable candidate (the second indirection) and confirms, by direct fix-then-reboot rather than inference alone, that NEITHER indirection's binding state has ever been the actual cause: the fault is fully and exclusively inside FEX's own vendored `libwow64fex.dll` call-dispatch path, unreachable/unpatchable from this floor's own C.

**Canonical state**: `build.sh`/`shell.c` hello9 swap applied (checkpoint `19e4484`) then fully reverted (`4978fad`, `git diff` against pre-swap `f9d4fe9` confirmed empty — net-zero); PE-timestamp/build-artifact churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before every commit; `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked (rebuilt via `mkpe32.sh` this atom, same convention every prior hello9 atom used). **`pe10 fex` re-run AFTER the full revert, WITH the C4-66 fix live** (`scratch/c4-66_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt{,_full}.log`): `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` STILL HOLDS** — the generalized binder now ALSO engages during the canonical hello8 boot (kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll/ntdll.dll's own imports get bound there too, live-confirmed: `bound NtTerminateProcess -> ...` appears in the canonical receipt), with zero regression. **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` → `pe8 ret=78`, `GATE PASS (required-green)`. `pe9_gate.py` → both backends `pe9 ret=0`, `GATE PASS (required-green, no regression, both CPU backends)`. `ramfb_gate.py` → 7/7 pattern checks OK, `GATE PASS (ramfb pattern correct)`.

**Commits** (source-only, each stage separate): `0746d64` (harden the salvaged diagnostic — in-bounds guard before the `rd32(slotp)` deref) → `19e4484` (TEMP hello9 swap, checkpoint before boot) → boot/measure (Step-1 diagnostic results above, `scratch/c4-66_diag_receipt{,_full}.log`) → `692fdfb` (the fix itself — `ntfile.c`/`pemod.c`/`pemod.h`) → reboot/measure (fix-engagement + Step-3 crash-unchanged results above, `scratch/c4-66_fix_receipt{,_full}.log`) → `4978fad` (revert hello9 swap, net-zero-confirmed) → canonical reconfirm + gate ladder (`scratch/c4-66_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt{,_full}.log`, `scratch/c4-66_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`) → this documentation update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-66), final commit. **Next atom**: same as C4-65's own named next step — disassemble the ACTUAL blob machine code FEX's JIT emits for hello9's specific `FF15` instruction (not the vendored `FEX/Source` tree, already shown to mispredict the real Hangover-11.9 blob's compiled behavior, C4-49/50/58-65's own precedent) to find where its own memory-read/call-dispatch path diverges from this floor's — this atom further narrows the search: it is provably NOT a floor-side data-preparation gap of ANY kind (first OR second indirection, reloc OR bind), so the next atom can skip straight to blob disassembly with no remaining floor-satisfiable candidates to re-check. Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-65 — BOTH the RELOC gap and the IAT gap REFUTED by direct, continuous live measurement — hello9_x86.exe's own `FF15` call operand is correctly relocated (`0x40205c`→`0x4766205c`, live-confirmed at Phase-0 map time) and its IAT slot is correctly bound AND stays durably bound (`0x2068`→`0x5D09D7AC`, `GetStdHandle`'s real resolved VA) continuously from bind-time straight through the fatal call and the terminal crash, in every one of ~5800 consecutive per-syscall probe readings bracketing the fault. C4-66 (the gated fix) is therefore NOT ATTEMPTED — per this atom's own explicit stop condition, no floor-side reloc/IAT gap exists to fix; the zeroing is fully inside FEX's own native call/branch-dispatch machinery (vendored `libwow64fex.dll`), reading a value that demonstrably does not match what this floor's own memory genuinely and continuously holds at the address in question. Sharper, stronger confirmation of C4-64's own verdict (c) — not a new candidate, a decisive elimination of the last two floor-satisfiable ones.

**Task's own two theories, tested empirically** (not by re-reading `pemod.c`/`ntfile.c` and trusting they look correct — by instrumenting the real boot and watching the real guest memory):
(a) RELOC gap — operand `0x40205c` is preferred-base-absolute; if the floor never applies `.reloc`, the guest would dereference the raw file value (`0x40205c`) instead of the delta-shifted correct address (`0x4766205c`).
(b) IAT gap — even if correctly relocated, slot `0x4766205c` holds the unbound `0x2068` thunk RVA unless something binds it, and unless that binding SURVIVES until the guest's own call actually executes.

**Method — three live reads, two new diagnostics** (`ntboot.c`/`ntsvc.c`/`ntfile.c`/`ntfile.h`, commits below), all cited straight from C4-64's own already-committed static disassembly, none re-derived: (1) a one-shot Phase-0-map-time dump (`ntboot.c`, gated on `wow64_mode`) of the raw dword at the LITERAL preferred-base address `0x40205c`, the LIVE in-memory `FF15` operand at `exe_entry+0x17`, and the LIVE IAT slot at `exe_base+0x205c`; (2) a per-syscall EXTENSION of C4-64's own `nt_wow64_live_state_probe` hook (`ntsvc.c`'s `nt_dispatch_log`) that ALSO reads the live dword at the boot exe's own IAT slot #0 on every single dispatch — using C4-57's own private `nt_find_boot_exe` helper, exposed non-static for this purpose (`ntfile.c`/`ntfile.h`). Used the established hello9 swap method (`build.sh`/`shell.c` two-line diff, `9c8eb2f`'s own pattern) — checkpoint, build, boot via `scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, revert, net-zero confirmed via `git diff` against the pre-swap commit.

**Result 1 (settles nothing on its own, but consistent)**: `CHALICE C4-65: preferred_base_dword@0x40205c=0x00000000` — the literal preferred-base address genuinely reads zero, live, no fault (this floor's own `mmu_on()`, `kmain.c`, maps `[0x200000,0x40000000)` as valid-but-unbacked device-nGnRnE identity territory except the explicitly-guarded null page at index 0 of `l2_dev_table` — `0x40205c` falls in `l2_dev_table[2]`, a real page-table entry, just not backed by any QEMU peripheral, so a genuine hardware read silently returns 0, no Data Abort). Matches the task's own framing exactly ("if zero-filled mapped territory, that alone explains call 0") — but this alone does NOT prove the guest actually reads THIS address; it only proves the address exists and would read 0 if dereferenced.

**Result 2 (decisive against theory (a))**: `CHALICE C4-65: ... call_operand_live@exe_entry+0x17=0x000000004766205C` — the guest's OWN in-memory `FF15` instruction operand, read directly from the mapped image right after `PeMapImage` returns (Phase-0, `ntboot.c`, `nt_boot_process(exe_bytes, ..., 0 /*do_imports*/, 1 /*apply_relocs*/, ...)`), is NOT the raw file value `0x40205c` — it is the CORRECTLY delta-shifted `0x4766205c`. Traced to source: `pemod.c`'s `PeMapImage` already implements `IMAGE_REL_BASED_HIGHLOW` (32-bit) relocations correctly, alongside `IMAGE_REL_BASED_DIR64` (the comment trail calls this out explicitly, `pemod.c`, `HANDOFF scar #6`) — `wr32(target, val + (u32b)delta)`, `delta = alloc − image_base = 0x47660000 − 0x400000 = 0x47260000`. Offline byte-level confirmation of the INPUT (no boot needed, a plain `python3 struct` read of `hello9_x86.exe` on disk, this atom): the `.reloc` directory (`rva=0x4000 sz=0x14`) contains ONE block, `page_rva=0x1000`, 6 entries — 5 real `HIGHLOW` (type=3) fixups at page offsets `0x17/0x22/0x48/0x5c/0x62` (RVAs `0x1017/0x1022/0x1048/0x105c/0x1062`) plus one `ABSOLUTE` (type=0) padding terminator — and `0x1017` is EXACTLY the `FF15` operand's own file position (`entry_rva(0x1000)+0x15(opcode)+2(skip ff 15)=0x1017`). `0x40205c + 0x47260000 = 0x4766205c` arithmetically, matching the live-measured value exactly. **RELOC gap (theory a): REFUTED** — `PeMapImage`'s `apply_relocs=1` HIGHLOW pass, already correctly implemented, DOES reach and correctly patch this exact instruction's operand, confirmed by direct measurement, not by re-reading code and trusting it.

**Result 3 (decisive against theory (b), the sharper finding)**: `CHALICE C4-65: ... iat_slot_live@exe_base+0x205c=0x00002068` at Phase-0 map time (unbound file value, EXPECTED — this print fires before kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll are even mapped) — but the PER-SYSCALL extension (`c465_iat0`, `ntsvc.c`) shows the SAME slot transitioning EXACTLY ONCE, from `0x00002068` to `0x5D09D7AC`, at the existing C4-57 binder's own already-proven-live bind event (transcript: `CHALICE C4-57: both syswow64 kernel32/kernelbase mapped -- binding boot exe IAT` / `bound GetStdHandle -> 0x000000005D09D7AC` / `bound WriteFile -> 0x000000005D09F9BC` / `IAT bind done bound=2 failed=0`, ~5700 probe-lines before the fault) — and then holds `0x5D09D7AC`, UNCHANGED, across every one of the ~5800 subsequent probe lines this atom captured, INCLUDING the exact `svc id=80` (`NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)`) line C4-64 pinned as the last syscall before the zeroing, and every one of the fault's own zero-`rip` lines through the terminal `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`/`pe10 ret=3221225477`. No `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call targets the IAT's own page (`0x47662000`) anywhere near this window (checked the full `BaseAddress=` sequence, C4-60's own diagnostic) — nothing plausibly re-protects or invalidates it. **IAT gap (theory b): REFUTED** — the slot is not merely bound once and forgotten; it is proven durably, continuously correct at the EXACT instant the guest's own `FF15` dereference must occur.

**Synthesis — a stronger elimination than either theory alone predicted**: with the operand correctly pointing at `0x4766205c` (Result 2) and that address correctly and durably holding `0x5D09D7AC` (Result 3), a textbook `CALL dword ptr [0x4766205c]` executed at this exact moment MUST compute a call target of `0x5D09D7AC` (real, valid `GetStdHandle` code inside `syswow64/kernelbase.dll`) — never `0`. Yet the already-established, byte-identical measurement (this atom's own fresh boot, unchanged from C4-64's own receipt) shows `State.rip` reading exactly `0` at this precise call, landing in the same `elr=0x26D0C02B0` self-fault trampoline (C4-58/59/61/62/63) and terminating the same way (`pe10 ret=3221225477`). Since this floor's own data (reloc target AND bound value) is PROVEN correct and stable at the moment of use, the discrepancy cannot be a floor-side data-preparation gap — it is fully inside FEX's own native call/branch-dispatch machinery (vendored `libwow64fex.dll`, unreachable/unpatchable from `kernel-arm/zoneA/*.c`), which is either reading a stale/different view of this exact guest memory address or computing the call target through some other path this atom's own instrumentation cannot see inside the blob. Same verdict CLASS as C4-58 through C4-64 (not floor-satisfiable), but this atom closes off the two remaining candidates this specific task named, with direct, continuous, bracketing live evidence rather than static code-reading alone.

**C4-66 (fix) — NOT ATTEMPTED, per this atom's own explicit stop condition**: the task's own law ("If diagnosis REFUTES the IAT/reloc theory (slot reads nonzero-and-valid live), STOP at C4-65, no speculative fix, document verdict") applies exactly — both named theories are refuted by live, nonzero, valid, DURABLE readings, not merely a one-shot snapshot. No relocation/IAT-binding code was changed (none needed; both mechanisms are already floor-correct); this atom names no floor-satisfiable target for `NtProtectVirtualMemory`/`IMAGE_BASERELOC`/IAT-walk code to fix. hello9's `WriteFile`-milestone chain remains blocked at the exact same C4-58-through-C4-64 frontier, now proven (not just suspected) to be entirely inside the vendored FEX blob's own call-dispatch path.

**Convention sweep**: PE-timestamp/build-artifact churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before every commit this atom; `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked (rebuilt via `mkpe32.sh` this atom, same convention every prior hello9 atom used). `build.sh`/`shell.c` confirmed byte-identical (`git diff`) to the pre-swap commit after revert — net-zero. `scratch/c4-65_*.log` receipts left untracked, same convention every one of the prior 60+ atoms' own receipts used (this atom briefly committed them by mistake, then `git revert`ed that commit and restored the files from the reverted blob so they stay on disk, untracked — see commit list below).

**Commits** (source-only, each stage separate): `d1a3578` (Phase-0 3-value diagnostic, `ntboot.c`) → `05bed7b` (TEMP hello9 swap, `build.sh`/`shell.c`) → boot/measure (Results 1-2 above) → `77200f8` (revert swap, net-zero-confirmed) → `cf53bee` (per-syscall IAT-slot watch extension, `ntfile.c`/`ntfile.h`/`ntsvc.c`, exposing C4-57's own `nt_find_boot_exe`) → reboot/measure (Result 3) → `2e10a5f`/`4df638c` (receipts commit + self-revert, net effect: receipts on disk, untracked, matching convention) → this documentation update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-65), final commit. Receipts: `scratch/c4-65_hello9_boot_receipt{,_full}.log` (the diagnostic hello9 boot, `c465_iat0` trace + both one-shot Phase-0 prints), `scratch/c4-65_pe10_fex_canonical_{prefix,final}_receipt{,_full}.log` (canonical hello8 `ret=42` reconfirmed twice, unaffected by the new diagnostics — hello8's own reads all land inside its `0x5000`-byte image, harmless zero/no-import reads, no OOB), `scratch/c4-65_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log` (all three GATE PASS, no regression). **Next atom**: if pursued further, would require disassembling the ACTUAL blob machine code FEX's JIT emits for this specific `FF15` instruction (not the vendored `FEX/Source` tree, which this whole thread has already shown can mispredict the real Hangover-11.9 blob's compiled behavior, C4-49/50/58-64's own precedent) to find where its own memory-read path diverges from this floor's — a genuinely deep blob-disasm atom, same class of effort C4-50's own `RtlImageNtHeader`/`HandleImageMap` disasm required. ## C4-64 — CANDIDATE #8 REFUTED as the corruption source, zeroing event PINPOINTED to one syscall boundary: hello8 (canonical) shows `State.rip==0` at ZERO of 2287 live per-syscall probe readings; hello9 shows it at 19 consecutive readings starting the line immediately after the shared `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)` call and persisting to the terminal crash. At that exact boundary hello8 performs a textbook x86 `ret` (rsp+4, new rip == the predecessor's own `[rsp]` value) into the WOW64 exit stub (`0x488D0000`), reaching `NtTerminateProcess(0x2A)` (`pe10 ret=42`); hello9 instead performs a `call`-shaped push (rsp-0x24, a fresh return address appears on the stack) and `rip` reads `0` at that same instant — hello9 never issues the second `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x488D0000)` call hello8 relies on, and instead runs into the already-documented `elr=0x26D0C02B0` self-fault trampoline (C4-58/59/61/62/63) and terminates via unhandled `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` (`pe10 ret=3221225477`). `[rsp]` stays mapped and nonzero at all 19 zero-`rip` readings, ruling out a zeroed-stack-slot restore. **Verdict: branch (c)** — hello9-specific, native-control-flow/JIT-cache-miss divergence at this one call site, not a corruption of the restore mechanism. No fix applied.

**Salvage atom**: a prior lane completed all boots/diagnostics/commits (banked: `5b3b9af` diagnostic, `3d341db`/`274e483` hello9 swap+revert, receipts already on disk) and died at the wall before gates/analysis/docs. This atom reran the two incomplete gates and performed the analysis only — no boot, no diagnostic change, no fix.

**Gates**: `pe8_gate.py` — banked `pe8 ret=78` GATE PASS confirmed, not rerun. `pe9_gate.py` — banked receipt was 0 bytes (prior lane's wrapper timeout was shorter than the script's own ~150s worst-case runtime, killing it before its final write); rerun with a 240s wrapper, both backends `pe9 ret=0`/`pe9 fex ret=0`, GATE PASS, clean. `ramfb_gate.py` — never ran; rerun clean, 7/7 pattern checks OK, GATE PASS, no flake either gate.

**Canonical exit, formally confirmed**: `c4-64_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt_full.log` — `NtTerminateProcess ExitStatus=0x2A` → `pe10 ret=42`, one hit, confirmed not just asserted.

**Probe/gregs-offset derivation** (read out of the committed diagnostic, `5b3b9af`, not re-derived): `nt_wow64_live_state_probe()` (`ntfile.c`/`.h`) reads `CpuStateFrame::State.gregs[REG_RSP]` and the 4 bytes at that guest-stack address — the inputs candidate #8 (`WowSyscallHandler::HandleSyscallImpl`, `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:434-464`) uses for `ReturnRIP = *(uint32_t*)(Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP])` (`:434`) then, gated on `State.rip` already equalling a fixed bridge-stub address, `Frame->State.rip = ReturnRIP` (`:464`). Offset, confirmed this atom by direct field-order read of `FEX/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h:91-106`: `InlineJITBlockHeader`@0 + `DeferredSignalRefCount`@8 + `pf_raw`/`af_raw`@0x10/0x14 + `rip`@0x18 (`:104`) → `gregs[16]`@0x20 (`:106`); `REG_RSP=4` (`FEX/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/X86Enums.h:15`) → `gregs[REG_RSP]` @ `0x20+4*8=0x40`, matching the diagnostic's own `current_frame+0x40` read. Timing model, from the diagnostic's own committed comment (`ntsvc.c`): each printed line shows the PREVIOUS dispatch's already-committed `rip`/`rsp` restore plus the CURRENT dispatch's not-yet-consumed `[rsp]`.

**Analysis**: hello8 2287/2288 probe lines parsed (1 malformed interleaved-print line, logging artifact, excluded), hello9 2222/2223, same artifact class both logs. `grep -c "rip=0x0...0"`: hello8 **0**, hello9 **19** (one unbroken run, the last 19 of 2222 entries) — decisive, refutes branch (b) outright. The entry before hello9's first zero is `svc id=80 rip=0x47661000 rsp=0x488CC000 [rsp]=0x488D0000` + `NtProtectVirtualMemory(*BaseAddress=0x47661000)` — BYTE-IDENTICAL to hello8's own `id=80` line at the same call. hello8's next line: `rip=0x488D0000 rsp=0x488CC004 [rsp]=0x0` (rsp+4, rip==predecessor's `[rsp]` — a `ret`), landing at the WOW64 stub page (`0x488D0000`, matches C4-30/58's own "STUB PAGE" dump), through a second `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x488D0000)`, then `NtTerminateProcess(0x2A)`. hello9's next line: `rip=0x0 rsp=0x488CBFDC [rsp]=0x4766101B` (rsp-0x24, pushes not a pop; `[rsp]` is a fresh address inside hello9_x86.exe's own entry page, not a carried-over stub target) — hello9 never issues the second `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x488D0000)` call (confirmed: 241 total NPVM calls in hello8 vs 252 in hello9 across the whole boot; hello8's own final `0x488D0000` call has no counterpart in hello9's sequence, which instead protects several EXTRA import/code pages unique to its own larger image, C4-57). `rip` stays 0 through the already-documented `elr=0x26D0C02B0` fault (C4-58/59/61/62/63, same fault, same register/instruction signature, no new fault site) → `NtRaiseException(0xC0000005)` → second-chance unhandled → `pe10 ret=3221225477`.

**Timing conclusion**: `rip(N+1)==[rsp](N)` literally holds for only 34/2286 (hello8) and 35/2221 (hello9) pairs (~1.5%) — NOT a refutation, since most consecutive lines are exact reprints of a stalled/retried dispatch (state hasn't advanced, equality vacuous — e.g. the same `id=49 rip=0x47661000 rsp=0x488CC000 [rsp]=0x488D0000` line repeats 14x straight in BOTH logs right before the divergence). At the ONE point where state genuinely advances — the `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)` return above — the rule holds EXACTLY in hello8 and is precisely where it breaks in hello9 (`[rsp](N)` is the SAME `0x488D0000` in both logs' identical predecessor line; only hello9's `rip(N+1)` fails to become it). The most surgical single data point across both ~2200-line transcripts.

**Candidate #8 verdict**: REFUTED as the corruption source — its own input (`[rsp]`) is validly mapped and nonzero at every hello9 probe line including all 19 zero-`rip` ones, so it cannot be relaying a zeroed slot. Consistent with C4-63's own algebraic finding (zero `Op->PC`-baked rip at `NoExecOp`/`BreakOp` requires the dispatch loop's own `AbsoluteLoopTopAddress` read, `Dispatcher.cpp:147`, to have already read 0) — this atom narrows WHERE that pre-existing zero first appears: immediately after `NtProtectVirtualMemory(0x47661000)`'s return, at the call-vs-ret fork. Native dispatch-loop/JIT-cache-miss event, not floor-satisfiable, same verdict class as C4-58 through C4-63.

**Shared tail window**: `NtProtectVirtualMemory` BaseAddress sequence identical between hello8/hello9 for the last 5 calls before divergence (`...0x5D0BA000, 0x5D0C0A68, 0x5D0C0A68, 0x47661000`), then hello8 alone has one more (`0x488D0000`). C4-59's named "18x id=49/QPC burst" shape (repeating `id=49`/`id=147`, stable state) is present and byte-identical, type/count/position, in both logs' probe lines through the `id=80` call. `NtClose` appears twice, identically positioned, both transcripts (unrelated, earlier in boot). `NtTestAlert`/`NtFlushInstructionCache` are unresolved numeric-`id`-only dispatches in this floor's logging in both logs (not name-printed) — not separately distinguishable from the `id=49` burst by name.

**Convention sweep**: PE-timestamp build churn reverted, confirmed via `git status --short` clean; `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked, standard convention.

**Commits**: banked and verified, not redone — `5b3b9af`/`3d341db`/`274e483`. This documentation update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-64) is the final commit, source-untouched. Receipts: `scratch/c4-64_pe10_fex_canonical_{prefix,final}_receipt{,_full}.log`, `scratch/c4-64_hello9_boot_receipt{,_full}.log`, `scratch/c4-64_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`. Full derivation/excerpts → FEX-MAP.md §**C4-64**. **Next atom**: disassemble hello9_x86.exe's own guest code at `0x47661000` to name the actual instruction(s) diverging from hello8's plain `ret`, and determine cache-miss vs. genuinely-different-code-path (import table, C4-57). Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-63 — SOURCE-READING ONLY: exhaustive grep of the vendored FEXCore tree for every explicit `State.rip`-writing site finds NO literal-zero write anywhere; the ONE mechanism that reaches the fatal `elr=0x26D0C02B0` trampoline (`NoExecOp`/`BreakOp`) is proven, algebraically from source, to be a pure REPORTING path that can only bake in the constant 0 if the dispatch loop's own `State.rip` READ (immediately before entering this trampoline sequence) already returned 0 — narrowing, not resolving, the C4-58/59 "zeroing event" down to one single load instruction. One new, unconfirmed, but genuinely plausible candidate named: `WowSyscallHandler::HandleSyscallImpl`'s native (non-JIT, not `grep`-discoverable) `Frame->State.rip = ReturnRIP` write, sourced from 4 bytes read directly off the GUEST's OWN stack at every real syscall dispatch — depends on runtime guest-stack contents, cannot be confirmed or refuted by static reading alone. Both remaining candidates are entirely inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob. No fix applied, no code changed, no build/boot performed (per this atom's own explicit law).

**Task**: find, from FEXCore's own vendored source (`./FEX`, already present in this repo), every site that writes a literal/default zero into `CpuStateFrame::State.rip` (or the equivalent guest-PC-tracking field) during normal (non-crash) operation, and determine whether any such site's triggering condition plausibly correlates with hello8-vs-hello9's own image-shape difference (LookupCache/BlockCache-miss path, cache size, etc — C4-59's own already-named leads).

**Step 1 — exhaustive grep, every write site to `.rip`/`State.rip` in the vendored tree** (`rip\s*=\s*0`, `rip\s*=\s*{}`, `->rip\s*=`, `.State.rip`/`->State.rip`, `SetRIP`/`InvalidRIP`, plus every `reset`/`invalidate`/`clear`-named function touching `CpuStateFrame`/`InternalThreadState`): **zero hits for a literal `rip = 0`/`rip = {}` anywhere in the tree.** Every genuine write site to `CPUState::rip`, file:line + trigger condition:
1. `FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h:217` — `rip = ~0ULL;`, inside `CPUState()`'s constructor, `#ifndef NDEBUG` only (debug-build default-init sentinel, deliberately ALL-ONES not zero, to catch spurious pre-init reads) — fires once per NEW `CPUState` object (`ContextImpl::CreateThread`), never mid-execution.
2. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp:412` — `Thread->CurrentFrame->State.rip = InitialRIP;`, `ContextImpl::CreateThread`, one-time thread-creation seed (this floor's own boot-seeded `exe_entry`, always nonzero).
3. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/JIT.cpp:539` — `Frame->State.rip = GuestRip;`, `Arm64JITCore::ExitFunctionLink`, gated on `TFSet` (trap-flag/single-step mode only) — `GuestRip=Record->GuestRIP`, a compile-time-baked block-link target; TF is never set in this boot (no single-stepping engaged, per C4-59's own transcript).
4. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp:501` — `str x1,[STATE,#rip]`, inside the `CallbackPtr` reentrant thunk-callback trampoline — writes whatever RIP the C++ thunk-invoking caller passes as its 2nd argument; hello8/hello9's own trivial `WriteFile`/`GetStdHandle`/syscall-only flow never invokes a thunked callback, path never entered.
5. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp:115` — `str EC_CALL_CHECKER_PC_REG,[STATE,#rip]`, `#ifdef ARCHITECTURE_arm64ec` only — this floor targets 32-bit x86 guests under `libwow64fex.dll` (WOW64), not x64-under-ARM64EC; not the active code path for this floor's blob.
6. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:4978` (`OpDispatchBuilder::BreakOp`, called by `NoExecOp`/`InvalidOp`/`PermissionRestrictedOp`/`UnimplementedOp`) — `_StoreContextGPR(GPRSize, GetRelocatedPC(Op, -Op->InstSize), offsetof(CPUState, rip))` — compiles, via `_GetRelocatedPC` (`OpcodeDispatcher.h:1564`: `Offs = Op->PC + Op->InstSize + Offset - Entry`) and `DEF_OP(EntrypointOffset)` (`JIT/ALUOps.cpp:57-60`: `Constant = Entry + Op->Offset`) → `InsertGuestRIPMove` (`JIT/Arm64Relocations.cpp:90`: an inline per-block `LoadConstant` immediate-load, NOT a runtime lookup) — this is exactly, and only, the trampoline C4-61/62 already disassembled at `elr=0x26D0C02B0`. See Step 2.
7. `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/BranchOps.cpp:154,190` (guest branch/call/jump `ExitFunction`-class ops) — `str RipReg/TMP1,[STATE,#rip]` — writes State.rip to the GUEST-COMPUTED branch target (compile-time-inline constant for direct branches, or a genuine runtime register value for indirect ones, e.g. `jmp eax`) — the ONE mechanism that can legitimately write a truly computed, runtime zero under normal operation, but strictly requires the guest CPU to have EXECUTED a branch instruction first; C4-58's own live guest-register readout at this exact fault already proved every register (including all GPRs) was still at its pristine boot-seed default — ruled out for this specific fault.
8. `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:464` (`WowSyscallHandler::HandleSyscallImpl`) — `Frame->State.rip = ReturnRIP;`, where `ReturnRIP = *(uint32_t*)(Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP])` — a plain native C++ pointer dereference reading 4 bytes directly off the GUEST'S OWN STACK at whatever address ESP currently holds, executed on EVERY real Nt* syscall FEX dispatches through its own `BridgeInstrs::Syscall`/`UnixCall` bridge (confirmed live-relevant: the function it calls, `Wow64SystemServiceEx`, is an external wine/`ntdll`-donor symbol — declared `Source/Windows/include/winternl.h:562`, not implemented in this vendored tree — that performs the real ARM64 `svc` trap this floor's own `kmain.c`/`ntsvc.c` intercept and log by `svc id=NN`, i.e. every syscall in C4-59's own "18x QPC burst" window passes through this exact function). Not a literal `rip=0` in source, not `grep`-discoverable — the value is a RUNTIME memory read, depends entirely on the guest's own stack contents at the moment of dispatch.

**Step 2 — is site #6 (`NoExecOp`/`BreakOp`) an independent zeroing site, or a reporting path?** Algebraic trace, not inference: `_GetRelocatedPC`'s `Offs = Op->PC + Op->InstSize + (-Op->InstSize) - Entry(frontend) = Op->PC - Entry(frontend)`; `DEF_OP(EntrypointOffset)`'s `Constant = Entry(JIT backend) + Offs`. Both `Entry`s are set, once per compile, from the SAME `GuestRIP` parameter threaded through `ContextImpl::CompileBlock`→`GenerateIR`→`OpDispatchBuilder::BeginFunction` (`Entry=RIP`, `OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:3979`) on the frontend side, and `ContextImpl::CompileCode`→`CPUBackend::CompileCode`→`this->Entry=Entry` (`JIT.cpp:824`) on the backend side — no code path sets them independently. Substituting: `Constant = Entry(backend) + Op->PC - Entry(frontend) = Op->PC` (the two `Entry`s cancel exactly, REGARDLESS of what `GuestRIP` actually is). **This proves `BreakOp`'s own RIP-store always bakes in `Op->PC` — the literal guest address of the instruction the frontend decoder determined was invalid/unmapped/no-exec — never an independently-computed or defaulted zero.** For this constant to compile to exactly 0, the very FIRST instruction of the block being compiled (`Op->PC == Entry == GuestRIP`, since `BreakOp` fires on `!BlockInstructionsLength`, i.e. the first-instruction position, per `Core.cpp`'s own `if (!BlockInstructionsLength) { ... NoExecOp(...); }` gate) must itself be at guest address 0x0 — meaning the dispatch loop's OWN block-cache-miss path (`Dispatcher.cpp`'s `NoBlock` label, fed by `ldr RipReg,[STATE,#rip]` at `AbsoluteLoopTopAddress`, `Dispatcher.cpp:147`, immediately followed by `CompileBlock(CTX,Frame,RipReg,0)` on any L1/L2/L3 cache miss) must have already read `State.rip==0` from memory BEFORE this NoExecOp/BreakOp/trampoline sequence ever starts. **This narrows the C4-59 "zeroing event" window from "somewhere inside FEXCore's native dispatch-loop code" down to one specific point: the value must already be 0 in memory strictly BEFORE the single `ldr RipReg,[STATE,#rip]` load at `AbsoluteLoopTopAddress` for this one dispatch-loop iteration** — everything downstream of that load (the cache lookup, the `NoBlock` fallback, `CompileBlock`, the frontend decode failure, `NoExecOp`, `BreakOp`, the trampoline itself) is proven, by this atom's own source trace, to behave correctly and merely relay/report whatever `State.rip` already held.

**Step 3 — candidate #8, plausibility for hello8-vs-hello9, genuinely unconfirmed**: `HandleSyscallImpl`'s guest-stack-sourced `ReturnRIP` write is exercised on every syscall in the exact window C4-59 already measured as byte-identical in TYPE/COUNT/POSITION between hello8 and hello9 (the 18x `NtQueryPerformanceCounter` + `NtClose` + `NtTestAlert` + `NtFlushInstructionCache` + `NtProtectVirtualMemory` burst immediately before the divergence point) — but its OUTPUT depends on the guest's OWN ESP register and the 4 bytes sitting at that stack address at each call, neither of which this atom's static source read can inspect (no source-level condition gates this differently for hello8 vs hello9 — any difference would have to come from the guest's actual runtime stack layout, which could plausibly differ for a larger/import-bearing image per hello9's own already-documented image-shape differences, C4-57). This is NOT ruled in or out by source reading alone — it is the single most concrete, NEW "next place to instrument" this atom can name: a live probe dumping `Frame->State.gregs[REG_RSP]` and the 4 bytes at that address on every `HandleSyscallImpl`/syscall-bridge dispatch in the shared-tail window, for both hello8 and hello9, diffed.

**Step 4 — verdict, per this atom's own instructions**: no site anywhere in the vendored FEXCore/Windows-WOW64 source explicitly writes a literal-zero `State.rip` under any condition reachable in this boot's own flow. The fault-shape's own mechanism (`NoExecOp`/`BreakOp`/`GuestSignal_SIGSEGV`, already identified by C4-61/62) is now PROVEN (algebraic source trace, not inference) to be a pure downstream reporting path, not the zeroing site itself — a genuine, useful narrowing result, not a repeat of C4-59's own runtime-measured conclusion (it confirms the SAME conclusion from the opposite, source-level angle, and additionally narrows the "where" down to one specific load instruction, `Dispatcher.cpp:147`). One plausible but unconfirmed candidate (`HandleSyscallImpl`'s guest-stack-sourced RIP restore, `Module.cpp:464`) is named for a future live-tracing atom. Both remaining candidates live entirely inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob (`FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/{Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp,JIT/*.cpp,OpcodeDispatcher.cpp}`, `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`) — none are reachable or patchable from this floor's own `kernel-arm/zoneA/*.c` sources: this floor's own C code supplies neither the JIT's internal `Entry`/`GuestRIP` bookkeeping (candidate #6) nor the guest's own ESP/stack contents at the exact moment `HandleSyscallImpl` fires natively, before this floor's own `svc` trap handler ever runs (candidate #8). **Not floor-satisfiable — same verdict as C4-58/59, now confirmed via a full source-level trace rather than pure runtime measurement alone. No fix applied, no code changed.**

**Bonus, resolves a residual C4-59 uncertainty**: C4-59's own hand-derived `State.rip` offset (`0x18` within `CpuStateFrame.State`) was flagged as NOT independently compile-checked (blocked by a missing `<malloc.h>`/uncommitted `fmt` submodule on the build host). This atom directly re-read `FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h`'s own field list/`static_assert`s (`InlineJITBlockHeader`@0, `DeferredSignalRefCount`@8, `pf_raw`@16, `af_raw`@20, `rip`@24=`0x18` after 8-byte alignment) and confirms the offset by direct structural reading of the real header (not a compile, but a full field-order read, not a guess) — `0x18` is correct. Does not change any prior atom's own verdict, but removes a previously-open residual doubt about the diagnostic's own correctness.

**Commits**: docs-only, this update (`HANDOFF.md`/`FEX-MAP.md` §C4-63) — no `kernel-arm/zoneA` source touched, no build, no boot performed (source-reading-only atom, per its own explicit law). Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-62 — TESTED (fault-as-signal theory) AND MATCHED IN SOURCE, but the match does not change our floor's own classification: `elr=0x26D0C02B0` (`mov w1,#0; ldr x1,[x1]`, C4-61) IS the exact FEXCore-documented `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` deliberate self-fault trampoline (vendored `FEX/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp:414-430`, source comment "Force a SIGSEGV by loading zero"), and its consumer (`FEX::Windows::HandleGuestException`, `FEX/Source/Windows/Common/Exception.h`) is gated on `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData`, not on the raw ARM64 ESR/DFSC/FAR bits we hand it. This floor's existing classification for this exact fault shape (`ec==0x25`, generic Data Abort branch → `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`, `params=2`) is already the SAME classification this project's own prior `NoExecOp` self-faults (kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0, exe_entry, the C4-27 stub page — all documented C4-30/31/33/45/46/53/54) used and were correctly delivered under — there is no missing branch to add, unlike the C4-38/39/40 alignment case (where the correct status code, `STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT`, was genuinely never produced before that fix). No fix applied.

**Task's own premise, re-tested**: whether `elr=0x26D0C02B0` is a deliberate fault-as-signal idiom that a FEX-internal handler is supposed to catch, gated on receiving the correct NT status code from this floor (the same bug-class shape as C4-38/39/40's alignment fix). Answer: it IS a deliberate fault-as-signal idiom (confirmed against real FEXCore source, not inferred) — but the FEX-side handler that catches it is NOT gated on our raw ESR/DFSC classification at all, and this floor's EXISTING classification for this fault shape is already what that handler expects — so there is no analogous "missing branch" to add.

**Step 1 — wider disassembly context, elr-128..elr+64 (192 bytes total)**: C4-61's own window only covered `elr-32..elr+64`. Extended `kmain.c`'s existing `elr==0x26D0C02B0`-gated diagnostic (`sync_try_guest_exception`) with a second dump, `elr-128..elr-32` (24 more words, none previously captured), +18 lines. Live capture (temp hello9 swap, `scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, receipt `scratch/c4-62_hello9_boot_receipt.log`) disassembled with `aarch64-elf-objdump -D -b binary -m aarch64`, combined with C4-61's own already-captured `elr-32..elr+64` window:
```
elr-128: stp x5,x6,[x28,#48]        (tail of a preceding block's own SpillStaticRegs, block start outside this window)
elr-124: stp x8,x9,[x28,#64]

elr-104: st1 {v20.2d-v23.2d},[x0],#64
elr-100: hlt #0                      <- terminal instruction of a SpillStaticRegs+hlt(0) block
elr-96:  mrs x0,nzcv
elr-92:  str w0,[x28,#1032]
elr-88:  str x25,[x28,#176]

elr-80:  stp x5,x6,[x28,#48]         <- next SpillStaticRegs call restarts here
elr-76:  stp x8,x9,[x28,#64]
elr-72:  stp x10,x11,[x28,#80]
elr-68:  stp w26,w27,[x28,#16]
elr-64:  add x0,x28,#0x1c0
elr-60:  st1 {v16.2d-v19.2d},[x0],#64
elr-56:  st1 {v20.2d-v23.2d},[x0],#64
elr-52:  brk #0                      <- terminal instruction of a SpillStaticRegs+brk(0) block
elr-48:  mrs x0,nzcv

elr-28..elr-8: (rest of the SAME SpillStaticRegs sequence, C4-61's own receipt)
elr-4:   mov w1,#0x0
elr:     ldr x1,[x1]                 <-- FAULT (C4-61)
elr+4..elr+28: mrs/str/str/stp -- start of the NEXT block's own SpillStaticRegs (C4-61's own receipt)
```

**Conditional-branch finding**: NONE. Every one of the 40 instructions across the full 192-byte window (`elr-128..elr+64`) decodes to a register spill (`stp`/`st1`), a fixed terminal trap (`hlt #0` / `brk #0` / `mov w1,#0`+`ldr x1,[x1]`), or `mrs`/`str` epilogue-shaped code belonging to the immediately following block — zero `cbz`/`cbnz`/`b.cond`/`tbz`/`tbnz` instructions anywhere in this window. The fault is reached via straight-line fallthrough in the compiled layout, not a runtime conditional skip within this arena region.

**Step 2 — FEX source match, exact**: `FEX/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp:394-430` (vendored tree, `./FEX`) emits, in this atom's own confirmed live memory order, THREE consecutive fixed trampoline blocks — `GuestSignal_SIGILL` (`SpillStaticRegs(TMP1); hlt(0);`), `GuestSignal_SIGTRAP` (`SpillStaticRegs(TMP1); brk(0);`), `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` (`SpillStaticRegs(TMP1);` then, quoted verbatim from source: `// Force a SIGSEGV by loading zero` / `LoadConstant(ARMEmitter::Size::i64Bit, ARMEmitter::Reg::r1, 0); ldr(ARMEmitter::XReg::x1, ARMEmitter::Reg::r1);`) — this is a byte-exact, instruction-for-instruction match to the `hlt`/`brk`/`mov w1,#0;ldr x1,[x1]` triad Step 1 disassembled, in the same order, with the same `SpillStaticRegs`-shaped prologue each time. `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV` is reached ONLY via `IR::Break(FAULT_SIGSEGV)` (`FEX/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MiscOps.cpp`, `DEF_OP(Break)`, already partially cited by C4-33's own FEX-MAP.md entry): `IR::Break` FIRST writes `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData` (`FaultToTopAndGeneratedException=1`, `Signal`/`TrapNo`/`si_code`/`err_code` from the IR op's own `Reason`, chosen at JIT-compile time by the x86 frontend — `OpcodeDispatcher.cpp`'s `NoExecOp`/`PermissionRestrictedOp`/`INT n`/`INTO`/`HLT` emitters, per FEX-MAP.md's own C4-33 citation) THEN branches (`ldr(TMP1, ...Pointers.GuestSignal_SIGSEGV); br(TMP1);`) into this trampoline — i.e. the DECISION to reach this fault is made earlier, at JIT-compile time inside `IR::Break`'s own emission, not by any runtime condition inside the trampoline itself (matching Step 1's own finding).

The consumer of `SynchronousFaultData` is `FEX::Windows::HandleGuestException` (`FEX/Source/Windows/Common/Exception.h:12-79`) — gated SOLELY on `Fault.FaultToTopAndGeneratedException`/`Fault.Signal`/`Fault.TrapNo`/`Fault.err_code` (read from `SynchronousFaultData`), NOT on the incoming native `EXCEPTION_RECORD`'s `ExceptionCode`/`ExceptionInformation` we supply beyond having reached this consume path at all — no explicit gate in this function requires a SPECIFIC status code value from us (unlike C4-39's `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl`/`EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT` gate, which DID require a specific value this floor originally never produced). For `FAULT_SIGSEGV`+`TrapNo==X86_TRAPNO_PF` (the `NoExecOp`/instruction-fetch signature), it sets `Dst.NumberParameters=2; Dst.ExceptionInformation[0]=EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_FAULT; Dst.ExceptionInformation[1]=Rip;` — and, UNCONDITIONALLY, before any of that, `Dst.ExceptionAddress = reinterpret_cast<void*>(Rip);` where `Rip` is FEX's OWN internally-tracked guest x86 program counter (`State.rip`), not anything this floor's classifier supplies.

**Cross-check against this floor's OWN prior successful deliveries**: this project's own FEX-MAP.md (§C4-30/31/33, `kmain.c`'s pre-existing `SynchronousFaultData` probe) already establishes that EVERY prior `NoExecOp` self-fault on this floor (`exe_entry`, `kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, the C4-27 stub page) carried the identical signature — `Signal=SIGSEGV(0xB) TrapNo=PF(0xE) si_code=2 err_code=0x15(PROT|USER|INSTR)` — and was delivered end-to-end correctly under this floor's SAME `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`/`params=2` classification (the generic `ec==0x24||0x25` Data-Abort branch, `kmain.c`'s `sync_try_guest_exception` — no `NoExecOp`-specific branch has ever existed or been needed there). Re-read (no rebuild needed) the ALREADY-ON-DISK `scratch/c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log`'s own `SynchronousFaultData` capture at the terminal `elr=0x26D0C02B0` fault (the SAME fault this atom targets): `raw=0x15020E0B01 FaultToTopAndGeneratedException=1 Signal=0xB TrapNo=0xE si_code=2 err_code=0x15` — BYTE-IDENTICAL to the canonical `NoExecOp` fingerprint every earlier, successfully-delivered instance also carried. This fault is not a new, unhandled signal class; it is the SAME class this floor's classifier already handles correctly.

**Step 3/4 verdict**: the task's own condition-3 ("FEX's own source shows a handler that expects THIS kind of fault ... PROVIDED it receives the right NT exception status code from us") is satisfied on the SOURCE-MATCH half (a real handler, `HandleGuestException`, does exist and does expect exactly this fault shape) but NOT on the "provided the right status code" half — because this floor's status code for this fault shape (`STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`) is ALREADY the right/expected one, proven not asserted, by every prior successful `NoExecOp` delivery under the identical classification. There is no narrower, `dfsc==0x06 AND far==0 AND elr-in-arena`-gated branch to add, because the branch this floor already has (unconditional `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` for any non-alignment Data Abort) is not wrong for this case — changing it would not alter `HandleGuestException`'s own `Rip`-sourced `ExceptionAddress`/`ExceptionInformation[1]` output, since neither is derived from our status code at all. Per the task's own condition-4 spirit (no actionable gap found that would change behavior): **no fix applied**.

**Why `addr=0x0` specifically, tying this back to C4-58/59 rather than reopening it**: `HandleGuestException`'s `ExceptionAddress`/`ExceptionInformation[1]` both resolve to `Rip` = FEX's own tracked `State.rip`, which C4-58/59 already live-measured to be exactly `0x0` at this precise fault ("the zeroing event", the FIRST and ONLY zero `State.rip` value either boot ever produces, located strictly inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll`'s own native dispatch-loop bookkeeping between the last successful `NtContinue` resume and this fault, with zero syscall/exception boundary crossing it visible to this floor). Since no NT status code choice this floor makes can change `State.rip`'s value, this atom's own theory (a misclassification masking a would-be self-heal) does not hold for the OBSERVED SYMPTOM either: even a hypothetically "more correct" status code would still yield `addr=0x0`, because the address comes from FEX's own already-corrupted internal state, not from anything this floor's exception classifier controls.

**hello9 boot re-tested anyway** (temp swap, exact established method — `shell.c`/`build.sh` two-line diff, `mkpe32.sh` rebuild — checkpoint-committed before and after, fully reverted, net-zero vs pre-swap `82eec0a`): receipt `scratch/c4-62_hello9_boot_receipt.log`. Outcome UNCHANGED from C4-56/57/58/59/61: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, `FirstChance=FALSE`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`; guest `Eip` frozen at `0x47661000` (`hello9_x86.exe+0x1000`, `exe_entry`) at termination, matching the boot-seed value exactly — the guest CPU never advanced. `hello9_x86.c`'s own milestone string, `"hello9: the door opens\n"` (`WriteFile`), never appears anywhere in the transcript — the milestone is **NOT reached**, consistent with C4-58/59's own already-established verdict that the guest never leaves `exe_entry` regardless of fix attempts downstream of that point.

**Canonical `pe10 fex` reconfirmed both before and after the swap**: pre-swap (`scratch/c4-62_pe10_fex_canonical_prefix_receipt.log`) and post-revert (`scratch/c4-62_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt.log`, run LAST) both show `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` STILL HOLDS**; the new C4-62 diagnostic is silent on both canonical boots (0 occurrences — canonical hello8 never reaches `elr=0x26D0C02B0`).

**Commits**: source-only. (1) `kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c` (`01a14c1`) — the wider `elr-128..-32` pre-context dump, gated to `elr==0x26D0C02B0`, +18 lines, measurement only. ## C4-61 — NAMED: the null register at the fatal `elr=0x26D0C02B0` fault is `x1`, set by the immediately preceding instruction as a hardcoded immediate zero (`mov w1, #0`), not a load from any structure field this project has reverse-engineered offsets for. No match against documented structures. No fix applied (nothing floor-side to patch).

**Step 1 — existing diagnostics, checked before adding anything**: grepped `kmain.c` for the fault-delivery path (`CHALICE ARENA`/`CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT`/`esr=`/`elr=`). The guest-exception-delivery function (`sync_try_guest_exception`) already snapshots the FULL SAVEALL_SYNC register frame into `regs_safe[0..30]` (`regs_snap`, C4-21/22) but only ever PRINTS two of them at this fault path (`resolve_line("lr ", regs_safe[30])`, `resolve_line("x0 ", regs_safe[0])`) — never a full x0-x30 dump. A full x0-x30 dump DOES already exist elsewhere in the same file (`sync_handler`, the raw-hardware-fault fatal path used when no self-heal/guest-exception path applies), but that function does not run for this fault — this fault takes the guest-exception-delivery path instead, confirmed by the transcript ending in a clean `NtRaiseException`/second-chance-terminate, not a bare kernel abort. Grepped every existing scratch log (`c4-5[6-9]_*.log`, `c4-60_*.log`) for `elr=0x26D0C02B0`: present in four (`c4-56_hello9_swap_reattempt_receipt.log`, `c4-57_hello9_swap_binder_receipt.log`, `c4-59_hello9_swap_receipt_full.log`, `c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log`) — all four already carry the existing `CHALICE C4-30 ELR WINDOW @elr-32..+32` print (16 raw ARM64 words, 64 bytes, elr itself at window index 8), i.e. the actual faulting instruction bytes were already on disk before this atom touched anything; none carry a full x0-x30 dump.

**Step 2 — static vs dynamic**: the SAME transcripts already print `CHALICE REGION elr=0x26D0C02B0 -> reserve-commit-arena committed base=0x26D0C0000 size=0x4000` unconditionally (`nt_fault_region`, pre-existing classifier, no new code needed) — this address is DYNAMIC, inside a bump-allocated 0x4000-byte arena, the same "FEX's own dispatcher trampoline class" region C4-17/18/21 already characterized (source comment, `kmain.c` ~line 570) — NOT the static vendored `libwow64fex.dll` image (whose ranges this file resolves separately via `map-fex-offsets.sh`/direct `objdump` on the blob, used for a different address family entirely). Read bytes accordingly: from the live memory window already captured, not from the on-disk blob file.

**Step 3 — diagnostic added, narrow, one-address-only** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c`, `sync_try_guest_exception`, +27 lines): a full `x0`-`x30` dump (reusing `regs_safe[]`, already captured) plus a 64-byte instruction hexdump starting at `elr`, gated strictly to `elr==0x26D0C02B0` — never fires on the ~20 unrelated shared self-heal cycles (20 different `elr` values, dfsc=0x21, C4-59) or on canonical hello8 (which never reaches this address). Built clean (`GAIA_NO_RUN=1 ./build.sh`, wrapped in `timeout 180`), checkpoint-committed. Canonical `pe10 fex` re-run FIRST, confirming the diagnostic is silent and `ret=42` unaffected (`scratch/c4-61_pe10_fex_canonical_prefix_receipt.log`). Then the C4-55..60-established hello9 swap method (`shell.c`'s `pe10` verb + `build.sh`'s disk-image mapping, exact same two-line diff every prior hello9 atom used, confirmed via `git show 9c8eb2f`) re-applied, `hello9_x86.exe` rebuilt via `mkpe32.sh`, checkpoint-committed, booted (`timeout 240 python3 scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, receipt `scratch/c4-61_hello9_boot_receipt.log` + full transcript `scratch/c4-61_hello9_boot_receipt_full.log`, 9017 lines, no command ran past its timeout).

**Register receipt, live, matches prediction exactly**:
```
CHALICE C4-61 FULL REGS @elr=0x26D0C02B0:
  x0=0x270001380 x1=0x0000000000000000 x2=0x2300009B0 x3=0x0
  x4=0x0 x5=0x0 x6=0x0 x7=0x0
  x8=0x488CBFDC x9=0x488CBFFC x10=0x0 x11=0x0
  x12=0x100 x13=0x0 x14=0x0 x15=0x2300106E0
  x16=0x47088324 x17=0x4116B470 x18=0x4116C120 x19=0x4116B6E0
  x20=0x0 x21=0x4766101B x22=0x1 x23=0x5A0610B8
  x24=0x4116BBC0 x25=0x299980FF0 x26=0x488CBFE0 x27=0xB7734007
  x28=0x270001140
  x29(fp)=0x4116BCF0 x30(lr)=0x26D0C017C
CHALICE C4-61 ELR BYTES @elr..+64: 0xF9400021 0xD53B4200 0xB9040B80 0xF9005B99 0xA9021F84 0xA9031B85 0xA9042788 0xA9052F8A 0x29026F9A 0x91070380 0x4C9F2C10 0x4C9F2C14 0x5800BF00 0xAA1C03E1 0x5800BF02 0xD63F0040
```
`x1=0x0`, live, matching `far=0x0` exactly (the same register that gets dereferenced). `x28=0x270001140` matches this transcript's own `CurrentFrame=0x270001140` (TLS PROBE print, same boot) — confirms x28 is FEXCore's reserved STATE-frame-pointer register at this fault, consistent with the file's own existing `CpuStateFrame`/`CurrentFrame` citations.

**Instruction-level disassembly** (bytes from the pre-existing `c4-59_hello9_v2_receipt_full.log`'s own `ELR WINDOW` print, byte-identical to this atom's own fresh capture above — same fixed host address, same code, every atom that has hit it): wrote the 16-word/64-byte window to a raw binary file, disassembled with `aarch64-elf-objdump -D -b binary -m aarch64` (same tool class prior atoms used for the vendored blob):
```
   0:	a9031b85 	stp	x5, x6, [x28, #48]
   4:	a9042788 	stp	x8, x9, [x28, #64]
   8:	a9052f8a 	stp	x10, x11, [x28, #80]
   c:	29026f9a 	stp	w26, w27, [x28, #16]
  10:	91070380 	add	x0, x28, #0x1c0
  14:	4c9f2c10 	st1	{v16.2d-v19.2d}, [x0], #64
  18:	4c9f2c14 	st1	{v20.2d-v23.2d}, [x0], #64
  1c:	52800001 	mov	w1, #0x0                   // #0
  20:	f9400021 	ldr	x1, [x1]                    <-- FAULT (elr)
  24:	d53b4200 	mrs	x0, nzcv
  28:	b9040b80 	str	w0, [x28, #1032]
  2c:	f9005b99 	str	x25, [x28, #176]
  30:	a9021f84 	stp	x4, x7, [x28, #32]
```
**Step 4 — origin of the null value**: the instruction 2 slots before the fault (`elr-4`, offset `0x1c` in the window above) is `mov w1, #0x0` — a plain `MOVZ` immediate-zero into `w1`, which zero-extends into `x1`. There is no intervening load, no base-register-plus-offset dereference between this `mov` and the faulting `ldr x1, [x1]` — `x1`'s value is a literal constant compiled directly into this code, not a runtime read of any structure. The surrounding code (the block above the `mov`) is a register-context SAVE sequence — `stp` pairs of `x5/x6`, `x8/x9`, `x10/x11`, `w26/w27`, then `st1` vector stores of `v16`-`v23` — all addressed off `x28` (confirmed `x28`==`CurrentFrame`, live, this atom's own register receipt above), i.e. this is FEXCore's own native state-checkpoint code, not a translation of guest x86 instructions. `x30` (`lr`, the return address) is `0x26D0C017C`, inside the SAME 0x4000-byte arena, ~0x134 bytes before the fault — consistent with this being a called subroutine within the dispatcher/trampoline block, not straight-line code that fell through from somewhere else.

**Step 5 — cross-reference against documented structures**: this project's own reverse-engineered FEX offsets (FEX-MAP.md §C4-33/34/49/50/51/52/53) are all MEMORY-LOAD-based struct fields read via `ldr`/`str` at a fixed `base+offset` — `InternalThreadState::CurrentFrame` (offset 0), `CpuStateFrame::State.rip` (offset `0x18`), `SynchronousFaultData` (offset `0x5D8`), `InvalidationTracker`'s `XIntervals`/`this` global (`ImageBase+0x34c430`), `Decoder`'s `ExecutableRangeBase`/`End`/`InstStream` fields (offsets `0x68`/`0x70`/`0x80`). None of them are relevant here: `x1`'s value in this instance does not come from a memory load at all — it is a compiled-in immediate. **No match against any already-documented structure field. Said plainly, per this atom's own instruction: no match found, not chased further, no theory invented to force one.**

One factual, already-grounded (not new-theory) observation worth recording rather than a guess: this exact `elr` address sits in the same source-comment-identified region (`kmain.c`'s own pre-existing citation of `IR::Break`, `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MiscOps.cpp:65-77`, "immediately before branching to the GuestSignal_SIGSEGV self-fault trampoline") this file already uses to explain HOW FEXCore synthesizes guest exceptions — that mechanism's own documented design is to deliberately execute a trapping memory access to generate a hardware fault the native signal/exception path then intercepts. A hardcoded null-pointer dereference is consistent with that already-cited design (a guaranteed trap, not a real bug in the sense of an uninitialized field), but this is not offered as a NEW theory — it does not change the step-5 verdict (no matching struct field) and is not chased further.

**Step 6 — fix proposal**: none. Step 5 does not land on a concrete, addressable, floor-satisfiable gap — the null value is a constant baked into FEXCore's own generated dispatcher code inside the vendored `libwow64fex.dll` blob, not a field this kernel's own C sources supply, compute, or could patch. Per this atom's own condition ("if it requires deeper vendored-library changes or isn't clearly safe, name it and STOP without applying anything"): named, not applied.

**Revert + canonical reconfirm**: hello9 swap (`shell.c`/`build.sh`) fully reverted (checkpoint `8063913`), confirmed net-zero via `git diff` against the pre-swap commit; `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` left untracked (rebuildable via `mkpe32.sh`, same convention every prior hello9 atom used); build-artifact/PE-timestamp churn (`chalice/{fake-ntdll.dll,hello1-7.exe,hello8_x86.exe,mini.dll}`, `zoneA/pehello_bin.c`) reverted before every commit this atom. Canonical `pe10 fex` re-run AFTER the full revert (`scratch/c4-61_pe10_fex_canonical_final_receipt.log`): `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` STILL HOLDS**, no regression from the new diagnostic (silent on this boot, confirmed both before and after the swap).

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` → `pe8 ret=78`, GATE PASS (`scratch/c4-61_pe8_gate.log`). `pe9_gate.py` → both backends `pe9 ret=0`/`pe9 fex ret=0`, GATE PASS (`scratch/c4-61_pe9_gate.log`). `ramfb_gate.py` → 7/7 pattern checks OK, GATE PASS (`scratch/c4-61_ramfb_gate.log`).

**Commits**: source-only. (1) `kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c` — the narrow x0-x30/instruction-byte diagnostic, gated to `elr==0x26D0C02B0`, +27 lines, checkpoint before build/boot. ## C4-60 — NAMED (unconditional `NtProtectVirtualMemory` raw-arg diagnostic, no vendored-blob disasm): hello8's own "extra JIT round" (C4-59's own sharpened clue) targets this floor's OWN C4-27 BOP-stub page, already notify-registered by the existing C4-54 fix — not a new floor-side gap, no fix applied.

**Diagnostic added** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, `NtProtectVirtualMemory`, +13 lines): an unconditional print of `BaseAddress`/`*BaseAddress`/`RegionSize`/`*RegionSize`/`NewProtect`, placed BEFORE the existing null/zero-size early return — makes every call visible in the transcript, including ones that would otherwise bail out invisibly with `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER`.

**Live `pe10 fex` re-run** (`scratch/c4-60_pe10_fex_canonical_receipt_full.log`, 8976 lines): the transcript's LAST `CHALICE C4-60: NtProtectVirtualMemory` print before the clean `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` / `pe10 ret=42` exit (line 8964 of 8976) reads `BaseAddress=0x488D0000 RegionSize=0x1000 NewProtect=0x20` (`PAGE_EXECUTE_READ`). Immediately preceding it (line 8943) is a SECOND-to-last call, `BaseAddress=0x47661000` (`exe_entry`) `RegionSize=0x1000 NewProtect=0x20` — both single-page protect-down calls, same shape. After the 0x488D0000 call: one `NtQuerySystemTime`(id=90, unimplemented) call, THEN `NtFlushInstructionCache`(id=147), THEN `NtTerminateProcess`(id=44) — `NtFlushInstructionCache` follows the target call but NOT immediately (one intervening unimplemented syscall).

**Address identity, cross-referenced against every documented image base in this file's own C4-4x/5x sections**: `0x488D0000` is NEITHER inside `hello8_x86.exe`'s own mapped range (`0x47660000`-`0x47666000`) NOR `syswow64/kernel32.dll` (`0x5D090000`+) NOR `syswow64/kernelbase.dll` (`0x5E1C0000`+) NOR `syswow64/ntdll.dll`, NOR a FEX JIT-cache address (the recurring `elr=0x26D0C02B0`-class addresses C4-58/59 named are all in the `0x2988xxxx`/`0x26D0xxxx` ranges, a different region entirely). It IS the C4-27 syscall-hinge BOP-stub page (`nt_territory_alloc(16)`, `ntboot.c`) — the SAME fixed address C4-53 named as a new-fault target and C4-54 fixed via retroactive `InvalidationTracker` registration. Confirmed live, same transcript: two `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification base=0x488D0000 size=0x10 prot=0x40` prints fire much earlier (once per subsequent dynamic x86 image map — kernel32.dll then kernelbase.dll — same "harmless re-insert both times" behavior C4-54 documented), from the RETROACTIVE one-shot call `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` makes the moment `nt_wow64_ready` first flips true — NOT from any `NtProtectVirtualMemory` call on this address.

**Filter-logic verdict**: `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect` (the function `NtProtectVirtualMemory` calls at every success return) applies UNCONDITIONALLY to any nonzero `(base,size)` once `nt_wow64_ready` is true and `libwow64fex.dll`'s base is resolved — no `Machine`/image-type filter of its own. The SEPARATE `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` (wired to `NtMapViewOfSection`) DOES filter on `Machine==0x14C` (guest x86 images only) and excludes `ntdll.dll` — but the BOP-stub page is never mapped via `NtMapViewOfSection` at all (it has no PE header, it's a raw kernel-side allocation), so it structurally cannot satisfy that filter; this is exactly the donor-gap class C4-54 already named and closed via a SEPARATE, unconditional one-shot registration call (`nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect(c454_stub_base, c454_stub_size, PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, teb)`) fired from inside `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` itself at the `nt_wow64_ready` flip point, independent of which image triggered the flip. Verdict: the C4-60 target already satisfies the applicable filter — it is already tracked, via the C4-54 path, before this final protect-down call ever fires. No new gap.

**Side curiosity, noted not chased**: the specific final protect-down call itself (targeting `0x488D0000`, `NewProtect=0x20`) does not produce its OWN matching `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` print (nor do most other single-page protect-down calls after the `nt_wow64_ready` flip — only whole-DLL-section save/restore pairs consistently do). Not chased this atom — immaterial to the outcome, since the range was already tracked via the earlier retroactive registration and the boot completes cleanly (`ret=42`) regardless of whether this specific call re-notifies.

**Mechanistic conclusion, answers C4-59's own "sharper clue"**: hello8's "extra round" (one more `NtProtectVirtualMemory`+`NtFlushInstructionCache` pair than hello9) is hello8's OWN C4-27 BOP-stub JIT-compile-and-protect-down step — `PeMain`'s bare `ret` lands in the synthetic BOP stub (C4-27/55), which FEX then JIT-compiles and protects down from RWX to EXECUTE_READ before the stub's own `NtTerminateProcess(-1,42)` call executes. hello9 cannot structurally take this same round, independent of any notify/registration gap: per C4-57/58/59 (unchanged this atom), hello9's guest Eip never leaves `exe_entry` at all — `PeMain` is never reached, so its `ret` never lands in the BOP stub, so the stub's own JIT-compile/protect/flush round never happens. The "missing pair" C4-59 measured is not a floor-side omission but a direct structural consequence of hello9 never reaching the code path that needs it.

**No fix applied, per this atom's own explicit condition**: steps 4+5 do not name a concrete, fixable floor-side gap (the target is already correctly notify-registered) — the underlying reason hello9 lacks the round is the ALREADY-DIAGNOSED (C4-58/59) fact that hello9's Eip never advances, confirmed blob-internal/unobservable from this floor's own C sources.
