## C4-59 — MEASURED (live State.rip instrumentation, no vendored-blob disasm): C4-58's own "21 identical cycles at elr=0x26D0C02B0" framing CORRECTED by direct measurement — the true shared prefix is 20 byte-identical `STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT` (dfsc=0x21) self-heal cycles at 20 DIFFERENT JIT addresses (never `0x26D0C02B0`), then an identical marker-reset (`NtSetInformationThread` eip=BOP-marker then eip=exe_entry, State.rip never zero, byte-identical both boots) then an identical 18x failed `NtQueryPerformanceCounter`(svc id=49) burst — hello8 then takes a 2nd JIT-compile/protect/flush round (`NtProtectVirtualMemory`+`NtFlushInstructionCache` again) and exits cleanly; hello9's own would-be 2nd round is missing exactly that pair and instead takes ONE NEW-CLASS fault (`STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`, dfsc=0x06 translation/null-deref, first-ever occurrence of `elr=0x26D0C02B0` in either transcript) whose OWN delivery is the first and only point live `State.rip` reads 0. Built a shared `nt_wow64_live_state_rip` reader (TEB→TlsSlots[16]→`InternalThreadState::CurrentFrame`→`CpuStateFrame::State.rip`, offsets independently re-derived AND compile-checked against the real `FEX/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/{Core/CoreState.h,Debug/InternalThreadState.h}` headers present in this quarry, not hand-guessed) and wired it into every guest-exception delivery, every `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context)` write, and (uncapped, was capped at 12) every `NtContinue` self-heal resume. Result: live State.rip tracks correctly and stays nonzero through EVERY single one of hello9's own 20 self-heal cycles and its own marker-reset — the zeroing event is PROVEN to occur strictly between the last observed valid read (State.rip=0x58040000, at the marker-reset's own SET syscall) and the new fault's own delivery, with ZERO further syscalls/exception-deliveries in that window besides the SAME 18x-QPC-burst+NtClose+NtTestAlert+NtFlushInstructionCache sequence hello8 ALSO takes identically — i.e. the zeroing genuinely happens inside FEXCore's own native ARM64 dispatch-loop code, unobservable from any syscall/fault boundary this floor can hook. All three of the task's own named floor-satisfiable candidates are DIRECTLY REFUTED by this measurement, not guessed away: syscall 49 (`NtQueryPerformanceCounter`, `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt:42`) is called the EXACT SAME 18 times in the EXACT SAME position with the EXACT SAME tolerance in BOTH boots — hello9 does not need it any more than hello8; `NtContinue`'s own context round-trip never loses rip (every one of hello9's 20 resumes reads a fully valid, correctly-tracking nonzero State.rip, the last one included); nothing on this floor's own side ever seeds/observes a zero value before the fault itself (the marker-reset SET call supplies a valid nonzero eip in both boots, byte-identical). New sharpened clue for next atom: hello9's own analogous JIT round is short exactly one `NtProtectVirtualMemory`+`NtFlushInstructionCache` pair vs hello8's — consistent with FEX's own internal JIT compiler needing (and failing partway through) more work for hello9's larger/import-bearing `exe_entry` block. Not floor-satisfiable this atom (confirmed blob-internal, now to the tightest measurable window) — documented, STOPPED, zero fix applied. Canonical `pe10 fex ret=42` + full gate ladder reconfirmed, no regression. Full State.rip-per-cycle table, syscall-sequence receipts, verdict → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-59**. Live 2026-07-24.

## C4-57 — kernel-side i386 IAT binder (Road B) built + engages correctly (2/2 hello9 imports resolved+bound), but the hello9 boot still faults BYTE-IDENTICAL to C4-56's pre-binder receipt — proves the unbound-IAT theory alone does NOT explain the crash; a NEW, deeper frontier named: guest Eip never leaves `exe_entry` at all, bind or no bind.

**Road A (route the main EXE through wine's own guest-loader `fixup_imports`) — evaluated by source-tracing, NOT live-tested, rejected as the wrong-sized change this atom**: C4-42..45 already PROVE wine's real guest-executed loader chain (`ntdll32 LdrInitializeThunk -> loader_init -> build_main_module(LDR_DONT_RESOLVE_REFS) -> fixup_imports(wm, NULL)` on the MAIN module, donor `dlls/ntdll/loader.c:4507-4513`) DOES run and DOES correctly bind DLL-to-DLL imports — but only for the DLLs FEX/Hangover's OWN per-process container bootstrap loads for its bookkeeping (`Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`, native ARM64, `BTCpuProcessInit`-driven) — `kernel32.dll`/`kernelbase.dll` themselves get this treatment. That chain's entry is FEX-internal and never reaches wine's PE-side USER EXE loader at all, because THIS floor's own Phase-0 (`nt_boot_process`, `ntboot.c`) maps the boot EXE directly and seeds the guest's FIRST Eip straight at the EXE's own entry point — never at any `ntdll32 LdrInitializeThunk` — so `build_main_module`/`fixup_imports` never fire for the boot EXE's OWN Import Directory. Road A would require re-architecting Phase-0 to inject the main image through `LdrLoadDll`/`build_main_module` instead of direct entry-seeding — a materially larger, riskier surface change than a self-contained kernel-side binder. Chosen: **Road B**.

**Road B implementation** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, +168 lines, commit `20c104c`): once BOTH `syswow64/kernel32.dll` and `syswow64/kernelbase.dll` are guest-mapped (proven live by the same C4-42..45 chain), walk the boot EXE's OWN PE32 Import Directory (`IMAGE_IMPORT_DESCRIPTOR` array, 4-byte `IMAGE_THUNK_DATA32`/`IMAGE_IMPORT_BY_NAME` shapes — deliberately NOT `pemod.c`'s existing `do_imports` code, which hardcodes 8-byte `IMAGE_THUNK_DATA64` reads/writes for PE32+/DLL-to-DLL imports, the wrong tool per C4-56's own verdict) and resolve each name via the already-`is_pe32`-aware `pe_find_named_export` (C4-36) against those two real, live-mapped images, writing the resolved 32-bit VA directly into the IAT slot. One-shot (`nt_iat_bind_done`), fires from `NtMapViewOfSection` the moment the second of the two DLLs registers (same call-site-driven-notify idiom C4-46/48/53/54 already use) — always strictly before the guest's own seeded Eip ever executes (both DLL loads are driven by FEX's own container bootstrap, which completes long before `BTCpuSimulate` ever reads the synthetic Eip/Esp seed).

**hello9 swap-boot, binder engaged, receipt captured** (temporary re-swap for documentation only, `scratch/c4-57_hello9_swap_binder_receipt.log`, reverted immediately after): binder fired and resolved BOTH imports cleanly —
```
CHALICE C4-57: both syswow64 kernel32/kernelbase mapped -- binding boot exe IAT
CHALICE C4-57: import DLL=KERNEL32.dll
CHALICE C4-57: bound GetStdHandle -> 0x5D09D7AC
CHALICE C4-57: bound WriteFile -> 0x5D09F9BC
CHALICE C4-57: IAT bind done bound=2 failed=0
```
**But the boot fault that follows is BYTE-IDENTICAL to C4-56's own pre-binder receipt** — same `CHALICE ARENA: esr=0x96000006 ec=0x25 iss=0x06 elr=0x26D0C02B0 far=0x0`, same `dfsc=0x06` (translation fault, not alignment), same `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, same `pe10 ret=3221225477`. Critically, the final `CHALICE C4-25 GUEST CTX` dump shows **`Eip=0x47661000` — the exe's OWN entry point, byte-identical to the value seeded at boot start** — the guest program counter NEVER ADVANCED from `exe_entry` at all, bound IAT or not. hello9's own `WriteFile`/`GetStdHandle` calls were never reached (no `door opens` string, no write syscall in the transcript) — not because the IAT was unbound (it was correctly bound this time), but because the guest's own code at `exe_entry` apparently never got dispatched/simulated in the first place.

**NEW FRONTIER named**: the C4-56 "unbound IAT" theory, while a real gap now genuinely fixed by this Road-B binder, is **NOT sufficient to explain the hello9 crash** — with the IAT correctly bound, the SAME fault fires at the SAME JIT-cache address (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, a translation-cache address, not inside `hello9_x86.exe`'s own mapped range `0x47660000-0x47666000`) with the guest Eip frozen at `exe_entry`, meaning whatever faults is upstream of ever simulating hello9's own entry bytes — most likely inside FEX's own per-image JIT-compilation/init path triggered by mapping this SPECIFIC exe (hello9 differs from hello8 in more than imports: real `.idata`/IAT section, larger image, `-lkernel32` link surface) — not a guest-code-level import-call fault at all. **Next-atom pointer**: instrument whether FEX's `BTCpuSimulate`/JIT-translate path is ever entered for hello9's `exe_entry` block at all (a guest-instruction-fetch trace at `0x47661000`, same idiom as `pe10_exec_trace.py`'s `-d exec` fallback) before assuming any further IAT/import work is the blocker — the fault happens BEFORE the guest's own PeMain code, bound imports or not.

**Canonical-state restore + full gate ladder (this atom's actual close-out)**: prior lane's revert (commit `59985fc`) was already byte-shape-identical to canonical `2eecfa8` (confirmed via `git diff 2eecfa8 59985fc` = empty) but left uncommitted rebuild-timestamp churn on disk (PE-header build-timestamp field embedded by every `build.sh`/`mkpe32.sh` run, `kernel-arm/chalice/*.exe`+`kernel-arm/zoneA/pehello_bin.c` — noise, not a real diff) plus untracked `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb`; both cleaned (`git checkout --`, `rm`), re-confirmed net-zero vs `2eecfa8` after EVERY subsequent build in this atom (including the documentation re-swap above and the final gate ladder run below). **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` → `pe8 ret=78`, `GATE PASS`. `pe9_gate.py` → both backends `pe9 ret=0`/`pe9 fex ret=0`, `GATE PASS (required-green, no regression, both CPU backends)`. `ramfb_gate.py` → 7/7 pattern checks OK, `GATE PASS (ramfb pattern correct)`. `pe10 fex` (canonical hello8, final receipt `scratch/c4-57_pe10_fex_final_canonical_receipt.log`) → `NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` → **`pe10 ret=42` STILL HOLDS**. **Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` (the Road-B binder itself, `20c104c`, kept — real, working, permanent fix for the import-binding gap) + the already-existing `d4e49a8`/`59985fc` swap/revert checkpoint pair (net-zero `shell.c`/`build.sh` diff, prior lane). Full receipts/ESR → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-57**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-56 — hello9 WriteFile-milestone leg re-measured: task's own premise (DFSC=0x21 alignment) DISPROVEN by live ESR — `dfsc=0x06` (translation fault, not alignment), so the C4-40 backpatch path was never a candidate here; the AV IS correctly synthesized/delivered (`NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x0`, clean second-chance terminate) — routing is NOT the bug. Live `SynchronousFaultData` readout (`raw=0x15020E0B01`) is byte-identical to C4-30/31's own `NoExecOp` (instruction-fetch) signature: the guest jumps to address `0x0` and faults trying to FETCH there. Root cause, source-traced: `nt_boot_process` (`ntboot.c:327`) maps the boot EXE with `do_imports=0` (by original, correct-for-`hello8` design — `hello8_x86.exe` has zero imports); `hello9_x86.exe` is the FIRST boot EXE with a real Import Directory (`KERNEL32.dll`: `GetStdHandle`/`WriteFile`) and its IAT is never bound — neither by our kernel (`do_imports=0`) nor by the guest's own real Wine/`ntdll32` loader (Phase-0 injects the main image directly, bypassing `LdrLoadDll`/`import_dll` entirely) — so its import slots stay at their unbound, zero, on-disk value, and the first indirect call through either one jumps to `0x0`. **NOT trivial** (would need either routing the main image through the real guest-loader import-fixup pass, or a from-scratch i386-correct kernel-side import binder — `pemod.c`'s existing `do_imports` code is the wrong tool, built for 64-bit PE32+ DLL-to-DLL thunks and a native kernel-stub resolver, not 32-bit guest imports against real `KERNELBASE.dll` exports). Named, not chased. hello9 WriteFile marker NOT reached — NOT the milestone. Swap fully re-applied then fully reverted (checkpoints `e5f3d38`/`b26f7b2`, byte-identical to pre-atom canonical `shell.c`/`build.sh`); `hello9_x86.exe`/`.pdb` untracked. `pe10 fex` post-revert: `ret=42` STILL HOLDS. `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS (one transient drain-timing flake, reproduced clean on rerun) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 — no regression. Commits (source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{shell.c,build.sh}`, both reverted to canonical net-zero diff). Full receipts/ESR-decode/disasm → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-56**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-55 — **`ret=42` ACHIEVED.** Named exactly where the 42 died (a live `ProcessHandle`/`ExitStatus`/guest-Eax/stack receipt at `NtTerminateProcess` itself, no guessing) and fixed it with a single 2-byte `push 0` in the C4-27 stub. Root cause: FEX's own `args_ptr = poppedReturnRSP+4` convention (C4-27's own donor comment) is calibrated for a REAL ntdll `Nt*` stub, whose stack at BOP-trap time ALWAYS carries TWO return-address slots above the args -- (1) the external caller's own return address (real ntdll functions are always reached via a `call`) and (2) the stub's own `call [Wow64Transition]` return address. This floor's C4-27 stub is instead reached via PeMain's bare `ret` (no external `call`, so slot (1) never existed) and only ever pushed 2 args + slot (2) -- ONE slot short, silently shifting BOTH marshaled arguments by 4 bytes.

**Measurement (before touching anything)**: added an unconditional receipt at `NtTerminateProcess` itself -- `ProcessHandle`, `ExitStatus`, and (from the live `WOW64_CPURESERVED` memory block) guest `Eax`/`Eip`/`Esp` + a 16-byte guest-stack-window dump. First boot (pre-fix, `scratch/c4-55_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`): `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0x2A ExitStatus=0x0` -- **`ProcessHandle` arrived as `0x2A` (42 decimal!) instead of the stub's own pushed `-1`, and `ExitStatus` arrived as `0` instead of the stub's own pushed `42`** -- a clean, deterministic ONE-STACK-SLOT arg shift, not corruption, not a missed call, not garbage. (The `ThreadWow64Context` sets seen jumping to `exe_entry`/BOP addresses right before this call, C4-54's own leading theory, turned out to be an unrelated, harmless FEX-internal context-stash bookkeeping artifact -- the REAL call genuinely reached our stub's own two pushed values, one slot too shallow.) The BOP stub DID execute -- confirmed both by this exact arg pattern (only explainable by the stub's own two real pushed values, shifted) and by the pre-existing `<- eip=0x58040000` (BOP address) `ThreadWow64Context` capture already in the C4-54 receipt.

**Fix, one line, source-only (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c`, `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready`)**: inserted one dummy 2-byte `push 0` (`6A 00`) between the stub's `push -1` (ProcessHandle) and `mov eax,0x2C; call BOP`, restoring the exact 4-slot stack shape (`[dummy][retaddr][-1][42]`... arg-relative: `[dummy-as-2nd-retaddr][ProcessHandle][ExitStatus]` above the trap) FEX's formula expects -- the value pushed is never read by anything (pure placeholder), it exists only to occupy the slot a real external caller's return address would have occupied. Stub grew from 13 to 15 bytes, still fits the existing 16-byte `nt_territory_alloc(16)` buffer untouched.

**Confirming `pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-55_pe10_fex_fixed_receipt_full.log`): `CHALICE C4-55: NtTerminateProcess ProcessHandle=0xFFFFFFFF ExitStatus=0x2A` -- both args now EXACTLY correct (`ProcessHandle=-1` matching the stub's own push, `ExitStatus=0x2A=42` matching `hello8_x86.c`'s `PeMain() { return 42; }`) -- **`pe10 ret=42`**. THE DONE-LINE CHAIN CLOSES: `PeMain ret` → C4-27 BOP stub → real `NtTerminateProcess(-1, 42)` → `pe10 ret=42`, crash-free, end-to-end, first time ever.

**hello9_x86 swap attempted, NOT trivial, reverted (new, separate, unchased frontier -- new-fault law)**: built `hello9_x86.exe` (`mkpe32.sh`, already present, unbuilt binary) and temporarily pointed `pe10`'s `nt_boot_process` call at it instead of `hello8_x86.exe` (plus a temporary disk-image mapping addition). Result: a NEW, DIFFERENT crash (`scratch/c4-55_hello9_swap_attempt_receipt.log`) -- `CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` alignment fault at `elr=0x26D0C02B0` (inside kernelbase, a DIFFERENT address than any prior fault) leading to `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005` / `pe10 ret=3221225477`, unrelated to the C4-27 stub (hello9's `PeMain` calls real `GetStdHandle`/`WriteFile` through the full kernel32/kernelbase import machinery -- a materially bigger, separate surface, genuinely next-atom work). Per the task's own explicit condition ("hello9_x86 swap if trivial"), this was NOT trivial -- fully reverted: `shell.c`'s `pe10` verb boots `hello8_x86.exe` again (canonical, unchanged), the temporary disk-image mapping addition removed, `hello9_x86.exe` binary removed (source `.c` untouched, was already present pre-atom); zero net diff against C4-54 other than the intended `ntsvc.c` stub fix. Named here, not chased -- next-atom work.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`scratch/c4-55_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`).

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` -- (1) the C4-55 diagnostic receipt (checkpoint before first boot), (2) the one-line stub fix (checkpoint before confirming boot); the hello9 swap detour was applied/reverted in-tree without a standalone surviving commit (temporary, fully undone, folded into this atom's own final state). Full receipts/disasm-grade arithmetic → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-55**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-54 — the C4-27 syscall-hinge stub page REGISTERED with FEX's InvalidationTracker (the `+0x488D0000` NoExecOp fault, C4-53's own new-fault, is GONE); `NtRaiseException`/`ret=3221225477` no longer happens at all -- but `ret=42` NOT achieved either: the process now terminates CLEANLY via a genuine `NtTerminateProcess` call (svc id=44, no crash, no unhandled exception, gates all green) with `ExitStatus=0`, not the expected 42 (`hello8_x86.c`'s `PeMain() { return 42; }`). Root cause of the 0-vs-42 mismatch NOT pinned this atom (new, distinct, unchased frontier -- time-box).

**Fix applied, mirrors C4-46/C4-53's donor-gap pattern exactly, THIRD instance closed**: the C4-27 stub page (`nt_territory_alloc(16)`, `ntboot.c`, holds the `push eax; push -1; mov eax,0x2C; call rel32->BOP` bytes `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready` writes, `ntsvc.c`) crosses NEITHER syscall this floor's notify chain already covers (`NtMapViewOfSection`/C4-46, `NtProtectVirtualMemory`/C4-53) because it is not a guest-visible syscall allocation at all -- it's this floor's OWN internal kernel-C bookkeeping buffer, written during `BTCpuProcessInit` itself (before any dynamic `LdrLoadDll` can run), so `XIntervals` never learned about it, byte-for-byte the same donor-gap class C4-46/53 already closed for the other two syscalls. **Two-part fix, both source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{ntfile.c,ntfile.h,ntsvc.c,ntsvc.h}`**:
1. **Retroactive one-shot registration** (`ntfile.c`, `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`, right where `nt_wow64_ready` first flips true -- the earliest safe moment, guaranteed to run AFTER `BTCpuProcessInit`/the stub-write, per C4-53's own already-proven ordering argument): calls the SAME `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` C4-53 wired (`nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect`, now non-static/exported via `ntfile.h` instead of file-local) with the stub's own `(addr,16)` range, fetched via a new getter `nt_wow64_stub_range` (`ntsvc.c`/`ntsvc.h`) that returns valid only once `nt_wow64_stub_written` is true (always true by this call site, since ready can't flip before `BTCpuProcessInit` completes). No new guest-visible syscall exists to hook for this floor-internal buffer -- this is the correct, minimal analog of a notify call for a page that was never allocated via any syscall in the first place.
2. **General correctness fix, per this atom's own literal mandate** ("wire the executable-allocation notify ... from NtAllocateVirtualMemory ... when Protect has EXEC bits"): `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` (`ntsvc.c`) now captures the guest TEB as its own first statement (same x18-clobber-safety idiom C4-48/53 established) and calls `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_protect(base,size,Protect,teb)` at EVERY real success return whose `Protect` carries an EXEC bit (`Protect & 0xF0`, the exact mask `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification`'s own disasm, C4-53, checks) -- `base`/`size` always the ACTUAL allocation result (IRON), never the caller's hint. Covers the commit-at-base, bookkeeping-only, unconstrained-arena-reserve, and LOW-pool allocation paths alike; harmlessly no-ops (via the shared `nt_wow64_ready` gate) for the BOP trampoline allocation itself and any other EXEC alloc made before FEX's WOW64 runtime is constructed, same protection C4-53 already established. `NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx` needed no separate wiring -- confirmed (not assumed) it unconditionally delegates to `NtAllocateVirtualMemory` (`ntsvc.c`).

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-54_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`, 8684 lines; excerpt `c4-54_pe10_fex_receipt.log`): `CHALICE C4-53: HandleMemoryProtectionNotification base=0x488D0000 size=0x10 prot=0x40` fires (twice -- once per subsequent dynamic x86 image map, kernel32.dll then kernelbase.dll; harmless re-insert both times) immediately confirming the retroactive registration runs; a live `XIntervals` dump (this boot's own pre-existing diagnostic bracket-list print) shows `[0x488D0000,0x488D1000)` present from that point on, through to the end of the transcript. Compared byte-for-byte (diff, address-values elided) against the immediately-prior C4-53 atom's own `c4-53_pe10_fex_receipt_full.log`: BOTH transcripts are IDENTICAL up to line ~8676 (same syscall sequence, same 42 `CHALICE ARENA`/`C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` alignment-fault diagnostics along the way, same counts) -- but where the OLD transcript's line 8677 begins a NEW `CHALICE ARENA`/`C4-30`/`CHALICE EXCEPTION` fault sequence that ends in `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x488D0000` / `pe10 ret=3221225477`, the NEW transcript instead runs straight through to a clean `CHALICE C4-54: NtTerminateProcess ExitStatus=0x0` / `pe10 ret=0`, ZERO additional fault of any kind. **This is a real, structural difference, not noise**: the exact NoExecOp AV this atom targeted is CONFIRMED GONE (the one and only place the two transcripts diverge is precisely where that fault used to fire).

**New frontier, NOT ret=42, NOT chased further this atom (time-box; new-fault law)**: the transcript's tail (last ~60 lines) shows the guest's `ThreadWow64Context` being reset multiple times across the whole run (`<- eip=0x47661000` (exe_entry) / `<- eip=0x486DE0A0` (an internal libwow64fex.dll marker address) early on, then again `<- eip=0x58040000` (BOP) immediately followed by `<- eip=0x47661000` (exe_entry, AGAIN) near the very end) -- the SAME pre-existing FEX-dispatcher-internal-marker-vs-full-NT-SEH architectural gap C4-18/21/24/26/29/30 already named (never fixed, ruled out-of-scope, deferred to Pascal each time) is still firing repeatedly throughout this boot, now simply surviving past the point that used to be fatal. A plausible (NOT verified this atom) explanation for `ExitStatus=0` instead of `42`: one of these mid-flight context resets re-enters PeMain (or re-dispatches into the stub) with a register file that never re-establishes `Eax=42` before the `push eax` executes, OR the observed `id=44 NtTerminateProcess` call is not our stub's own explicit call at all but a DIFFERENT internal FEX/wow64 teardown path (native process-exit default, `ExitStatus=0`) firing once `BTCpuSimulate` returns for some other reason. Distinguishing these needs either a guest-EAX-at-push-time hexdump right before the final `id=44` call (a new, targeted probe, not yet built) or a disasm of `libwow64fex.dll+0x...` at the `0x486DE0A0`/similar reset-marker addresses -- genuinely next-atom work, not attempted here (this atom's own mandate -- closing the notify-registration gap -- is done and gate-proven; the reset-marker gap is the SAME pre-existing, already-repeatedly-deferred design question, not a new one this atom introduced). hello9_x86 swap (the done-line's WriteFile milestone) explicitly gated on `ret=42` by this atom's own task text -- NOT attempted, since that condition was not met.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`scratch/c4-54_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`).

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/{ntfile.c,ntfile.h,ntsvc.c,ntsvc.h}` -- (1) the notify-wiring itself (checkpoint prior to first boot), (2) an added unconditional `NtTerminateProcess ExitStatus=` diagnostic print (checkpoint prior to second boot, used to produce the `ExitStatus=0` receipt above). Full disasm/receipts → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-54**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-53 — PROTECT-NOTIFY CONTRACT COMPLETED: the `+0x68BD0` fault is GONE and `kernelbase_entry` is proven ALIVE at fault time (both goal conditions met simultaneously). Wired `NtProtectVirtualMemory` to call `FEX::Windows::InvalidationTracker::HandleMemoryProtectionNotification` (the REAL remover/inserter for `XIntervals`, real-blob disasm-named this atom, export `BTCpuNotifyMemoryProtect` VMA `0x1800043e8`, body VMA `0x1801696d4` — donor gap symmetric to C4-46's `NtMapViewOfSection` gap: this floor's `NtProtectVirtualMemory` had ZERO call sites into FEX's notify chain before this atom), gated by a new `nt_wow64_ready` flag (set the first time a real guest x86 image other than ntdll.dll is mapped — guarantees `InvalidationTracker` is constructed before any protect-notify call, fixing the REGRESSION the prior lane hit mid-atom: calling into the tracker before `BTCpuProcessInit` runs faults inside its own not-yet-constructed `shared_mutex`). Also fixed a REAL pre-existing correctness bug in the same function: `NtProtectVirtualMemory`'s `*OldProtect` was hardcoded `PAGE_READWRITE` regardless of the region's true protection — this silently broke the guest PE loader's own universal save/restore VirtualProtect idiom around relocation-fixup writes (`VirtualProtect(RW) -> patch -> VirtualProtect(OldProtect)`), because the "restore" call always requested `PAGE_READWRITE` (the wrong value we ourselves handed back as `OldProtect`) instead of the section's true `EXECUTE_READWRITE` — the executable range, correctly REMOVED by the FIRST (genuinely-non-executable) protect call via the new notify wiring, was NEVER RE-INSERTED. Fixed: `OldProtect` now derives from the image registry (inside any registered image → `PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE`; outside → unchanged `PAGE_READWRITE`), matching this floor's existing flat-arena convention (no real per-page W^X). **Named the C4-52 remover, live, exact-address match**: the guest kernelbase.dll loader's own save/restore `VirtualProtect` pair around its `.text` section (`base=0x5E1C1000 size=0x69000`, the SAME range C4-52 was diagnosing) is the mechanism — `HandleMemoryProtectionNotification`'s body (traced end-to-end via disasm): a `NewProtect` WITHOUT any `PAGE_EXECUTE*` bit REMOVES the matching interval from `XIntervals` (binary-search + `Remove`); a `NewProtect` WITH an EXECUTE bit `Insert`s it. Live receipt confirms both halves firing on the exact kernelbase `.text` range: `prot=0x04` (save, REMOVE) then `prot=0x40` (restore, now correctly EXEC → INSERT) — this is what C4-52 observed as an unexplained "later removal" of its own single experimental insert; it is in fact this SAME loader idiom, just that C4-52's world had zero protect-notify call sites at all, so its own vanishing-insert was a coincidental adjacent effect (most likely FEX's own internal SMC/write-detection invalidation on the ensuing reloc-patch WRITE, C4-52's own leading theory, left unrefuted — moot now that the donor-correct contract removes the need to chase it). **Removed the C4-52 experimental direct `HandleImageMap` bypass** (kept through C4-52 as a diagnostic crutch) now that the proper contract holds without it — reboot-confirmed byte-identical outcome with the crutch gone (see receipt below).

**New, later fault reached (named, not chased — new-fault law)**: execution now runs measurably deeper than ever before — `hello8_x86.exe`'s `PeMain` fully executes and its own `ret` lands correctly in this floor's C4-27 syscall-hinge stub (`NtSetInformationThread ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x488D0000`, matching C4-27's own design exactly, reached for the first time). The stub's OWN first instruction then faults: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x488D0000`, second-chance unhandled, `pe10 ret=3221225477`. Root cause (named via the stub's own byte dump, decoded as `push eax; push -1; mov eax,0x2C; call rel32→BOP`, matching `nt_wow64_stub_write_if_ready`, ntsvc.c, exactly): this stub's own code page is allocated via `NtAllocateVirtualMemory(...,PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE)` (ntboot.c) and NEVER subsequently `VirtualProtect`'d — it crosses NEITHER of the two syscalls this floor forwards to FEX's notify chain (`NtMapViewOfSection`/C4-46, `NtProtectVirtualMemory`/C4-53), so it is never registered in `XIntervals` at all. A THIRD instance of the same donor-gap class (`NtAllocateVirtualMemory` itself never calls `BTCpuNotifyMemoryProtect` on this floor either) — out of scope this atom, named per the new-fault law, not fixed.

**Live receipt** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-53_pe10_fex_receipt{,_full}.log`, reboot AFTER the C4-52 crutch removal): zero occurrences of `68BD0` anywhere in the transcript (the old fault is GONE); `CHALICE C4-49 exe_entry(...) enclosed=1 kernelbase_entry(0x5E228BD0) enclosed=1` present repeatedly through to the new fault (kernelbase range ALIVE at fault time) — BOTH goal conditions met simultaneously, with the crutch already removed. New fault as described above, `pe10 ret=3221225477`.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-53_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`, same post-crutch-removal build): `pe8_gate.py` PASS (`pe8 ret=78`) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7.

**Commits**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` — (1) protect-notify wiring + `nt_wow64_ready` guard + `OldProtect` fix (checkpoint prior to this doc commit), (2) removal of the C4-52 experimental direct-call crutch, commit `0f6a7cf`, rebuilt+rebooted to confirm no behavior change. Full disasm/receipts → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-53**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-52 — DECISIVE EXPERIMENT RUN: called `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` DIRECTLY (bypassing `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s own wrapper), via `pe_enter6`, immediately after the normal notify, for kernelbase.dll. Outcome: **no fault** — returned cleanly, and `XIntervals` count went `8→9` IMMEDIATELY after (a real insert, confirmed by the vector's own `end` pointer growing by exactly one `Interval`, no realloc). **Verdict: the WRAPPER is the swallower, not `Insert`/`HandleImageMap` itself — C4-51's "silently-caught C++ exception" sub-theory is REFUTED for this call** (calling the identical function directly does not throw and does insert correctly). But a genuinely NEW, unexpected finding supersedes it: by the time of the LATER self-fault dump (same run, ~1200 transcript lines later), `XIntervals` count is back to `8` — the SAME buffer, `end` pointer shrunk by exactly one `Interval` (16B) — our freshly-inserted kernelbase range was REMOVED again before the guest ever got to execute there. **The true, still-unnamed swallower is this SECOND, later removal — not (per C4-51's theory) an exception inside the insert path.** Not fixed this atom (a naive "call it once at map time" bypass does not survive to fault time); fault UNCHANGED, NOT `ret=42`. Diagnostic-only source change kept (does not regress any gate). Live 2026-07-23. **SUPERSEDED by C4-53 above: the remover is now named (loader's own save/restore VirtualProtect idiom), the wrapper-swallower verdict stands, and the crutch this block describes has been removed.**

**Signature/ABI, disasm-verified against the REAL blob this atom** (`objdump -d kernel-arm/chalice/stock/wow64-chain/libwow64fex.dll`, not the vendored source, not C4-50's quoted symtab RVA alone): `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` sits at VMA `0x180169b10` (C4-50's own quoted COFF-symtab address `0x168b10` is the RAW symtab value; per C4-50's own toolchain note `VMA=ImageBase+0x1000+symtab` → `0x180001000+0x168b10=0x180169b10`, the SAME address, just expressed as a different section-relative RVA, `0x169b10`). Prologue: `mov x20,x0` (`this`), `mov x0,x3; mov x19,x3` (`Address`=4th arg, `x3`), `stp x1,x2,[sp]` (name `{ptr,len}` saved) — confirms the 4-arg shape `HandleImageMap(this=x0, name_ptr=x1, name_len=x2, Address=x3)`, exactly matching the vendored source's own signature (C4-50's own citation), byte-for-byte. **Mutex**: the ONLY lock `HandleImageMap` itself takes is a `shared_mutex` at `this+0x30` (disasm-confirmed: the section-walk loop's lock/unlock call sites both compute `x23=x20(this)+0x30` and pass it to `__shared_mutex_base::lock/unlock`), acquired/released INTERNALLY per qualifying section — the caller (us, or the real wrapper) never needs to pre-acquire it. This is a DIFFERENT mutex from `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s own OUTER global mutex at `0x18034c398` (which guards the whole notify call, unrelated).

**The experiment itself** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`, `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`, fires once, only for `name=="syswow64/kernelbase.dll"`, right after the real notify's own `pe_enter6`-routed call completes): `this=fex_base+0x34c430` (the SAME fixed global C4-49/50/51's own dumper reads and C4-51 proved is the ONE container the decode-time exec-check consults), `fn=fex_base+0x169b10`, `Address=base` (kernelbase's own mapped base, `0x5E1C0000`), a throwaway `"kernelbase.dll"` string_view for `name` (per C4-49's own source read, `Name` is used ONLY for an irrelevant `MonoHacks` string-equality special case in this function — any valid string is safe). Called via `pe_enter6(fn, teb, this, name_ptr, name_len, Address, 0, 0)` — the SAME shim/idiom the real notify path already uses (C4-48), 4 real args in `a0..a3`, `a4/a5` unused padding.

**Live receipt** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-52_pe10_fex_receipt{,_full}.log`): baseline `XIntervals count=8` unchanged through kernel32.dll's own wrapper-routed notify (transcript lines 4299/4337, AFTER kernel32's notify, still count=8 — confirms the wrapper-driven insert never persisted for kernel32 either, consistent with every prior atom). kernelbase.dll's own wrapper-routed notify runs (line 4946, `rc=0x0`, no crash) — no dump immediately after THAT call, but our OWN direct call runs immediately next (line 4947) and its OWN post-call dump (line 4949) shows `count=9` — i.e. exactly ONE net insert since the last-known baseline of 8, meaning **the wrapper's own two calls (`InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` + `ImageTracker::HandleImageMap`) contributed ZERO inserts, and OUR bypass call contributed exactly ONE** — decisive, arithmetic proof the wrapper swallows the insert, not `HandleImageMap`/`Insert` itself. Later, at the self-fault dump (line 6179, ~1230 transcript lines afterward), `count=8` again, SAME buffer address (`begin=0x230111580` unchanged, no reallocation) with `end` shrunk back from `...610` to `...600` — a genuine single-`Interval`-sized removal, not a full vector reset (all 8 baseline ranges still present, unchanged, in the SAME order). Fault, unchanged: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=0x5E228BD0 (syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0)`, `pe10 ret=3221225477` — NOT `ret=42`, no new fault, no regression.

**Sharpened next leg (not chased this atom, time-box)**: something between our post-call dump (line 4949) and the self-fault dump (line 6179) explicitly removes exactly the one newly-inserted kernelbase interval — most likely `FEX::Windows::InvalidationTracker::InvalidateContainingSection(Address,bool)` (the sibling function immediately following `HandleImageMap` in the disasm, VMA `0x180169d74`, never itself disassembled this atom), plausibly triggered by a genuine self-modifying-code (SMC) write-detection hook firing on an ordinary WRITE somewhere inside kernelbase.dll's own mapped image (e.g. TLS/reloc/IAT patching during its own loader-driven init, between our notify-time insert and the guest's own jump into `DllMainCRTStartup`) — this floor has no page-level write-protection trap implemented, so if this IS the mechanism, FEX's own frontend/JIT must be calling it directly as part of its own bookkeeping, not via a hardware fault. Needs (a) disasm of `InvalidateContainingSection` itself to find ALL its callers/triggers, and (b) either an intervening diagnostic dump between our insert and the fault (to narrow the exact removal instant) or a live breakpoint-style hook on that function's entry. NOT a swallowed-exception problem (C4-51's own leading theory, refuted this atom) — a genuine, separate, LATER invalidation is now the sole named blocker.

**Gates, no regression** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/scratch/c4-52_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`): `pe8_gate.py` PASS (`pe8 ret=78`) · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7.

**Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` (`nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view`: C4-52 direct `HandleImageMap` experimental call + immediate post-call `XIntervals` dump, gated to fire once, only for kernelbase.dll, kept as a diagnostic — NOT removed, since no persisting fix was reached this atom), commit `abe9bcb`. Full disasm receipts, byte-level ABI verification → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-52**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-51 — MONAD REFRAME REFUTED (blob disasm proves our dumper watches the ONLY structure the exec-check reads); C4-50's remaining candidates (ii)/(iii) also REFUTED live; sole remaining candidate (silently-caught C++ exception inside the prebuilt blob) named, not fixable without new floor plumbing or a bypass workaround, NOT chased this atom.

**Task's monad check answered directly**: disassembled `FEXCore::Frontend::Decoder::CheckRangeExecutable` (VMA `0x18001ce90`) instruction-by-instruction — on a cache miss it makes exactly ONE virtual call, which resolves (traced through the vtable slot to `WowSyscallHandler::QueryGuestExecutableRange`, a literal 5-instruction tail-call trampoline) straight into `InvalidationTracker::QueryExecutableRange(Address)` at a FIXED global object (`ImageBase+0x34c430`) — the EXACT SAME address our own C4-33/49/50 `kmain.c` dumper has been reading this whole thread. `ImageTracker` (its own, separate global at `ImageBase+0x34c4b0`, a wholly different AOT-code-cache subsystem, RVA `0x165710`, own `HandleImageMap`/`LookupExecutableFileSection`/`OpenCodeMapFile` members) is referenced NOWHERE in this query chain. **We are not gauging the wrong tank — there is only one tank, and it's the one we've been watching.**

**C4-50's three open candidates, this atom's verdict**: (iii) object-identity mismatch — REFUTED (the notify-call site's `this` for `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap`, `adrp 0x18034c000;add x0,x0,#0x430`, is byte-identical to the query chain's own `this`). (ii) EXECUTE-filter excludes every section — REFUTED (extended the C4-50 live diagnostic to walk kernelbase.dll's REAL section table this atom: section[0]/`.text` has `Characteristics=0x60000020` (EXECUTE bit set) and `VSize=0x68F15` (nonempty), and `SectionBase..SectionEnd` (`0x5E1C1000..0x5E229F15`) PROVABLY ENCLOSES the actual fault address `0x5E228BD0`). (i) `RtlImageNtHeader` failing — effectively refuted by disasm (a NULL return would fault the very next instruction, `[x0+0x6]`, producing a NEW, never-observed crash) plus reasoning (this floor's own memory read of the same bytes, via a wholly separate path, agrees with what the header gate implies). **All three checked candidates hold, for a real, live, in-range, correctly-flagged executable section — and `IntervalList::Insert` (fully disassembled, writes directly into the SAME `this+0x0` vector our dumper reads, only skips on a `Base==End` empty-interval case, not applicable here) should have fired — yet this atom's own `pe10 fex` re-run still shows `XIntervals count=8` unchanged, `kernelbase_entry enclosed=0`.**

**New, sole remaining candidate, named not chased**: the prebuilt blob's own `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` wrapper hardcodes `rc=0` UNCONDITIONALLY (C4-50's own finding) and both it and `InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` carry genuine C++ exception-unwind landing pads (disasm-confirmed: `unique_lock::~unique_lock()` + `_Unwind_Resume` cleanup blocks) — meaning an exception thrown partway through the section-walk loop (plausible sources: `IntervalList::Insert`'s own `vector::insert` reallocation, `FEXCore::Allocator::aligned_alloc`, or the RWX-warning branch's `LogMan::Msg`/`fmt` call) would silently unwind straight through the wrapper's own catch-and-swallow, still returning `rc=0` — perfectly explaining a "successful", non-crashing, yet no-op notify call. Not verifiable further without either new host-side single-step/watchpoint plumbing (out of this atom's scope) or accepting a WORKAROUND fix for next atom: have this floor's own `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` hook directly write kernelbase.dll's/kernel32.dll's executable range into `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` itself (the vector layout at `ImageBase+0x34c430+0x0` is now fully, disasm-confirmed understood), bypassing the blob's own (apparently-failing) insert path entirely — floor-satisfiable, a real candidate, NOT attempted this atom (out of this atom's own three-question scope).

**No new fault, no regression**: `pe10 fex` re-run (`scratch/c4-51_pe10_fex_receipt.log`/`_full.log`, 6196 lines) is BYTE-IDENTICAL to every prior atom (`elr=0x26D0C02B0`, `syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, `pe10 ret=3221225477`) — NOT `ret=42`. **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`zoneA/scratch/c4-51_{pe8,pe9,ramfb}_gate.log`). **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c` (per-section EXEC/VirtualSize live diagnostic, extending C4-50's block, no control-flow change), commit `452dc16`. Full byte-level disasm receipts, donor/blob citations → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-51**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-46 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED (KERNELBASE NoExecOp NAMED+FIXED, new deeper fault found+named, not chased): C4-45's `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0` pinned end to end. Disasm (`objdump -d`, `stock/i386-windows/kernelbase.dll`, ImageBase `0x7b000000`) confirms `0x68BD0` is `_DllMainCRTStartup@12`'s OWN entry byte (`66 90 nop; 55 push ebp; ...`), the module's real `AddressOfEntryPoint` from its own PE header — NOT a section-boundary/truncation artifact (`.text` spans RVA `[0x1000,0x69F15)`, entry sits comfortably inside, `0x1345` bytes of margin). LIVE-confirmed (not a stale `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg` read, C4-45's own flagged staleness class): TWO independent fresh sources agree byte-for-byte — the guest's own `NtSetInformationThread`/`NtQueryInformationThread ThreadWow64Context` round-trips report `eip=0x5E228BD0` moments before the raise, AND the `ExceptionRecord`'s own `ExceptionAddress` field matches. The ARM64-side signature (`kmain.c`'s existing C4-31 `SynchronousFaultData` dump: `Signal=SIGSEGV(11) TrapNo=PF(14) si_code=2(SEGV_ACCERR) err_code=0x15(Present|InstrFetch)`) byte-matches FEX's own documented `NoExecOp` self-fault (FEX-MAP.md C4-31/32) — FEX's frontend decoder believes the fetch address is not executable.

**Root cause, donor-traced** (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp`, FEX-MAP.md C4-31/32's own quoted decompile): `InvalidationTracker::XIntervals` gets populated exactly three ways — (1) the tracker's ctor `VirtualQuery` sweep of whatever is ALREADY mapped at `BTCpuProcessInit` time (one-shot, early); (2) `BTCpuProcessInit`'s own two explicit `HandleImageMap` calls, for the main EXE (`PEB.ImageBaseAddress`) and the 32-bit ntdll (`LdrSystemDllInitBlock->ntdll_handle`) — both already correctly wired (C4-29); (3) the `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` export, which on REAL Wine fires from `dlls/wow64/syscall.c`'s own `NtMapViewOfSection` THUNK immediately after the real 32-on-64 syscall returns, for any image mapped AFTER process-start via the guest's own `LdrLoadDll` (kernel32.dll/kernelbase.dll, here). This floor's `NtMapViewOfSection` (`ntfile.c`) is a DIRECT kernel-mode syscall implementation with no wow64.dll thunk layer in between — grepped the whole tree for any `BTCpuNotify*`/`HandleImageMap` call site outside `.md` docs: zero hits before this fix. Every guest x86 DLL loaded via a REAL runtime `NtMapViewOfSection` call (as opposed to this floor's own Phase-0 C-direct exe/ntdll32 maps, which precede paths (1)/(2)) never reaches FEX's `InvalidationTracker` at all — confirmed unambiguous, floor-fixable (a real, already-exported donor function, same resolve-by-name idiom this floor uses everywhere else), unlike C4-31/32/33/34's EARLIER "exe_entry NoExecOp" investigation, which C4-35 later overturned as a stale-memory-read mirage (the real bug there was `pe_find_named_export`'s missing PE32/PE32+ dispatch) — this atom's fault is NOT that mirage (live-double-confirmed above, target is genuinely-mapped code, not a NULL/garbage jump).

**Fix**: `NtMapViewOfSection` (`ntfile.c`) now lazily resolves `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection`'s address (`pe_find_named_export` against whichever mapped image is actually the CPU backend — a harmless no-op probe on every other image) and calls it for every dynamically-mapped GUEST X86 (Machine `0x14C`) image other than `ntdll.dll` (already covered by path (2)'s dedicated, working registration, C4-29) — replicates the real (bypassed) wow64.dll thunk's own post-map notify call, donor-exact per FEX-MAP.md C4-32's quoted signature (`BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection(Unk1, Address, Unk2, Size, AllocType, Prot)`, body only locks a mutex then calls `HandleImageMap(Address)`).

**`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, transcript `/tmp/gaia-pe10-probe.log`, 4151 lines, saved excerpt `zoneA/scratch/c4-46_pe10_fex_notify_fault.log`): the C4-45 kernelbase.dll DllMain NoExecOp fault is **GONE** (kernel32.dll now maps and the notify hook fires without reproducing that specific fault) — but a NEW, DEEPER, qualitatively DIFFERENT fault appears, and it is WORSE in character: a raw, UNRECOVERABLE ARM64 fault (`elr=0x40209E0C`, resolves to NO known image/arena/vm range at all; `lr=ntdll.dll+0x64F84`, a return address inside NATIVE ntdll.dll, consistent with `HandleImageMap`'s own `RtlImageNtHeader`/`GetSectionFilePath` call chain going one level deeper than anything previously exercised on this floor) fires the very first time `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` ever runs (on kernel32.dll's own self-map, before kernelbase.dll even loads this pass). This hits `kmain.c`'s KERNEL fatal path (not the guest-exception-delivery path — `sync_try_guest_exception`'s own `known_guest` gate fails for this address), which has no recovery (`dumps then for(;;) wfe`) — **no `pe10 ret=` line is ever printed**, unlike C4-45's clean `ret=0xC0000005`. Named, NOT chased further this atom (out of scope/time-box; likely candidate: FEX's own `GetSectionFilePath`/mutex-lock internals hitting a native code path this floor has genuinely never exercised before, since `BTCpuNotifyMapViewOfSection` was never called prior to this fix — needs a guarded/nt_setjmp-wrapped native call or a disasm of `0x40209E0C`'s containing function next).

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 — none of these synthetic gates exercise a dynamically-loaded guest x86 DLL the way `pe10` does, so none regress. **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c` (`NtMapViewOfSection`'s new `nt_wow64_maybe_notify_map_view` hook), commit `8310bcd`. Full receipts, donor citations → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-46**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-45 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED (the C4-44-named SECOND `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT` call site, pinned + fixed): **PRIME RIVAL hypothesis (task's own monad check) DISPROVEN FIRST, by direct log evidence**: the task suspected this floor's `NtQuerySystemInformationEx(SystemSupportedProcessorArchitectures)` might be stubbed/ignored, forcing the wine loader's supported-machine gate to reject the guest. Grepped the C4-44 receipt: this floor has answered that exact class since C4-6, and the receipt shows it called TWICE (lines 221/2641) both returning `native=0xAA64 current=0x14C` successfully — not the producer, ruled out by evidence not assumption. **Measure step**: added a one-shot diagnostic to `NtTerminateProcess` (`ntsvc.c`) that, on `ExitStatus==STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT`, dumps the live guest I386_CONTEXT from `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg` (the SAME WOW64_CPURESERVED-block mechanism C4-25's RAISE#1 receipt proved live) — fired cleanly but returned the boot-time-SEEDED Eip/Esp (`0x47661000`/hello8's own entry stub), not fresh data: proof that FEX only refreshes this block via `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` on an actual EXCEPTION, never on an ordinary syscall like `NtTerminateProcess` — an honest negative result, not chased further (kept as a harmless diagnostic, gated one-shot). **Real pin, via static donor-source tracing of the C4-44 receipt's tail** (the task's own alternate measure: "scan the receipt log for the LAST syscalls before NtTerminateProcess"): the log's last distinctive lines before `NtTerminateProcess` (id=44) are `NtOpenFile kernel32.dll dir=C:` → a `__wine_dbg_header` recheck → `NtFsControlFile`(id=57)/`NtQuerySystemTime`(id=90) both `UNIMPLEMENTED`. Traced `NtFsControlFile`'s caller in vendored `wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c`'s `open_dll_file` (`FSCTL_GET_OBJECT_ID`, failure tolerated, red herring — donor code only branches on SUCCESS). The REAL chain: `process_init` (`loader.c:4493-4499`) calls `load_dll(NULL, L"kernel32.dll", 0, &kernel32, FALSE)` — `load_path=NULL` → `find_dll_file` → `search_dll_file(paths=NULL,...)` defaults `paths = default_load_path = peb->ProcessParameters->DllPath.Buffer` (`loader.c:4482`). This floor's OWN `nt_wow64_build_params32` (`ntsvc.c`, C4-37) set `DllPath` to literally `"C:\\"` (ALIASED to the SAME buffer as `CurrentDirectory`, a bare drive root with no `;`-separated segments) — `search_dll_file`'s loop (`loader.c:3236-3238`) therefore tries EXACTLY ONE candidate, `"C:\\kernel32.dll"`, byte-for-byte matching the observed `dir=C:` receipt line. This floor's `NtOpenFile` dirname-redirect (`ntfile.c`, C4-4) only rewrites `dirname=="system32"`(+wow64-active)/`"syswow64"`/`"sysnative"` to the `syswow64/` blob root — `"C:"` matches none, so the lookup falls through to the BARE-name registry entry, which `mkdisk.sh` registers as the NATIVE (AMD64) `kernel32.dll` first — the WRONG architecture for this WOW64 32-bit guest. Donor's own `is_valid_binary` (`loader.c:2515-2529`, fed by this floor's already-correct `NtQuerySection(SectionImageInformation).Machine`) then rejects the mismatched image → `STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED`; `search_dll_file`'s single-candidate loop exhausts with that status; `find_dll_file`'s own conversion (`loader.c:3323`, `if (status == STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED) status = STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT;`) turns it into exactly `0xC000007B`; `process_init`'s kernel32-load check (`loader.c:4493-4499`) then calls `NtTerminateProcess(GetCurrentProcess(), status)` — the pinned call site, end to end, zero remaining guesswork. **Fix (donor-exact default)**: gave `DllPath` its OWN buffer, `"C:\\windows\\system32"` (real Windows/Wine's own default DllPath, `%SystemRoot%\\System32`) instead of aliasing `CurrentDirectory`'s bare `"C:\\"` — this floor's EXISTING `system32`+`nt_wow64_process_active` redirect (already true for every WOW64 boot) now correctly resolves the SAME lookup to `syswow64/kernel32.dll`, zero new redirect logic needed. **`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/c4-45_pe10_fex_receipt_postfix.log`, 62 lines vs the pre-fix 4337): the `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT` exit is GONE — guest now loads `kernel32.dll`+`kernelbase.dll` for real and runs deep inside `syswow64/kernelbase.dll` code before a NEW, later, genuinely different fault: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/kernelbase.dll+0x68BD0`, second-chance-unhandled, clean terminate — `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005` `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`), NOT `0xC000007B`, NOT `ret=42` — a materially further-forward frontier, new bug class (a real fault deep in KERNELBASE, not a loader dependency-resolution gap), named not chased (out of this atom's scope/budget). **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py ret=78` PASS, `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, `ret=0`), `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`zoneA/scratch/c4-45_pe8_gate.log`/`c4-45_pe9_gate.log`/`c4-45_ramfb_gate.log`). **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` (`NtTerminateProcess` C4-45 live-Eip diagnostic + `nt_wow64_build_params32`'s `DllPath` fix), commits `fc97a2a` (diagnostic checkpoint) + `acd2fa8` (DllPath fix). Full receipts, donor citations → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-45**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-43 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED (the C4-42-named `TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer` NULL deref, wired): disasm-confirmed byte-exact (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump`, `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll`, ImageBase `0x7bc00000`) that the C4-42 fault site (`syswow64/ntdll.dll+0xFF05`) is `mov %fs:0x18,%eax; mov 0x1a8(%eax),%eax; mov (%eax),%eax` at `7bc0fef9`-`7bc0ff05` inside `RtlFindActivationContextSectionString`, byte-for-byte matching `wine/include/winternl.h:1087` `_TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer` (offset `0x1a8`). This floor's TEB32 builder (`nt_build_wow64_teb32`, `ntsvc.c`) never wired it (only `ImageBaseAddress`/`ProcessParameters` wired so far, C4-29/C4-37). **Fix (donor-exact, `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c:4010-4013`, the SAME real Wine function that wires the 64-bit TEB's `ActivationContextStackPointer` the earlier C4-28 fix independently mirrors)**: the field points at an EMBEDDED `ACTIVATION_CONTEXT_STACK32` (`winternl.h:687-694`) IN-PLACE inside TEB32 itself at its own `_TEB32` C_ASSERT-anchored offset `0x184` (`winternl.h:1084`) — NOT a separate allocation, exactly as donor code does it (`teb32->ActivationContextStackPointer = PtrToUlong(&teb32->ActivationContextStack)`) — with `FrameListCache` (a `LIST_ENTRY32`, `winternl.h:661-665`) self-linked (`Flink==Blink==&FrameListCache`, donor `virtual.c:4011-4013`) and `ActiveFrame`/`Flags`/`NextCookieSequenceNumber`/`StackId` left `0` (teb32 is `nt_zalloc`'d — only `ActiveFrame` is load-bearing on this fault site's own disasm: `mov (%eax),%eax; test %eax,%eax; je ...`, a null-check on the RESULT taking the fast empty-stack branch — same load-bearing-field-only pattern the C4-28 64-bit fix already established). **`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, transcript `zoneA/scratch/c4-43_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, 4313 lines): the C4-42 `NtRaiseException addr=...+0xFF05` fault is **GONE** (0 occurrences) — guest runs measurably deeper: `NtQueryInformationProcess` (id=25, real syscall 25) is called a SECOND time late in the run (the first, early call is the already-handled `ProcessWow64Information` class 26, logged at line 201; this second call is silent — matching donor `build_main_module`'s (`wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c:2895` region) `NtQueryInformationProcess(GetCurrentProcess(), ProcessImageInformation=37, &info, sizeof(info), NULL)`, a class this floor's `NtQueryInformationProcess` does NOT implement, silently returning `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` without touching the caller's buffer) — followed shortly by `NtTerminateProcess` (id=44, confirmed via `wine-arm64-syscalls.txt`) with **NO intervening `NtRaiseException`/fault trace of any kind** (a clean, deliberate guest-driven exit, not a crash). **NEW, DIFFERENT, FURTHER-FORWARD frontier** (named via donor source-grounded reasoning — no ARM64 disasm needed since this is a clean exit, not a fault): `pe10 ret=3221225595` (`0xC000007B` = `STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT`) — donor `build_main_module`'s local `SECTION_IMAGE_INFORMATION info;` is declared WITHOUT zero-init (unlike the sibling `wow64/syscall.c` copy which uses `= {0}`); this floor's silent `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` for `ProcessImageInformation` leaves `info.ImageCharacteristics` as guest-stack garbage, which `build_main_module` then tests unconditionally (`if (info.ImageCharacteristics & IMAGE_FILE_DLL) { ...; NtTerminateProcess(GetCurrentProcess(), STATUS_INVALID_IMAGE_FORMAT); }`, `loader.c:2900-2906`) — a garbage-bit false-positive genuinely different bug CLASS from anything C4-18 through C4-42 touched (not a TEB/PEB null-deref crash at all — a missing `NtQueryInformationProcess` class feeding an uninitialized-by-donor-design local). **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (`zoneA/scratch/c4-43_pe8_gate.log`/`c4-43_pe9_gate.log`/`c4-43_ramfb_gate.log`). **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` (`nt_build_wow64_teb32`: `TEB32_ACTIVATIONCONTEXTSTACK*`/`TEB32_ACTIVATIONCONTEXTSTACKPOINTER_OFF` defines + wiring), commit `9104f4d`.
