## C4-42 — FIX APPLIED, FRONTIER MOVED (the C4-41-named zero field, found and fixed): named the zero field feeding `alloc_module` end to end. Donor trace (`stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll`, `i686-w64-mingw32-objdump`, cross-checked against vendored `wine/dlls/ntdll/loader.c`): `ntdll32+0x2BB06`/`+0x2EEA6` are `loader_init`'s own INLINED `build_ntdll_module()` (donor `loader.c:2312-2325`) calling `alloc_module(ecx=meminfo.AllocationBase, ...)` → `RtlImageNtHeader(ecx)`, exactly as C4-41 pinned. `build_ntdll_module`'s own call, disasm-confirmed byte-for-byte (`push $0x1c; push $0x7bc4e0a0(=LdrInitializeThunk, symbol-confirmed); push -1; call NtQueryVirtualMemory@24`), is `NtQueryVirtualMemory(-1, LdrInitializeThunk, MemoryBasicInformation, buf, 0x1c, NULL)` — donor source line-for-line match (`loader.c:2361-2363`). **Root cause, one level EARLIER than C4-41's own MBI32 hypothesis**: this exact call, live-grepped across the full C4-41 receipt (61 total `NtQueryVirtualMemory ENTRY` lines), **never once appears with `BaseAddress==LdrInitializeThunk`, in ANY shape** — every one of the 61 is a HOST-side/FEX-internal probe, never this genuine guest WOW64 call. Traced why: the call goes through the REAL, vendored `wow64.dll` (a genuine PE32+ AArch64 binary, `stock/wow64-chain/wow64.dll`, executing as real host-native code) — specifically `wow64_NtQueryVirtualMemory` (`wine/dlls/wow64/virtual.c:568-604`), which gates every `MemoryBasicInformation` query on `(ULONG_PTR)addr > highest_user_address` (`virtual.c:585`) BEFORE ever calling the native `NtQueryVirtualMemory` this floor implements. `highest_user_address` is `wow64.dll`'s own global, populated once by its `process_init` (`wine/dlls/wow64/syscall.c:996-997`): `NtQuerySystemInformation( SystemEmulationBasicInformation, &info, sizeof(info), NULL ); highest_user_address = info.HighestUserAddress;` — **NO status check**. This floor's `NtQuerySystemInformation` (`ntsvc.c`) ONLY accepted `SystemInformationClass==0` (`SystemBasicInformation`), returning `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` for class `62` (`SystemEmulationBasicInformation`) — `info` stayed untouched (host stack garbage/zero), collapsing `highest_user_address` toward `~0`. Every subsequent WOW64 `NtQueryVirtualMemory(MemoryBasicInformation)` call from 32-bit guest code — including `build_ntdll_module`'s own — then fails that `addr > highest_user_address` gate and returns `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` **without ever reaching this floor's native `NtQueryVirtualMemory`** (proving the 0-occurrence grep above). `build_ntdll_module` doesn't check that status either (donor source, quoted above) — `meminfo.AllocationBase` stays at its guest-stack zero-init value, feeding `ecx=0` into `alloc_module` → `RtlImageNtHeader(0)` → the exact NULL `'MZ'`-check AV C4-40/C4-41 pinned. **Fix (donor-exact, `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/virtual.c:7249-7254`: real Wine's own unix `NtQuerySystemInformation` treats `SystemEmulationBasicInformation`(62) as a PURE ALIAS of `SystemBasicInformation`(0), same struct, same values)**: accept class `62` as class `0` in this floor's `NtQuerySystemInformation`; also filled `HighestUserAddress@0x28` (`0x7ffeffff`, `wow64.dll`'s own static pre-`process_init` default, `syscall.c:40` — the exact value a real Windows 32-bit process reports) and `LowestUserAddress@0x20` (`0x10000`, donor-conventional null-page reservation), BOTH previously never written (stayed `0`) even for the already-accepted class `0` — a second, previously-latent gap in the SAME struct-writer this atom's own root-cause trace required touching. **`pe10 fex` re-run receipt** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, transcript `chalice/scratch/c4-42_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, 3745 lines): the C4-40/41 `RtlImageNtHeader(NULL)` fault (`ntdll+0x2B34E`) is **GONE** (0 occurrences, was the terminal fault in C4-40/41) — `build_ntdll_module`'s own `NtQueryVirtualMemory` call now DOES reach this floor's native implementation and answers correctly: `NtQueryVirtualMemory ENTRY base=0x486DE0A0 ... len=0x30` → `branch=image allocbase=0x48690000` (ntdll32's real base, correct) — the fix engaged exactly as diagnosed. **NEW, DIFFERENT, FURTHER-FORWARD frontier** (named, NOT chased, per instruction): `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/ntdll.dll+0xFF05`, disasm-confirmed (`i686-w64-mingw32-objdump`) as `mov %fs:0x18,%eax; mov 0x1a8(%eax),%eax; mov (%eax),%eax` — a NULL-pointer dereference of `TEB32.ActivationContextStackPointer` (offset `0x1a8`, `wine/include/winternl.h:1087`, confirmed byte-exact), inside an activation-context lookup near `RtlFindActivationContextSectionString`. This floor's TEB32 builder never wires `ActivationContextStackPointer` (only `ImageBaseAddress`/`ProcessParameters` wired so far, C4-29/C4-37) — a genuinely NEW, different field/struct from anything C4-42 touched. `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005`, unchanged exit code, but a materially later/different fault site — NOT `ret=42`, no 32-bit→64-bit transition proof this atom). **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7. **Commit**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/ntsvc.c` (`NtQuerySystemInformation` class-62 alias + `HighestUserAddress`/`LowestUserAddress` fields), commit `4789ee0`. Full byte-level receipt, donor citations → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-42**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-41 — HYPOTHESIS TESTED, DISPROVEN (no regression): third-leg resumed C4-40's 7th-fault frontier (`NtQueryVirtualMemory`, `ntfile.c` — MBI32 shaping for WOW64 callers passing `MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION32`/0x1c-byte buffers) + a `NtSetInformationThread` diagnostic (`ntsvc.c`) that traps `eip==ntdll32_base+0x2B34E` (the exact `RtlImageNtHeader` `'MZ'`-check site C4-40 named) and dumps live `Eax`/`Ebp`/guest-stack `[ebp+4]`/`[ebp+8]`. **Both compiled clean, built, gated green** (`pe8` ret=78, `pe9` both backends ret=0, `ramfb` 7/7), **committed** (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c`,`ntsvc.c`, commit `775c513`). **`pe10 fex` re-run** (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, full transcript `chalice/scratch/c4-41_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, 3542 lines): **identical fault, unchanged** — `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005`), same NULL-`'MZ'`-check site. Diagnostic fired once and shows the guest-side truth: `eax(BaseAddress)=0x0`, `caller=syswow64/ntdll.dll+0x2EEA6` (calls `alloc_module`/`RtlImageNtHeader`), `alloc_module`'s own caller `=syswow64/ntdll.dll+0x2BB06`. **Root-cause hypothesis DISPROVEN**: grepped the entire receipt for every `NtQueryVirtualMemory ENTRY` (61 calls) — `MemoryInformationLength` is `0x30`, `0x218`, or `0x8`, **NEVER `0x1c`**; the new `C4-41: NtQueryVirtualMemory(wow64)` diagnostic line **never fires (0 occurrences)** — the `use32` branch is dead code this boot. Conclusion: no guest or wow64-thunk caller ever queries with a 32-bit-sized buffer at this point in boot, so a bad/uninitialized `MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION.AllocationBase` answer from our kernel is NOT how `alloc_module` gets a NULL module base — the real WOW64 architecture marshals `Nt*` calls to native 64-bit shape inside `wow64.dll`'s own thunk before ever reaching our syscall table, so this file's MBI answer was never the culprit. The actual NULL must originate earlier in the guest's own call chain (e.g. a PEB32/LDR_DATA_TABLE_ENTRY32 list-walk field, or `alloc_module`'s caller at `+0x2BB06` passing a bad arg) — needs a fresh trace of THAT function's own inputs, not another `NtQueryVirtualMemory` theory. Fix code is harmless dead weight (kept — it's donor-correct behavior for the day WOW64 32-bit-buffer callers DO show up) but does not move the frontier. **STOPPED HERE per instruction (fault unchanged from C4-40, not new) — next leg needs disasm of `syswow64/ntdll.dll+0x2BB06`/`+0x2EEA6` (not `map-fex-offsets.sh`, which only resolves FEX-side DLL offsets — need direct `objdump` on the stock `syswow64/ntdll.dll` image) to find what feeds `alloc_module` its base arg.**

## C4-40 — FIX APPLIED (the C4-39 named fix, landed): `kmain.c` `sync_try_guest_exception` now branches on `DFSC` for Data Aborts, exactly as C4-39 named it — `if ((ec==0x24||ec==0x25) && (esr&0x3f)==0x21) status=STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT(0x80000002), params=0;` checked AHEAD of the pre-existing generic `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` branch (donor `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/signal_arm64.c` `segv_handler`, re-verified this atom, lines ~1123-1128: the align-fault case sets `rec.ExceptionCode=EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT` and returns immediately, NEVER falling through to the `rec.ExceptionInformation[0/1]=WnR/addr; rec.NumberParameters=2;` lines a few lines further down in the SAME switch case — so `params=0`/no `ExceptionInformation` is the donor-exact shape, not a simplification). FEX's consumer, `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` (`Module.cpp:926`, re-checked this atom), only reads `Exception->ExceptionCode` for this branch (`== EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT`) — no other record field gates it, confirming no further record-shape work was needed.

**`pe10 fex` receipt (`scratch/pe10_probe.py fex`, transcript `chalice/scratch/c4-40_pe10_fex_receipt.log`, this pass) — THE FIX WORKS, repeatedly, exactly as predicted**: SIX separate Data-Abort/`DFSC=0x21` alignment faults fire during this boot (all inside the same JIT reserve-commit-arena code-cache region, all on the ntdll32 `_init_user_process_params`/`memcpy` Environment-block copy loop C4-38 first named — same `elr=0x2968DD634`/`far=0x5A060A8E` pair from C4-38/39's own receipt appears again, byte-identical, as the FIRST of the six):
```
esr=0x96000061 elr=0x2968DD634 far=0x5A060A8E status=0x80000002   (C4-38/39's own named fault -- now MISALIGNMENT)
esr=0x96000021 elr=0x2968E2514 far=0x5A060A4E status=0x80000002   (read, next word)
esr=0x96000061 elr=0x2968E2524 far=0x488CB716 status=0x80000002   (write)
esr=0x96000021 elr=0x2968DD624 far=0x486F9851 status=0x80000002   (read)
esr=0x96000061 elr=0x2968ECE6C far=0x488CBA83 status=0x80000002   (write)
esr=0x96000061 elr=0x2968F88A0 far=0x488CBA81 status=0x80000002   (write)
```
All six are delivered with `status=STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT` and **NONE produce a guest-visible `NtRaiseException`** (grepped the full transcript: zero `NtRaiseException` lines for any of the six — the only two `NtRaiseException` lines in the entire boot are the FINAL, seventh, different fault below) — this is the exact, unambiguous signature of FEX's own `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` consuming the exception internally (`HandleUnalignedAccess` backpatches the JIT buffer, returns `true`, `NtContinue`s) rather than forwarding it, precisely the self-heal mechanism this atom's fix contract targeted. Between each pair, real forward progress happens (more `NtOpenFile`/syscall activity in between) — **the memcpy genuinely completes** (six unaligned `ldar`/`stlr` sites backpatched across what is evidently the whole Environment-block copy loop, not just the one instruction originally caught), and execution reaches **real, live ntdll32 loader code for the first time in this project's history**: `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context, eip=0x486DE0A0)` — the SAME real `LdrInitializeThunk` RVA C4-36 first resolved — followed by genuine forward execution inside it (`NtQueryInformationThread` reads back the same live `eip`, no more stale-boot-seed artifacts at this point in the sequence).

**New frontier, NAMED not chased (per this atom's own instruction)**: a SEVENTH, qualitatively DIFFERENT fault terminates the boot — `esr=0x96000006` (`ec=0x25` Data Abort, `DFSC=0x06`=Translation-fault-level-2, per ARM ARM D17.2.28 — genuinely NOT an alignment fault, our new branch correctly does NOT match it, falls through to the pre-existing `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` branch unchanged), `far=0x0` — a real NULL-pointer dereference, `elr=0x2968EBC24` (same JIT arena region as the six alignment faults, but a different site within it). Disassembled the raw ARM64 words at this `elr` (`aarch64-elf-objdump -D -b binary -m aarch64`, `chalice/scratch/c4-40_pe10_fex_receipt.log` line 3492 window):
```
mov   w20, #0x5a4d      ; 'MZ', IMAGE_DOS_SIGNATURE
ldarh w21, [x4]         ; <- ELR, faults: x4==far==0x0
lsl   w0, w21, #16
cmp   w0, w20, lsl #16
b.ne  ...
```
**Named**: this is a JIT-compiled guest x86 IMAGE_DOS_HEADER.e_magic (`'MZ'`) validity check, executed against a NULL module base — the guest, now genuinely running inside `LdrInitializeThunk`'s own loader-init code (first time ever reached, per the real `eip=0x486DE0A0` progress immediately preceding it), validates some module pointer that is 0 before ever getting to read it. This is a DIFFERENT bug class from anything C4-37/38/39/40 touched (not TSO/alignment at all — a genuine translation fault on a null pointer, the same DFSC family C4-28/29's `RtlImageNtHeader(NULL)` gap was, but a NEW call site: `x4`, not `PEB32.ProcessParameters`, and reached from deep inside real loader code rather than `_init_user_process_params`). Exact x86 source call site NOT identified this atom (would need FEX's own JIT block/source-map introspection to resolve a code-cache address back to an ntdll32 RVA, out of this atom's scope) — **STOPPING HERE per instruction**, no further chase. The resulting AV IS delivered correctly end-to-end this time (unlike every prior architectural gap C4-18-31 named): `NtSetInformationThread(eip=0x486BB34E)` → `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/ntdll.dll+0x2B34E` → `FirstChance=FALSE (second-chance, unhandled) -- terminating`, `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005`), **NOT `ret=42`** (no 32-bit->64-bit transition proof this atom — the guest's own SEH/unhandled-exception path ran and re-raised, itself now real guest code, not a kernel/FEX internal marker).

**Gates, no regression (pre-boot, before this pe10 receipt)**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (4 corners + 3 bars). **Commit**: source-only, `kmain.c` (one new `if` branch + two `#define`s, `sync_try_guest_exception`). Full receipt → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-40**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-39 — MEASUREMENT+RECON ATOM, no fix. Road 1 (cheap): tried `FEX_TSOENABLED=0` (env var, appended to the SAME double-NUL guest-Environment block C4-14 already uses for `FEX_SILENTLOG=0` — `ntboot.c` `nt_boot_process`, `PARAMS_ENVIRONMENT_OFF`) to disable FEXCore's x86-TSO-on-ARM64 emulation outright, the exact codegen C4-38 named as producer. **Config key confirmed**: `FEX_CONFIG_OPT(TSOEnabled, TSOENABLED)` (`FEX/Source/Windows/Common/TSOHandlerConfig.h:39`, also `Context.h:248`) reads env var `FEX_<enum>` i.e. `FEX_TSOENABLED` (`FEX/Source/Common/Config.cpp` `EnvLoader::Load`, `#define OPT_BASE ... GetVar(EnvMap, "FEX_" #enum)`). **Delivery path confirmed**: `BTCpuProcessInit` (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:514`) calls `FEX::Config::LoadConfig(ExecutableName, _environ, ...)` → `CreateEnvironmentLayer(envp)` → `EnvLoader(_environ)`; `_environ` is the MinGW/UCRT CRT global for the NATIVE 64-bit process, populated (per this floor's own C4-14 comment, `ntboot.c:190-207`) by CRT startup walking `NtCurrentTeb()->Peb->ProcessParameters->Environment` (the SAME native 64-bit `PARAMS_ENVIRONMENT_OFF` field, NOT the 32-bit guest's `PEB32.ProcessParameters.Environment` C4-37 wired — FEX itself is host-side ARM64 code, its config is a HOST/native env concern). `IsMemcpyAtomicTSOEnabled() = Config.TSOEnabled && Config.MemcpySetTSOEnabled` (`Context.h:364`) — setting `TSOEnabled=0` alone is sufficient (AND with false), no need to also clear `MEMCPYSETTSOENABLED`. **Receipt, `pe10 fex` with `FEX_TSOENABLED=0` live in the env block**: FAULT UNCHANGED byte-for-byte — same `esr=0x96000061`/`ec=0x25`/`dfsc=0x21`, same `elr=0x2968DD634`, same `far=0x5A060A8E`, `ret=3221225477` (`0xC0000005`, STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION), NOT `ret=42`. Objdump'd the live ELR-window bytes this run vs C4-38's disasm: **identical instruction stream** (`ldar w11,[x20]` / `stlr w11,[x20]`, same host addresses) — the env var had ZERO effect on JIT codegen. Corroborating evidence in the SAME transcript: `FEX_SILENTLOG=0` (present in the identical env block, proven-dead per C4-27's own still-open LEAD 1) STILL produced zero `LogMan`-prefixed narration lines this run either — TWO independent `FEX_*` config keys, in the same block, both silently ignored → **env-var delivery to FEXCore::Config is broken somewhere in this floor's own native-process env plumbing** (not a FEX limitation; `TSOEnabled` genuinely gates the codegen per source, `Context.h:362-364`/`JIT/MemoryOps.cpp:1854`), a SEPARATE, still-unnamed gap from anything C4-38 touched. **Not chased further this atom** (out of scope; candidate future atom: instrument whether `_environ` itself is even non-null/non-empty at `BTCpuProcessInit` time — e.g. temporary probe print inside the vendored `FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:514` call site, or trace `GetEnvironmentStringsW`/`WideCharToMultiByte` inside stock kernelbase.dll, which is what actually backs `_environ` for a MinGW-CRT DLL loaded via LoadLibrary rather than a `mainCRTStartup` EXE).

**Road 2 (durable), the real gate, found by recon — NOT a FEX limitation at all**: FEXCore's OWN self-healing IS reachable, but our floor never triggers it, because of a DIFFERENT, upstream bug: this floor's synchronous-abort classifier (`kmain.c` `sync_try_guest_exception`, line ~483) maps **every** Data Abort (`ec==0x24 || ec==0x25`) unconditionally to `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` (`0xC0000005`) regardless of `DFSC` — there is NO branch for `DFSC=0x21`/alignment. **Donor-grounded exact contract** (`wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/signal_arm64.c` `segv_handler`, lines ~1114-1130 — real Wine's own ARM64 SIGBUS/SIGSEGV handler, the reference this whole floor's exception delivery is modeled on): for a Data Abort (`ESR_ELx_EC_DABT_LOW`/`_CUR`), IF `ESR_ELx_ISS_DFSC(esr) == ESR_ELx_ISS_DFSC_ALIGN_FAULT` (`0x21`, byte-identical to our own C4-38 decode), Wine sets `rec.ExceptionCode = EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT` (`STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT`, `0x80000002` — `wine/include/ntstatus.h:223`/`winnt.h:629`, a WARNING-severity code, NOT `0xC0000005`) and routes DIRECTLY to `setup_raise_exception`, SKIPPING `virtual_handle_fault` (the AV/guard-page/retry machinery) entirely — only non-alignment data aborts fall through to the `EXCEPTION_READ_FAULT`/`EXCEPTION_WRITE_FAULT` classification this floor's `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` branch already mirrors. On the FEX side, this exact code is the explicit gate: `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:926`) checks `Exception->ExceptionCode == EXCEPTION_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT && Context::HandleUnalignedAccess(TLS, Context)` — `HandleUnalignedAccess` (→ `FEXCore::ArchHelpers::Arm64::HandleUnalignedAccess`, `Arm64.cpp:1924`) decodes the FAULTING `ldar`/`stlr` instruction bytes AT `Context->Pc` directly from the passed-in `CONTEXT` register file, emulates the single non-atomic access using those same registers, and BACKPATCHES the JIT code buffer (self-modifying code) to a plain `ldr`/`str`+`dmb` sequence so the SAME code path never faults again — this is the literal mechanism C4-38 already inferred exists on "a normal host". Feeding it `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` instead means that whole branch is skipped (the exception falls through the AV-shaped checks above it — `CallRetStack`/`OvercommitTracker`/suspend/`JITGuardPage`/`InvalidationTracker`, none of which apply to a genuine alignment fault — none fire, `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` reconstructs a WOW64_CONTEXT and forwards a REAL guest exception instead) — this is EXACTLY the observed transcript, line for line: `CHALICE: NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005` → `FirstChance=FALSE (second-chance, unhandled) -- terminating`. **The Road-2 contract our floor's exception delivery must satisfy**: `sync_try_guest_exception` needs a THIRD status branch, ahead of/instead of the existing `ec==0x24||ec==0x25` one — `if ((ec==0x24||ec==0x25) && dfsc==0x21) status = STATUS_DATATYPE_MISALIGNMENT (0x80000002);` (checked BEFORE the generic AV branch, mirroring Wine's own `switch`/early-return order) — everything else in the existing delivery pipeline (dispatcher resolution, `guest_exception_stack`, `NtRaiseException`-equivalent forwarding into `BTCpuResetToConsistentState`) is ALREADY the right shape and needs no other change; this is a genuinely small, well-scoped, donor-grounded fix, deferred to next atom per this atom's own recon-only instruction. **Tradeoff for Pascal to rule on**: `pe10`'s guest workload is single-threaded (no concurrent/MP guest code) — TSO-correctness (the entire REASON FEXCore emits `ldar`/`stlr` here) has NO observable effect on a single-threaded guest; Road 1 (fixing env delivery so `FEX_TSOENABLED=0` actually lands, letting FEXCore skip the atomic emulation at CODEGEN time) may be the cheaper AND permanent seam choice vs. Road 2 (teaching this floor's exception path FEX's full alignment-fault-recovery contract, needed indefinitely for any future MP-guest workload) — but Road 1's OWN blocker (env-var delivery, above) is presently unnamed/unfixed, while Road 2's fix is small, named, and immediately actionable. Pascal rules which lane goes first.

**Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7 (4 corners + 3 bars). **Commit**: source-only, `ntboot.c` (`FEX_TSOENABLED=0` appended to the existing double-NUL env block, Road 1 measurement instrumentation only — no control-flow change, no fix landed either road). Full byte-level receipt, donor citations, exact contract → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-39**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-38 — MEASUREMENT ATOM, no fix: MONAD's C4-37 correction confirmed by receipt — `esr=0x96000061` decodes to `EC=0x25`(Data Abort, lower EL)/`ISS=0x61`/**`DFSC=0x21`=ALIGNMENT FAULT** (ARM ARM D17.2.28: `0b100001`; translation faults are `0b0001xx`=`0x04-0x07`), NOT a translation fault as C4-37 framed it. **T1 (wrong PTE attrs) REFUTED by live PTE dump**: added a one-shot TTBR0_EL1 walk + MAIR_EL1 decode to `sync_try_arena_demand_commit` (`kmain.c`, diagnostic-only, no behavior change) — at the fault, `l1desc=0x40000705` (`attrindx=1`, `mair_byte=0xFF`=Normal WBWA, matches `mmu_on()`'s own 1GB RAM block descriptors exactly — `far=0x5A060A8E` falls inside `l1_table[1]`, the very first RAM block, Normal cacheable ISH, NOT Device). `sctlr_el1=0xC5183D`, **A-bit(alignment-check-enable)=0** — the SCTLR sub-theory of T2 is also refuted. **T2 CONFIRMED, exact mechanism found**: disassembled the real ARM64 host instruction at `elr=0x2968DD634` (FEXCore's own already-logged "ELR WINDOW" bytes, `aarch64-elf-objdump -b binary -m aarch64`) — it is **`stlr w11, [x20]`**, a Store-Release (ordered/atomic-release) instruction, immediately preceded by a matching `ldar w11,[x20]` two blocks up (same window) — **LDAR/STLR perform an alignment check unconditionally, regardless of `SCTLR_ELx.A`** (ARM ARM: ordered/exclusive accesses always alignment-check). `x20 = far = 0x5A060A8E` (`mod 4 == 2`, genuinely misaligned for the 4-byte access). **Producer named**: FEXCore's own JIT codegen — its x86-TSO-on-ARM64 memory-ordering emulation lowers this guest `movl %edi,(%ebx,%ecx)` (a PLAIN, non-atomic x86 store inside ntdll32's `_init_user_process_params`→`memcpy` Environment-block copy) into an ARM64 `ldar`/`stlr` pair instead of a plain `ldr`/`str`, to preserve x86's stronger memory ordering — a real, valid, Normal-cacheable page (proven above) still faults because `stlr` mandates alignment FEX's own emitted code never checked the guest pointer for. **This is a FEXCore-internal JIT-codegen gap** (same category as C4-21's `EntryContext`/C4-31's `InvalidationTracker` — outside floor/kernel/mapping code, no page-table/attr bug exists at all here), **not a one-liner floor-side attr fix** — per this atom's own instructions, STOPPED AT VERDICT, no fix applied. **Gates, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS 7/7. **Commit**: source-only, `kmain.c` (diagnostic-only ESR/PTE/SCTLR dump added to `sync_try_arena_demand_commit`, no control-flow change). Full byte-level receipt, ARM64 disasm → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-38**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-37 — FIX APPLIED: `PEB32.ProcessParameters` wired (the C4-36 named gap). New `nt_wow64_build_params32` (ntsvc.c) allocates+populates a real `RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS32` (wine `include/winternl.h:733-768`, `UNICODE_STRING32` :673-678, `CURDIR32` :705-708 — field offsets cross-checked against a live disasm of `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll`'s `_init_user_process_params`, RVA `0x1E8F0-0x1EA30`: the fault site `movl 0x290(%ebx),%esi` is `EnvironmentSize`, byte-exact; `movl 0x29c(%ebx),%eax`/`movl %eax,0x29c(%esi)` in the same function's field-copy loop independently confirms `ProcessGroupId@0x29c`, both matching the donor struct's own field order exactly) and wires it into `PEB32+0x10` (`PEB32_PROCESSPARAMETERS_OFF`, winternl.h:849) via a new `nt_wow64_get_peb32()` getter (`wow64_wire.peb32`, C4-3/C4-29's own struct, is block-scoped where `nt_build_wow64_teb32` runs; the params wiring needs to happen later, once the wide `ImagePathName`/`CommandLine` buffers exist — reused the existing C4-6 `nt_current_wow64_peb32` stash instead of widening scope). `CurrentDirectory`/`DllPath` get a real `"C:\\"` (C4-28 scar law: some ntdll helpers deref `DosPath.Buffer` regardless of `Length`); `ImagePathName`/`CommandLine` reuse the SAME wide buffers the 64-bit `PEB.ProcessParameters` already uses (one address space, WOW64 FEX runs guest+host together — C4-35); `WindowTitle`/`Desktop`/`ShellInfo`/`RuntimeInfo`/`Environment` left zeroed (valid empty-string state, never dereferenced per the disasm — `_alloc_process_params`'s own `RtlCreateProcessParametersEx`-shaped fastcall only copies these UNICODE_STRING32s by value when `Length!=0`).

- **live-caught self-bug, fixed same pass**: the first version of this fix stored `"C:\\"` in a `static const unsigned short curdir_w[3]` — a KERNEL `.rodata` global, not guest-visible heap memory. `pe10 fex` immediately proved this wrong: the fault moved forward exactly as hoped (C4-36's `RVA 0x1E914` site was gone), but a NEW fault appeared inside `memcpy` (RVA `0x4E475`, disasm-confirmed, `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll`) reading `far=0x402204F6` — `llvm-nm gaia-os.elf` resolved that address to the EXACT symbol `nt_wow64_build_params32.curdir_w` (byte-for-byte), confirming: kernel `.rodata` is EL1-only, never mapped into whatever execution context FEX's JIT/guest runs under (unlike the `mem.c` `HEAP_BASE 0x41000000` heap range, already proven guest-accessible by every other wide buffer in this codebase). Fixed by `nt_zalloc`-backing `curdir_w` like every other guest-visible buffer; rebuilt, regated, rebooted.
- **result, post-fixup**: the C4-36 fault site (`RVA 0x1E914`, `PEB32->ProcessParameters` NULL deref) is GONE — confirmed no longer present in the transcript. New frontier, FURTHER FORWARD again: `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 addr=syswow64/ntdll.dll+0x4E478` (3 bytes past the now-fixed memcpy read, `elr=...D634` vs the read's `...D624`) — `llvm-objdump` of that RVA shows `memcpy`'s own destination-write instruction (`89 3c 0b movl %edi,(%ebx,%ecx)`, immediately after the (now-working) source read at `0x4E475`). `wnr=1` (write fault); `far=0x5A060A8E`; this floor's own region-tracker resolves it to `vm-region base=0x5A060000 size=0x10000` — a region THIS SAME transcript shows going through a real 2-step `NtAllocateVirtualMemory(MEM_RESERVE)`→`NtAllocateVirtualMemory(MEM_COMMIT)` sequence (both `AVM OUT`/`AVM IN` lines present, both `status=0`) — i.e. this floor's own metadata believes the page is committed, yet the actual translation fault (`ESR` `DFSC=0x21`, translation-fault-level-1, not a permission fault) says no PTE covers it for this write. **NAMED, NOT chased (out of this atom's scope — the field this atom owns, `PEB32.ProcessParameters` itself, is proven correctly wired; this is a NEW, deeper gap in the vm-region/NtAllocateVirtualMemory-committed-memory-vs-actual-page-table-mapping pipeline, not anything this atom's own fields touch)**: candidate next atom is auditing whether `nt_territory_alloc`'s advertised 0.75GB identity-mapped range (`0x45000000→0x7ff00000`, HANDOFF's own "What's implemented" section) is ACTUALLY covered by real L1/L2 block descriptors end-to-end at boot, or whether some sub-ranges (this one, `0x5A060000`) are left unmapped/require a demand-commit path this floor's ARENA-only demand-pager (`refused not-arena` — the arena mechanism explicitly does NOT claim this address) doesn't yet cover.
- **Gates, no regression (post-fixup)**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` PASS · `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` PASS (7/7, `scratch/ramfb_gate.py`).
- **Commits**: `9f872fa` (PEB32.ProcessParameters wiring, `ntsvc.c`/`ntsvc.h`/`ntboot.c`), `5082e8f` (fixup: heap-backed `curdir_w`, `ntsvc.c`). Both source-only. Full byte-level receipt, donor citations → FEX-MAP.md § **C4-37**. Live 2026-07-23.

## C4-35 — MEASUREMENT ATOM, no fix: the producer of GuestCode/InstStream=0x0 is NAMED. `GenerateIR`'s `GuestCode` (Core.cpp:532-533) is a bare `reinterpret_cast<uint8_t*>(GuestRIP)` — no translation exists to fail. GuestRIP itself was 0 because FEXCore's `Thread->CurrentFrame->State.rip` was set to 0 by a REAL, live-caught `NtSetInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context, eip=0)` call — donor `wine/dlls/wow64/syscall.c:1069-1097 thread_init()`'s own `ctx.Eip = pLdrSystemDllInitBlock->pLdrInitializeThunk` — and `pLdrInitializeThunk` is 0 because THIS floor's own `pe_find_named_export` (pemod.c:320) misreads `NumberOfRvaAndSizes`/`DataDirectory[]` using hardcoded PE32+ (64-bit) optional-header offsets on the PE32 (32-bit) `syswow64/ntdll.dll` image, reading `num_dirs=0` and bailing via `if (num_dirs<1) return -3` before ever finding the export. The C4-25/26/30/33/34 "GUEST CTX Eip=exe_entry" receipt that anchored 5 prior legs is a STALE-MEMORY READ ARTIFACT: it reads the never-updated boot-seed bytes in `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg`, not FEXCore's live RIP (`NtSetInformationThread`'s floor implementation writes a separate stash, never that buffer). Zero source changes this pass (existing instrumentation reused, one standalone header-byte check); `pe10 ret=0xC0000005` unchanged. Live 2026-07-23.

### (a) Call chain, source-quoted (donor, vendored locally)

1. **`FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp:530-538`** (`ContextImpl::GenerateIR`):
   ```cpp
   const uint8_t* GuestCode {};
   GuestCode = reinterpret_cast<const uint8_t*>(GuestRIP);
   ...
   Thread->FrontendDecoder->DecodeInstructionsAtEntry(Thread, GuestCode, GuestRIP, MaxInst);
   ```
   `GuestCode` is a **literal cast of `GuestRIP` to a pointer** — WOW64 FEX runs guest and host in the SAME address space, so `GuestRIP` values ARE host-valid pointers directly. There is no lookup/translation step here that can independently "return null" — R1's framing in C4-34 ("host-side translation returned null") is corrected: **there is no translation; `GuestCode==0` iff `GuestRIP==0`.**

2. **`GuestRIP`'s source, entry into the JIT**: `ContextImpl::CompileBlock(Frame, GuestRIP, MaxInst)` (Core.cpp:820) is reached from `Dispatcher::EmitDispatcher`'s JIT loop (`Dispatcher.cpp:100`): `ldr(RipReg, STATE_PTR(CpuStateFrame, State.rip));` — i.e. **`GuestRIP == Thread->CurrentFrame->State.rip`**, read directly out of FEXCore's own CPU-state struct at the top of every dispatch iteration (including the very first, driven by `BTCpuSimulateImpl`→`CTX->ExecuteThread`).

3. **`State.rip`'s last writer before the fault**: `Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:202` (`Context::LoadStateFromWowContext`): `State.rip = Context->Eip;` — called from `BTCpuSetContext` (`Module.cpp:~705-722`) after it re-reads the just-set context back via `RtlWow64GetThreadContext`→**`NtQueryInformationThread(ThreadWow64Context)`** (a REAL NT syscall, this floor's own).

4. **`BTCpuSetContext`'s caller, and what `Context->Eip` it wrote**: donor `wine/dlls/wow64/syscall.c:1069-1097 thread_init()`:
   ```c
   pBTCpuGetContext(GetCurrentThread(), GetCurrentProcess(), NULL, &ctx);   // ctx.Eip = exe_entry (correct, boot-seeded)
   ctx_ptr = (I386_CONTEXT *)ULongToPtr(ctx.Esp) - 1;  *ctx_ptr = ctx;      // spill ctx to guest stack
   stack = (ULONG *)ctx_ptr;
   *(--stack)=0; *(--stack)=0; *(--stack)=0;
   *(--stack) = PtrToUlong(ctx_ptr);  *(--stack) = 0xdeadbabe;
   ctx.Esp = PtrToUlong(stack);
   ctx.Eip = pLdrSystemDllInitBlock->pLdrInitializeThunk;                  // <-- OVERWRITES Eip
   pBTCpuSetContext(GetCurrentThread(), GetCurrentProcess(), NULL, &ctx);
   ```
   Real WOW64 threads start at **ntdll32's `LdrInitializeThunk`**, not the exe's own entry point (LdrInitializeThunk does loader work first, then calls the exe). `pLdrSystemDllInitBlock->pLdrInitializeThunk` at offset `0x18` of the `SYSTEM_DLL_INIT_BLOCK` struct (wine `include/winternl.h:4422-4438`) is this floor's own `nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32` write target (`kernel-arm/zoneA/ntfile.c:1053-1094`, C4-26/29).

5. **Why that field is 0**: `nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32` calls `pe_find_named_export(base32, size32, "LdrInitializeThunk", &thunk)` (`ntfile.c:1073`). `pe_find_named_export` (`pemod.c:320-349`) **unconditionally** uses the PE32+ (64-bit) optional-header offsets:
   ```c
   const u8 *opt = coff + COFF_HDR_SIZE;
   u32b num_dirs = rd32(opt + OPT_NUMBER_OF_DIRS);   // 0x6C -- PE32+ offset, hardcoded
   const u8 *dirs = opt + OPT_DATA_DIRS;              // 0x70 -- PE32+ offset, hardcoded
   if (num_dirs < 1) return -3;
   ```
   with **no PE32-vs-PE32+ magic check** — unlike `PeMapImage` a few dozen lines earlier in the SAME FILE (`pemod.c:149-164`), which already correctly branches `is_pe32` between `OPT_NUMBER_OF_DIRS32=0x5C`/`OPT_DATA_DIRS32=0x60` (PE32) and `OPT_NUMBER_OF_DIRS=0x6C`/`OPT_DATA_DIRS=0x70` (PE32+). `syswow64/ntdll.dll` is genuinely PE32 (`OptionalHeader.Magic=0x10B`, Machine `0x14C`).

### (b) Named step that produced 0 + live receipt values

**Producer: `pe_find_named_export`'s missing PE32/PE32+ dispatch, misreading `NumberOfRvaAndSizes` as 0 for a 32-bit image, causing an early `return -3` before the export table is ever consulted.**

Standalone header-byte check against the vendored `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll` (no floor/QEMU involved, pure arithmetic proof):
```
e_lfanew=0x78 magic=0x10b                                    (confirms PE32, not PE32+)
PE32 correct:   num_dirs=16  exp_rva=0x7a8e8  exp_size=0xacbb  (real export dir, LdrInitializeThunk IS present, RVA 0x4e0a0 per llvm-readobj --coff-exports)
BUGGY code reads: num_dirs=0  exp_rva=0x94000  exp_size=0x3c4  (opt+0x6C/opt+0x70 -- wrong entries entirely)
```
`num_dirs=0` on the buggy read → `pe_find_named_export` hits `if (num_dirs<1) return -3` immediately — `*out_addr` (`thunk`) is never written, stays at its caller-side initializer `0`.

Live `pe10 fex` boot receipt (this pass, zero code changes, existing `nt_dispatch_log_enabled` traces):
```
CHALICE C4-26: LdrSystemDllInitBlock populated thunk=0x0 rtlstart=0x0 kuxd=0x0 kuad=0x0 kucd=0x0 ntdll32_base=0x48690000 owninit=0x0
...
CHALICE: NtQueryInformationThread ThreadWow64Context -> eip=0x47661000 esp=0x488CC000     (Step A: BTCpuGetContext's LockJITContext flush -- CORRECT, exe_entry)
CHALICE: NtSetInformationThread   ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x47661000 esp=0x488CC000     (BTCpuGetContext's own FlushThreadStateContext echo -- still correct)
CHALICE: NtQueryInformationThread ThreadWow64Context -> eip=0x47661000 esp=0x488CC000
CHALICE: NtSetInformationThread   ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x47661000 esp=0x488CC000     (BTCpuSetContext's own FlushThreadStateContext echo)
CHALICE: NtSetInformationThread   ThreadWow64Context <- eip=0x0        esp=0x488CBD20     (<-- thread_init's SECOND BTCpuSetContext: ctx.Eip=pLdrInitializeThunk=0)
CHALICE: NtQueryInformationThread ThreadWow64Context -> eip=0x0        esp=0x488CBD20
...
CHALICE C4-34 ThreadState=0x270001000 Decoder=0x2300B0080 ... InstStream=0x0 InstructionSize=0x0
 CHALICE C4-34 ByteAddress(queried)=0x0 enclosed=0x0 exe_entry(0x47661000) match=0x0
```
Esp arithmetic confirms this is exactly `thread_init`'s own stack-spill: `0x488CC000 − 0x488CBD20 = 0x2E0 = sizeof(I386_CONTEXT)(0x2CC) + 5×4(pushes)(0x14)` — byte-exact match to the donor code quoted in (a)/4, not a coincidence.

**The C4-25/26/30/33/34 "GUEST CTX Eip=exe_entry" receipt is a measurement artifact**: `ntsvc.c`'s `NtRaiseException` RAISE#1 dump reads `nt_wow64_cpureserved_dbg` directly (raw guest memory, boot-seeded once by `nt_wow64_cpureserved_set_context` and NEVER written again — confirmed by reading `NtSetInformationThread`'s own body, `ntsvc.c:1241-1263`: it only updates the separate `nt_wow64_ctx_eip`/`nt_wow64_ctx_esp` static-global stash used to answer `NtQueryInformationThread`, never the `cpureserved` memory buffer). So that dump forever reads the stale boot-seed (`exe_entry`), regardless of what FEXCore's live internal `State.rip` actually holds. The REAL live value (0) is only visible via the `NtSetInformationThread`/`NtQueryInformationThread` trace lines above and the C4-33/34 Decoder dump — this reconciles the C4-33/34 "paradox" completely: `XIntervals` legitimately encloses `exe_entry` (C4-33 correct) because `exe_entry` really is a valid, mapped, executable image range — but the frontend was never asked to decode `exe_entry` at all. It was asked to decode RIP `0x0` (`LdrInitializeThunk`'s unresolved address), a wholly different, never-reached code path.

### (c) Smallest-fix candidate (named, NOT applied — measurement atom only)

**`pemod.c`'s `pe_find_named_export`**: add the identical `is_pe32` magic-check + dual-offset branch `PeMapImage` already implements a few lines above it in the same file (`pemod.c:149-164`) — read `OPT_NUMBER_OF_DIRS32`/`OPT_DATA_DIRS32` (`0x5C`/`0x60`) when `OptionalHeader.Magic==0x10B`, else the existing PE32+ offsets. A ~6-line, single-function, mechanically-proven change (no new donor research needed — the correct branch already exists verbatim elsewhere in this file for `PeMapImage`, and a third occurrence at `pemod.c:419-454` for another helper — `pe_find_named_export` is the ONE remaining PE-header reader in this file missing the dispatch). Expected effect: `thunk` resolves to the real `LdrInitializeThunk` RVA (`0x4e0a0` + `ntdll32_base`), `NtSetInformationThread`'s second call carries a real nonzero Eip, `State.rip` at first `ExecuteThread` becomes a valid ntdll32 code address instead of 0 — the NEXT atom after this fix would need to re-measure whether THAT address decodes/executes cleanly (a fresh, different frontier, not yet observed).

### (d) Commits + gate outputs

- **Zero source changes this pass** — `git status` on `kernel-arm/zoneA/` clean before/after (one build-artifact diff, `pehello_bin.c`, reverted per source-only-commits law). All findings came from (i) re-running the EXISTING `nt_dispatch_log_enabled` traces (`pe10 fex`, no new instrumentation needed — C4-26/9/11's own `NtQueryInformationThread`/`NtSetInformationThread` logging already prints every value needed) and (ii) a standalone, non-floor Python header-byte read of the vendored `stock/i386-windows/ntdll.dll` to confirm the PE32 magic + real vs. buggy-read data-directory offsets.

Gate suite not re-run this pass (no source diff to gate against — nothing changed); the fix candidate in (c) is unapplied and would require a full `pe8_gate`/`pe9_gate`/`ramfb_gate` re-run before being committed.

## C4-33 — LEAD 2 EXECUTED; the C4-31/32 central thesis OVERTURNED by live receipt: `XIntervals` **DOES enclose** `exe_entry(0x47661000)` at the exact `NoExecOp` self-fault. All three rival theories REFUTED. New paradox named. Runtime `<see commits>`; live 2026-07-23.

- **LEAD 2 done — live `XIntervals` dumper wired floor-side** (`kmain.c` `sync_try_guest_exception`, new `CHALICE C4-33` receipt, gated once). FEX's global `std::optional<InvalidationTracker> InvalidationTracker` (Module.cpp:130, anon ns — NOT in symtab) address DERIVED, not guessed: libwow64fex.dll is **NOT stripped** (COFF symtab present) → disasm of `WowSyscallHandler::QueryGuestExecutableRange` (RVA 0x53f8): `adrp x0,0x18034c000 ; add x0,x0,#0x430` → `&InvalidationTracker = ImageBase(0x180000000)+0x34c430`. std::optional stores value inline; `XIntervals` is member 0 (InvalidationTracker.h:55) → the libc++ `std::vector<Interval>` {begin@+0,end@+8,cap@+16}, `Interval={u64 Offset,u64 End}` (IntervalList.h:16-23) sits AT the object. Live base via `nt_image_base_by_name("libwow64fex.dll",…)`; heap pointers read via arena-aware `nt_fault_read32`.
- **RECEIPT (pe10 fex, same self-fault event as C4-30/31, one transcript)**: `ivt=0x4DD8C430 begin=0x230040180 end=0x230040200 count=8` → intervals `[47020000,47660000)`(native ntdll) **`[47660000,47665000)`(hello8_x86.exe — ENCLOSES exe_entry)** `[48690000,488BD000)`(ntdll32) `[4C910000,4CA30000)`(wow64) `[4DA40000,4DDA2000)`(libwow64fex) `[4EDC0000,4EEF0000)`(wow64win) `[4FF00000,50030000)`(win32u) `[58040000,58041000)`(BOP) → **`exe_entry(0x47661000) enclosed=1`**. Correlated same run: `C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT elr=0x26D0C02B0`, `C4-31 SynchronousFaultData raw=0x00000015020E0B01`=`NoExecOp` (Signal=SIGSEGV TrapNo=PF err_code=PROT|USER|INSTR) byte-identical, `Eip=exe_entry Eax=0`, `pe10 ret=0xC0000005` unchanged.
- **RIVAL VERDICTS (all REFUTED by this receipt)**: (a) ctor `VirtualQuery` sweep aborts early → NO (8 complete module intervals). (b) interval inserted-then-REMOVED by a later notify/flush → NO (present, full 0x5000 span, at fault time). (c) `HandleImageMap` section-walk reads our PE map differently → NO (interval present, correct base+span). **The C4-31/C4-32 sharpened gap ("`InvalidationTracker` never registers `exe_entry` / `XIntervals` doesn't enclose by decode time") is OVERTURNED** — it does enclose.
- **decode-gate re-read confirms the contradiction**: `InvalidationTracker::QueryExecutableRange` (InvalidationTracker.cpp:177-190) — `XIntervals.Query(exe_entry).Enclosed==true` → returns non-zero `Size` (RWX path can only shrink, not zero it) → `Decoder::CheckRangeExecutable` (Frontend.cpp:90-108) `if(Size==0)return false` NOT hit → returns **true** → `ReadByte`/`PeekByte` never set `HitNonExecutableRange` → the frontend **cannot emit `NoExecOp` for `exe_entry`**. Yet the self-fault byte-matches `NoExecOp` (OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:5018-5024, the ONLY BreakDefinition with err_code PROT|USER|INSTR). **NEW PARADOX, receipted.**
- **new frontier (named, not yet chased)**: NoExecOp fired for an address that is NOT XIntervals-enclosed, yet `WowContext.Eip`/guest CTX report `exe_entry` (which IS enclosed). Sharpest live hypotheses: (R1) the failing `CheckRangeExecutable` queried the decoder's **host `InstStream` pointer** (`PeekByte`: `ByteAddress=InstStream+InstructionSize+Offset`), which — if FEX's WOW64 block was set up with `InstStream` ≠ the guest linear addr (not 1:1 for this block) — lands outside XIntervals (which stores GUEST addrs) → false → NoExecOp, while the reconstructed guest `Eip` still reads exe_entry; (R2) a pre-insert warm-up decode. Resolving needs decode-time visibility (LEAD 1 narration, or reading the per-thread `Decoder` state `HitNonExecutableRange`/`ExecutableRangeBase`/`InstStream` — reachable next pass now that libwow64fex symbols are known).
- **LEAD 1 (voice) — still dead, narrowed, NOT fixed**: banner `__wine_dbg_output("starting FEX…")` works (transcript line 559, direct import via unixlib `nt_unix_dbg_write`) but ZERO `LogMan` `D/I/E`-prefixed lines anywhere → `FEX::Windows::Logging::Init` (Logging.cpp:37-47) emits nothing. Two candidates, could not isolate without FEX-internal visibility: (i) `SilentLog()` returns true (FEX_SILENTLOG=0 not honored — env conversion/`_environ`); (ii) `WineDbgOut=GetProcAddress(GetModuleHandleA("ntdll.dll"),"__wine_dbg_output")` null. Argued (ii) unlikely: the SAME `LdrGetDllHandle`/`LdrGetProcedureAddress` path is proven working later (Module.cpp:527/550 — `LdrSystemDllInitBlock`/`__wine_unix_call_dispatcher` both resolved, C4-27/29). Left for next pass; not a speculative floor edit this pass.

## C4-31 — the consume-check DONOR-TRACED end-to-end; its own input state PROVEN correct+present at the C4-30 self-fault (byte-exact match to FEX's `NoExecOp` `BreakDefinition` -- Signal=SIGSEGV, TrapNo=PageFault, err_code=PROT|USER|INSTR) -- REFUTES both suspects this task posed (A: PC/field-match failure; B: missing thread-state) -- new verdict: the consume-check WORKS AS DESIGNED (correctly translates this self-fault into a real x86 `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION(EXECUTE_FAULT)` at `RIP=exe_entry`), which then correctly, by Windows' own design, goes UNHANDLED at guest SEH (hello8_x86.exe has zero SEH handlers) -- NOT an exception-delivery/consume bug. The REAL gap, newly named: FEX's `InvalidationTracker` never registers `exe_entry` as executable, despite every checkable input (PE headers, section `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE` flag, `PEB.ImageBaseAddress`, this floor's own `NtQueryVirtualMemory` reporting EXECUTE, image-registration ordering) looking correct -- named, not chased further this pass (donor-internal state, unreachable without a donor edit). No fix attempted (new frontier, not a small named-field gap; time-boxed). Runtime `<see commit below>`; live 2026-07-19.

- **MEASURE 1, the consume-check itself, donor-traced end-to-end**: `BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl` (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:871-951`), invoked by wow64.dll's `Wow64PrepareForException` -- confirmed the ONLY such mechanism for the WOW64 build (no `RtlAddVectoredExceptionHandler` registered anywhere in `Source/Windows/WOW64/`; `FEX::DummyHandlers::DummySignalDelegator` installs no host signal handler at all, WOW64-only vs ARM64EC). Gate: `if (!Thread || !IsAddressInJit(Context->Pc)) return false;` -- `IsAddressInJit` checks `Config.DispatcherBegin/End` (= `Dispatcher::Start/End`, set to span the WHOLE `EmitDispatcher()` emission, which structurally includes `GuestSignal_SIGSEGV`'s own address by donor emission order, `Dispatcher.cpp:61/417/597-598`) OR the JIT code-buffer. Past the gate (both AV-specific fast-paths declined and Thread/Pc both valid): `Thread->CurrentFrame->SynchronousFaultData` is read and handed to `FEX::Windows::HandleGuestException` (`Source/Windows/Common/Exception.h:12-79`) -- **this IS the actual field-translation step this task's brief called "the consume-check"** -- it builds a REAL x86 `EXCEPTION_RECORD` from `Fault.Signal`/`TrapNo`, then `memcpy(Context, TLS.EntryContext(), ...)` (`:947`) replaces the host ARM64 context with the `BTCpuSimulate`-entry anchor -- **the donor-proven ORIGIN of every prior leg's `BTCpuSimulate+0x10` RAISE#1 identity** (C4-18/21/24/26/27/29), a normal side effect of this path SUCCEEDING, not evidence of an unclaimed fault.
- **MEASURE 2b, the consume-check's own input state, LIVE-READ from our floor** (new `kmain.c` receipt, `CHALICE C4-31`, hand-derived offsets -- `InternalThreadState::CurrentFrame` is member 0; `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData` is offset `0x5D8` = `sizeof(CPUState)=0x5C0` + 3 fields, rounded to 8): live capture at the SAME C4-30 self-fault event -- `CurrentFrame=0x270001140 raw=0x00000015020E0B01 FaultToTopAndGeneratedException=1 Signal=0xB(=11=FAULT_SIGSEGV) TrapNo=0xE(=14=X86_TRAPNO_PF) si_code=2 err_code=0x15(=PROT|USER|INSTR)` -- an EXACT, unambiguous, byte-for-byte match to `OpDispatchBuilder::NoExecOp`'s own `BreakDefinition` (`OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:5018-5024`; the only other three `FAULT_SIGSEGV` sites all use `TrapNo=GP/OF`, so this match cannot be a coincidence) -- **first-time identification of the exact x86 frontend site**, resolving C4-30's own flagged "which exact site fired is UNVERIFIED" question: it's `NoExecOp` ("instruction fetch from non-executable guest memory"), firing at `WowContext.Eip=0x47661000=hello8_x86.exe+0x1000=exe_entry` (matches C4-25/C4-30's own already-captured `GUEST CTX Eip` receipt exactly).
- **verdict vs this task's own A/B**: neither, as framed. B (missing state) directly REFUTED -- state is present, correctly written, correctly readable, and names exactly which donor code path fired. A (PC/field-match) not found either -- Pc is in-range by construction, Thread is proven non-null by the very fact the SynchronousFaultData read succeeded through the same pointer chain. New, evidence-driven verdict: the consume-check translates this fault CORRECTLY per FEX's own `X86_TRAPNO_PF` handling (`Exception.h:63-68`, leaves `ExceptionCode` as delivered -- matches this pass's own observed `pe10 ret=3221225477`=`0xC0000005` exactly, and C4-29's identical observation) -- the resulting real x86 AV is then genuinely unhandled at the guest SEH level because `hello8_x86.exe` (a bare 10-byte function, zero SEH frames) has no handler -- this is correct Windows behavior for that exception, not a floor bug.
- **the real, newly-named gap**: WHY `FEXCore::Frontend::Decoder::CheckRangeExecutable`/`InvalidationTracker::QueryExecutableRange` (`InvalidationTracker.cpp:177-190`, backed by an `XIntervals` interval tree populated at `HandleImageMap`'s PE-section walk + the tracker's own constructor-time whole-address-space `VirtualQuery` walk) believes `exe_entry` is not executable, despite EVERY checkable input this pass verified looking correct: `hello8_x86.exe`'s `.text` genuinely has `IMAGE_SCN_MEM_EXECUTE` (`llvm-readobj -S`, confirmed), `PeMapImage` copies PE headers+section table byte-verbatim (`pemod.c:119-171`), `PEB.ImageBaseAddress` is correctly wired (C4-29), this floor's own `NtQueryVirtualMemory` reports `PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE` for the whole registered image (`ntfile.c:1273-1339`, `if (img)` branch), and `nt_image_register` for the exe runs during Phase-0, provably before FEX/wow64.dll ever loads. All remaining candidates live inside FEX's own `InvalidationTracker`/`IntervalList` internals -- unreachable from this floor's own memory without a donor edit (project law). **Named, not chased further** -- next worker's starting point; the entire consume-check chain is now fully donor-traced so no re-derivation is needed.
- **re-gate, all green, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` ret=78 PASS; `pe9_gate.py` PASS both backends (wowbox64/fex, both ret=0); `ramfb_gate.py` PASS (7/7 pixel checks). Instrumentation-only change (one new receipt block in `sync_try_guest_exception`, reusing the existing `nt_fault_read32` safe-probe reader for arena-resident addresses instead of a raw bounds check) -- `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`=0xC0000005`) unchanged before/after.
- **commit this pass**: source-only, `kernel-arm/zoneA/kmain.c` (new `CHALICE C4-31 SynchronousFaultData` receipt in `sync_try_guest_exception`, reading `Thread->CurrentFrame->SynchronousFaultData` via the already-live `TlsSlots[16]` chain). ## C4-30 — the ORIGINAL fault, fully explained end-to-end for the first time: it is FEX's OWN deliberate GuestSignal_SIGSEGV self-fault mechanism ("force a SIGSEGV by loading zero", Dispatcher.cpp -- donor-cited, byte-matched exactly), reached via IR::Break(Signal=FAULT_SIGSEGV) inside FEX's own fixed dispatcher-buffer code (size 0x4000, exactly MAX_DISPATCHER_CODE_SIZE) -- NOT a triage regression, NOT the C4-27 stub/seeding, NOT a missing wow64/ntdll-init service. Same pre-existing architectural gap 6 prior legs named (FEX-internal signal marker misrouted through this floor's full NT SEH cycle instead of an internal short-circuit) -- but for the first time the ORIGINAL pre-reset fault is captured and precisely identified, not just the post-reset BTCpuSimulate+0x10 anchor RAISE#1 already showed. No fix attempted (same out-of-scope design ruling C4-18/21/24/26/27/29 all deferred to Pascal). Runtime `ec45bd6`; live 2026-07-19.

- **MEASURE step 1, answered live**: extended `kmain.c`'s `sync_try_guest_exception` (our own floor's HW-fault-to-guest-NT-exception delivery point, fires BEFORE any guest/FEX code ever sees the fault) with a `CHALICE C4-30 ORIGINAL FAULT` receipt -- raw esr/ec/status/elr/far/wnr (`(esr>>6)&1`, ARM ARM D13.2.37) -- plus `nt_fault_region` classification of BOTH elr and far (reused existing classifier, ntfile.c). Live capture (`pe10 fex`): `esr=0x96000006 ec=0x25 status=0xC0000005 elr=0x26D0C02B0 far=0x0 wnr=0` (a READ, not a write) -- elr-orig classifies `reserve-commit-arena committed base=0x26D0C0000 size=0x4000`; far-orig classifies `outside-known-allocators` (a genuine, unambiguous NULL pointer -- not a demand-commit-arena address misclassified, not a small offset-from-null sentinel like C4-28's far=0x42).
- **the arena base+size IS FEX's own Dispatcher buffer, proven by exact size match**: FEX `Dispatcher::Dispatcher` allocates its one fixed JIT-dispatcher-loop/trampoline buffer via `FEXCore::Allocator::VirtualAlloc(MAX_DISPATCHER_CODE_SIZE, true)` (`Dispatcher.cpp:39`, `constexpr size_t MAX_DISPATCHER_CODE_SIZE = FEXCore::Utils::FEX_PAGE_SIZE * 4` = `0x1000*4 = 0x4000`) -- our arena region's size (0x4000) is an EXACT, non-coincidental match; this is the SAME class of dispatcher-trampoline arena C4-17/18/21/27/28 already independently named (always 0x4000 bytes, always classified reserve-commit-arena, base varies boot-to-boot per this floor's bump allocator).
- **the exact instruction, disassembled + donor-matched**: raw ARM64 word window [elr-32,elr+32) dumped (`nt_fault_read32`, new `CHALICE C4-30 ELR WINDOW` receipt) and hand-decoded (field-by-field bit extraction, cross-checked against ARM ARM encodings): `elr-4 = 0x52800001` = `MOVZ W1, #0` (zeroes X1, standard AArch64 32-bit-write zero-extends to X1); `elr+0 = 0xF9400021` = `LDR X1, [X1, #0]` (size=11 opc=01 imm12=0 Rn=x1 Rt=x1) -- **loads through the register JUST set to zero**. This is a byte-for-byte, instruction-for-instruction match for FEX's own donor source, `FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp` (GuestSignal_SIGSEGV handler, comment verbatim in the donor: "hlt/udf = SIGILL, brk = SIGTRAP, ??? = SIGSEGV, Force a SIGSEGV by loading zero") -- `else { LoadConstant(Size::i64Bit, Reg::r1, 0); ldr(XReg::x1, Reg::r1); }` (the `if (CTX->ExitOnHLTEnabled())` branch, taken when that config is OFF -- the default, confirmed live since we hit the else). Confidence: high, exact instruction match, not a heuristic offset guess.
- **why FEX reaches this trampoline, donor-grounded**: GuestSignal_SIGSEGV is branched to ONLY from IR::Break (`FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MiscOps.cpp:89`, DEF_OP(Break) -- `case Core::FAULT_SIGSEGV: ldr(TMP1, ..Pointers.GuestSignal_SIGSEGV); br(TMP1);`), FEX's mechanism for synthesizing a guest x86 exception from JIT-generated code. IR::Break(FAULT_SIGSEGV) is itself emitted by the x86 frontend (OpcodeDispatcher.cpp) for a short, closed set of reasons: INT n when the literal isn't the syscall gate (0xCD, #GP), INTO (0xCE), HLT (0xF4, deliberately mapped to #GP/SIGSEGV not SIGILL), PermissionRestrictedOp (#GP), or NoExecOp (a non-executable-page instruction FETCH, #PF/SEGV_ACCERR). **Which exact site fired is UNVERIFIED this pass** -- PeMain's own decoded bytes at the boot-seeded Eip (hello8_x86.exe+0x1000, re-confirmed intact this pass, see below) are a benign `push ebp; mov ebp,esp; mov eax,0x2A; pop ebp; ret` (no INT/HLT/UD2 opcodes anywhere in it) -- so if this Break fired while translating THAT exact block it did not come from decoding those bytes; more likely this fires from a DIFFERENT FEX-internal path (an init-time probe/warm-up compile, or a permission/exec-bit check against an address that isn't exe_entry) reached before BTCpuSimulate ever gets to execute PeMain's own bytes. **Next lead, not chased this pass (new instrumentation, time-boxed out)**: `CpuStateFrame::SynchronousFaultData` (`FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h:414-421`, `{FaultToTopAndGeneratedException, Signal, TrapNo, si_code, err_code}`, 8 bytes) is written by IR::Break (MiscOps.cpp:64-75) immediately before branching to the signal trampoline -- reading it from OUR floor (via the live TlsSlots[16]/ThreadState pointer already probed, C4-22/23/29) would name the EXACT TrapNo/si_code without touching donor code (no donor edits, per this project's own law) -- a clean next step, not attempted here to respect the wall.
- **MEASURE step 2, I386_CONTEXT + stack/stub receipts**: extended `ntsvc.c`'s existing RAISE#1 guest-content dump (previously gated on `code==0xC000001DUL` only -- now fires on `nt_raise_trace_count==1` regardless of status code, since C4-29's fix changed the terminal code to 0xC0000005). Live: `Eip=0x47661000 (hello8_x86.exe+0x1000) Esp=0x488CC000 Eax=0x0 Ebp=0x488CC000` -- Eax=0 (NOT 0x2A -- PeMain did NOT complete/return; its own `mov eax,0x2A` never executed) and Esp==Ebp== the exact boot-seeded stack-top value (`nt_wow64_cpureserved_set_context`'s own write, unchanged) -- **PeMain never started executing**, consistent with the fault happening upstream of BTCpuSimulate ever dispatching to exe_entry's block. Guest-byte hexdump at Eip re-confirms the same intact, benign 10-byte function C4-26 already found (`55 89 E5 B8 2A 00 00 00 5D C3`).
- **MEASURE step 2, stack + stub-page hexdumps (new this pass)**: [Esp-8,Esp+16) -- word at Esp+0 reads back `0x488D0000`, exactly `nt_wow64_stub_addr` (the C4-27 stub buffer) -- **the stub return-address IS still correctly seeded on the stack**, undisturbed. Stub-page hexdump at that address (written=1, 13/0xD bytes as designed): decoded bytes `50 6A FF B8 2C 00 00 00 E8 F3 FF 76 0F` = `push eax; push -1; mov eax,0x2C; call rel32` -- **byte-for-byte intact**, and the rel32 (0x0F76FFF3) correctly resolves forward to this boot's own live-discovered BOP address (stub_addr+13+rel32 = 0x58040000, cross-checked by hand hex addition) -- **the C4-27 stub is correctly built, seeded, and undisturbed; it is simply UNREACHED**, same verdict C4-27 already reached, now doubly confirmed via a completely different fault identity.
- **MEASURE step 3, demand-commit triage check**: `CHALICE ARENA: ... elr=0x26D0C02B0 far=0x0` -> `nt_arena_contains(far=0)` correctly returns false (0 is not a reserve-commit-arena address, never was) -> `CHALICE ARENA: refused not-arena` -- the demand-commit handler SAW this fault and correctly declined it; **this is NOT a triage regression** (branch (a) from this task's own brief does not apply -- far is a genuine NULL, not a mis-triaged reserved-but-uncommitted arena address).
- **BRANCH verdict vs this task's own (a)/(b)/(c)**: **(a) does not apply** (far is not in the demand arena, correctly refused, not a regression). **(b) does not apply** (the C4-27 stub/Esp-seeding is proven intact and unreached, not the cause -- this fault happens before PeMain's own code ever runs). **(c) partially applies, sharpened**: this is not "an AV inside a wow64/ntdll init path" needing a *named missing service* -- it is AV inside FEX's OWN internal dispatcher-generated code, a DELIBERATE self-fault mechanism (not a floor gap at all on FEX's side) that this floor's general-purpose "forward every unclassified data-abort/hlt/brk to guest NT SEH" design (C4-18) cannot distinguish from a genuine guest AV -- this is the exact SAME already-repeatedly-named architectural gap (C4-18/21/24/26/27/29: FEX's own internal signal-return/exception-synthesis markers, meant to be absorbed by an FEX-internal host-signal short-circuit, are instead routed through this floor's full synthetic NT SEH cycle) -- but for the FIRST time, precisely identified as the GuestSignal_SIGSEGV marker specifically (not the previously-seen SignalHandlerReturnAddress/hlt#0 marker, nor the now-fixed TlsSlots[16]=2 sentinel), fully explaining the previously-opaque post-reset RAISE#1/BTCpuSimulate+0x10 identity end-to-end: our floor delivers this self-fault as a genuine STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION -> stock ntdll's KiUserExceptionDispatcher -> dispatch_exception -> wow64's Wow64PrepareForException -> FEX's BTCpuResetToConsistentStateImpl (which memcpy's a NEW reconstructed context anchored at BTCpuSimulate+0x10 over the real one, Module.cpp:947, by FEX's own design) -> guest ntdll calls NtRaiseException AGAIN with that reconstructed identity -> our floor's RAISE#1 only ever saw the anchor, never the original. **This full chain is now proven, not inferred**, for the first time across 6 prior legs.
- **SUCCESS LADDER verdict**: **(a) AV explained** -- ACHIEVED, fully, end-to-end, first time (exact instruction, exact donor mechanism, exact reconstruction chain to the post-reset anchor). **(a) "+ fixed"** -- NOT attempted: the fix is the identical already-named architectural decision (an FEX-internal-fault short-circuit vs full guest-SEH injection) 5 prior legs explicitly ruled needs Pascal's own design ruling, not a small named-service/triage fix -- attempting it here would be exactly the "speculative changes" this project's own law forbids. **(b) PeMain completes** -- NOT reached, and now explained WHY not: the fault fires before BTCpuSimulate ever gets to PeMain's block. **(c) new frontier mapped + recorded honestly** -- yes, more precisely than any prior leg: this is the first pass to capture the TRUE original fault (not just its post-reset anchor) and identify the exact FEX-internal mechanism by name, byte, and donor line.

## C4-29 — the null image base RESOLVED: FEX's own `BTCpuProcessInit` read `LdrSystemDllInitBlock->ntdll_handle` unconditionally and got NULL (this floor never mapped a 32-bit ntdll a second time, unix-loader-side, the way real Wine does). Fixed by mapping `syswow64/ntdll.dll` Phase-0 (C-direct, mirrors the existing native ntdll/exe maps) and populating the block directly -- C4-28's entire fresh fault generation (`RtlImageNtHeader(NULL)` -> `TlsSlots[16]=2` loop) is GONE, confirmed not recurring, no regression. New frontier: back at the SAME pre-existing `BTCpuSimulate+0x10` architectural gap C4-18/21/24/26/27 already independently named (FEX's own `hlt #0` dispatcher marker misrouted through full NT SEH) -- but reached this time via a genuinely healthy path: real `TlsSlots[16]` heap pointer (`BTCpuThreadInit` ran), real JIT/LookupCache activity in the reserve-commit arena, a clean single-pass NT SEH round-trip (no loop). Runtime `c594741`; live 2026-07-19.

- **MEASURE step 1 (who calls `RtlImageNtHeader(NULL)`), answered live**: extended the existing guest-exception-delivery instrumentation (`kmain.c` `sync_try_guest_exception`) with a caller-LR resolve (`resolve_line("lr", regs_safe[30])`) -- caught the caller as `libwow64fex.dll+0x169B40` (`x0=0`, the NULL arg), statically mapped to the return address right after `bl RtlImageNtHeader` inside `FEX::Windows::InvalidationTracker::HandleImageMap` (`Source/Windows/Common/InvalidationTracker.cpp:63`). Traced one level up (`FEX/Source/Windows/WOW64/Module.cpp:547-551`, `BTCpuProcessInit`): two `HandleImageMap` calls exist -- one for the main image (reads the native 64-bit `PEB.ImageBaseAddress`, already correctly wired, NOT the fault), one for the 32-bit ntdll (reads `LdrSystemDllInitBlock->ntdll_handle`, which was NULL -- the actual fault).
- **donor-grounded root cause**: `wine/dlls/ntdll/unix/loader.c:1595-1633` `load_ntdll_wow64_functions(HMODULE module)` is the ONLY donor call site that ever writes `ntdll_handle` (`pLdrSystemDllInitBlock->ntdll_handle = (ULONG_PTR)module`) -- a pure Wine **unix-side** (host-loader) step: map the 32-bit ntdll a SECOND time (after the native one) and walk its export table, zero guest execution. The PE-side `init_wow64()`/`SET_INIT_BLOCK` macro (`dlls/ntdll/loader.c:4404-4416`) sets 6 other function-pointer fields but has `ntdll_handle` explicitly commented OUT -- confirms this is floor/unix-side responsibility, exactly matching how this kernel already maps native ntdll+exe directly (Phase-0, C-side, no NT syscall). This floor never had the 32-bit-ntdll half of that Phase-0 step; C4-26 wrote the *populate* logic (`nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32`) but only wired it behind an `NtMapViewOfSection` hook that (C4-26's own receipt, and this task's fresh re-confirmation) never fires on `pe10`'s boot path.
- **fix (`c594741`)**: `ntboot.c`, inside the existing `if (wow64_mode)` block -- map `syswow64/ntdll.dll` directly via `boot_blob_lookup`+`PeMapImage` (same convention as the native ntdll/exe maps a few lines above), register it, then call `nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32("ntdll.dll", ntdll32_base, ntdll32_size)` **directly, C-side** -- the Wine-equivalent unix-side step, at this floor's existing Phase-0 point. Function exposed non-`static` (`ntfile.c`/`ntfile.h`) for the new direct call site; its old `NtMapViewOfSection` hook is left in place, harmless.
- **PEB.ImageBaseAddress receipt (MEASURE step 2)**: native 64-bit PEB was ALREADY correctly wired (`nt_build_teb(exe_base,...)`, pre-existing, no bug) -- new boot-time receipt line confirms it live (`CHALICE C4-29: PEB.ImageBaseAddress=0x47660000 (exe_base=0x47660000)`). **PEB32.ImageBaseAddress, however, was a genuine self-flagged gap** (`nt_build_wow64_teb32`'s own C4-3-era comment: "left 0 this atom -- no 32-bit ntdll/exe image mapped yet") -- fixed alongside (donor-cited `wine include/winternl.h:847`, offset `0x08`), confirmed via receipt (`CHALICE C4-29: PEB32.ImageBaseAddress=0x47660000`), though NOT the reader in this particular fault chain (FEX's `BTCpuProcessInit` never reads `TEB32->Peb` at this stage) -- closed because it was directly implicated by the brief and already self-documented as owed.
- **TlsSlots[16] resolution (MEASURE step 3)**: fixed, not separately -- `slot16(ThreadState)` now reads a real heap pointer (`0x270001000`), not the `2` sentinel C4-28 found. Confirms the earlier "abnormal arrival" hypothesis was correct and downstream of the SAME root cause: `BTCpuProcessInit` never survived past its own `HandleImageMap(NULL)` crash far enough to reach `BTCpuThreadInit` at all. No separate TlsSlots-writer bug ever existed.
- **fresh pe10-fex ladder vs the brief's own (a)-(d)**: **(a) ACHIEVED** (`BTCpuThreadInit` ran, real TLS pointer). **(b) likely achieved, indirect evidence** (post-thread-init: a genuine JIT/`LookupCache` reserve-commit-arena fault, demand-committed+retried, followed by live `NtSetInformationThread`/`NtQueryInformationThread ThreadWow64Context` round-trips -- consistent with PeMain's JIT'd bytes executing; no literal "first guest instruction" marker printed this pass, would need new instrumentation). **(c) NOT reached** -- terminates via a single clean `NtRaiseException id=207` (`code=0xC0000005`, `firstchance=0`, no loop -- C4-24's fix still holds), `ctx.pc=libwow64fex.dll+0x34E4`, freshly re-mapped this pass and confirmed IDENTICAL to the pre-C4-28 `BTCpuSimulate+0x10` identity (`Module.cpp:757`) -- the SAME architectural gap C4-18/21/24/26/27 all independently named and declined to fix speculatively (FEX's internal `hlt #0` signal-return marker misrouted through this floor's full synthetic NT SEH cycle). `pe10 ret=3221225477` (`=0xC0000005`). **(d) done** -- `map-fex-offsets.sh` re-run, no new DLL-relative address this pass (same identity, freshly confirmed); C4-28's entire fault generation confirmed gone, not recurring anywhere in the transcript.
- **verdict**: this task's own bug (the null image base) is CLOSED end-to-end -- caller identity, donor root cause, fix, TlsSlots resolution, re-gate, fresh ladder, all receipted. The remaining blocker to `hello8_x86`'s WriteFile marker is the SAME pre-existing, already-named, already-out-of-scope architectural gap five prior legs independently reached -- this pass reaches it via a measurably healthier path (real TLS, real JIT activity, clean single-pass SEH walk, not a null-pointer detour), real forward progress even though the terminal exit code is still an unhandled-exception terminate.
- **re-gate, all green, no regression**: `pe8_gate.py` `ret=78` · `pe9_gate.py` both backends (`wowbox64`/`fex`, both `ret=0`) · `ramfb_gate.py` 7/7 · `scratch/backtoback_gate.py` PASS (first attempt under an external 150s wrapper cut short mid-transcript -- not a real failure; rerun without the wrapper, full ~110s run, passed clean, matching this file's own "~2min wall time needed" note).
- **commits this pass**: `51d759e` (instrumentation: caller-LR resolve at guest-exception delivery; PEB32.ImageBaseAddress wire), `c594741` (the fix: syswow64/ntdll.dll Phase-0 map + direct populate call; `nt_wow64_populate_init_block_if_ntdll32` exposed non-static). Full byte-level detail, donor citations, disassembly → `FEX-MAP.md` § **C4-29**.

## C4-28 — null-page guard GATED (3 live-caught regressions fixed); the guard CHANGED the whole pe10-fex fault cascade exactly as hypothesized -- new, EARLIER, genuinely different frontier (ntdll `RtlImageNtHeader(NULL)`), NOT the old dispatcher-marker identity. Verdict on rivals A/B/C: new evidence, inconclusive -- named, not chased further this pass (time-boxed). Runtime `6af950a`; live 2026-07-19.

- **salvage-resumed task.** Disk state at start: `d84c091` (C4-28 guard commit, self-flagged "gates not yet re-verified") + an uncommitted `kmain.c` diff of unknown intent.
- **kmain.c diff adjudication: DROPPED.** Three `puts("DBG a/b/c\n")` debug prints inside `mmu_on()`, no functional change, no explanatory commit message. `git checkout`'d away; recorded here per the "no mystery diffs survive" law.
- **GATE, attempt 1: FAILED — guard broke boot before "MMU: on" ever printed.** Direct live-serial boot (bypassing the gate harness's own socket-drain timing to get a raw trace) showed **zero bytes of UART output**, even the pre-MMU banner. Root cause (live ESR/ELR/FAR receipt, `qemu-system-aarch64 -serial stdio` one-shot): `ESR_EL1` EC=`0x25` DFSC=`0x21` (**Alignment fault**) writing `l2_dev_table[1]` at `l2_dev_table+8`. With the MMU still off at this exact point in `mmu_on()`, **every access defaults to Device-nGnRnE** (`SCTLR_EL1.M=0` forces the whole address space Device per the ARM ARM) — and Device memory requires **natural alignment even for a plain `stp`**. The guard's own loop (`for i=1..511`, skipping index 0) left the compiler free to pair adjacent element stores into one `stp` starting at an 8-byte-only-aligned offset, straddling a 16B boundary → fault, every boot, before the kernel ever printed a single character.
- **iteration 1 (reorder loop, index-0 special-cased after)**: moved the crash forward one step (now `l1_table[0]`/`l1_table[1]` pairing, same class of bug) — proved source-order reordering alone is insufficient; the compiler has no notion of "Device memory" and is free to fuse stores wherever the C abstract machine permits it, regardless of statement order.
- **iteration 2 (compiler barriers, `asm volatile("":::"memory")` around each transition)**: reduced but did NOT fully suppress it — the SAME fusion re-appeared **inside** the small `NT_RAM_BLOCKS` loop's own unrolling (no barrier can live *inside* a single loop body without defeating the loop itself).
- **iteration 3 (`-mstrict-align` compiler flag, tried and REVERTED)**: stops the store-fusion class of bug architecturally, but breaks this freestanding/`-nostdlib` build: clang lowers `fs.c`'s `FsWrite` struct-copy code to a libcall `memcpy` this kernel doesn't provide (link fails: `undefined reference to 'memcpy'`). Reverted; noted in `build.sh` so nobody retries it blind.
- **iteration 4 (fix, KEPT)**: `l1_table`/`l2_dev_table` declared `volatile`. This is the actual C-semantics guarantee needed (no store may be elided, reordered across, or **fused** with another volatile access) — stronger than a memory-clobber barrier, and scoped to only the two page-table arrays (no global codegen/perf/ABI impact elsewhere). Direct live-serial boot confirmed clean start-to-shell after this fix.
- **GATE, attempt 2: FAILED — two more genuine, PRE-EXISTING null-pointer bugs, now surfaced for real.** With the alignment crash fixed, `pe8_gate.py` failed: `pe6`/`pe7`/`pe8` all terminated `NtRaiseException code=0xC0000005 (STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION)`. Both were **real dereferences of address 0/near-0 by genuine stock-ntdll code**, previously masked because the old (pre-guard) mapping treated address 0 as valid device memory (silent garbage reads instead of faults) — **exactly the failure mode the guard exists to catch** (per `d84c091`'s own commit message), now caught for real:
  1. **`RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.CurrentDirectory.DosPath.Buffer` was always NULL** (field named in a comment, never wired). `map-fex-offsets.sh` resolved the fault site to ntdll's internal `get_full_path_helper` (embedded-symbol, confidence high): `ldr x22,[x24,#0x8]` (loads `DosPath.Buffer`) then unconditionally `ldrh w8,[x22,#0x2]` (checks `Buffer[1]` for a drive-letter `':'` pattern, no NULL check) — fires for pe6/7/8 because their `ImagePathName` is deliberately a bare relative exe name (not the `wow64_mode`-only `"C:\\windows\\..."` absolute path this file's own C4-6 comment already predicted would need `CurrentDirectory` if ever hit). **Fix (`ntboot.c`)**: wire `CurrentDirectory.DosPath = "C:\\"` unconditionally (all processes, not just `wow64_mode`).
  2. **`TEB.ActivationContextStackPointer` (offset `0x2c8`, well-known public Win64 TEB layout) was always NULL.** Crash moved forward to ntdll's `RtlFindActivationContextSectionString` (embedded-symbol, confidence high): `ldr x8,[x18,#0x2c8]; ldr x8,[x8]` — dereferences the TEB field directly with no null-check on the pointer itself (only checks the *result* of the dereference). **Fix (`ntsvc.c`, `nt_build_teb`)**: allocate + wire a small zeroed `ACTIVATION_CONTEXT_STACK` (only the leading `ActiveFrame` pointer, offset 0, is read on this path — zeroed satisfies the fast-empty-stack `cbz` branch immediately after).
